Its \(\kappa\)-regularity gives \(\lambda_{\min}(B^{\top}\Omega B)\ge n\kappa\), so it instantiates the boundedness and nondegeneracy clauses of Zhao's Assumption 2\(^{\dagger}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Member properties.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}block-\allowbreak{}separable-\allowbreak{}basis}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}projective-\allowbreak{}normalization}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}kappa-\allowbreak{}regularity}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}coordinate-\allowbreak{}envelope}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:zhao-published-pseudoinverse-domain]\label{def:zhao-published-pseudoinverse-domain}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal B\_\allowbreak{}Z\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Under Zhao's convention that the inverse of every symmetric positive-semidefinite matrix is obtained by taking spectral reciprocals on positive eigenvalues and assigning zero to zero eigenvalues, Zhao's published unquotiented domain consists of all block-separable matrices
\[
B=\begin{bmatrix}b_1&0\\0&b_0\end{bmatrix},\qquad b_1,b_0\in\mathbb R^n,
\]
including ranks zero, one, and two.  Define \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) to be the note's exact scale-invariant projective quotient of that unquotiented domain.  Two representatives are equivalent when, separately in each arm, both corresponding columns are zero or one is a nonzero scalar multiple of the other.  Each class is represented with every nonzero arm column having squared norm \(n\), while a zero arm remains zero.  Thus \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) is the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain, not a projective class named by Zhao.  Its rank-two positive-definite stratum is the image of \(\mathcal B_Z\) under \(\iota\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:zhao-well-defined-ordinary-domain]\label{def:zhao-well-defined-ordinary-domain}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widetilde\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal B\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}Z,\allowbreak{}q\_\allowbreak{}Z}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z=\{B:B=\begin{bmatrix}b_1&0\\0&b_0\end{bmatrix},\ b_1,b_0\in\mathbb R^n,\ B^{\top}\Omega B\succ0\}\).  This is the unquotiented positive-definite rank-two restriction of Zhao's published pseudoinverse basis domain, not the full published domain.  Define \(q_Z:\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\to\mathcal B_Z\) by \(q_Z(B)=[B]\), where \(\mathcal B_Z\) is its quotient by independent nonzero arm-column rescaling.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:completion-pseudoinverse-loss]\label{def:completion-pseudoinverse-loss}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{g\_\allowbreak{}B\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}(c),\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}ell\_\allowbreak{}B\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}(c),\allowbreak{}w\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}(B),\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} For \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\), let \(S_B^+\) denote Zhao's published spectral pseudoinverse of \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\), and define
\[
g_B^+(c)=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\!\left[S_B^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Q(c)\Pi^{-1}\Omega B\right],
\qquad \ell_B^+(c)=h(c)-g_B^+(c),
\]
\[
w^+(B)=\max\{\ell_B^+(c_L),\ell_B^+(c_U)\},
\qquad v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+:=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B),
\]
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
=\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:w^+(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\},
\qquad \mathcal A_0^+=\operatorname*{argmax}_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}g_B^+(0).
\]
The equality set \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) and the zero-covariance argmax set may be empty because the rank-stratified pseudoinverse criteria need not attain their extrema across rank changes.  These are criteria induced by Zhao's published unquotiented carrier and expressed on the note's projective quotient; no attainment is built into the definition.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}gain-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}moment-\allowbreak{}matrix}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:robust-objective]\label{def:robust-objective}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{w\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{}(B),\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}w(B),\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}r(B),\allowbreak{}v\textasciicircum{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}star}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} The primary robust criterion on the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain is \(w^+(B)=\max\{\ell_B^+(c_L),\ell_B^+(c_U)\}\), with value \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B)\).  On the deployable fixed-threshold rank-two restriction, retain \(w(B)=\max\{\ell_B(c_L),\ell_B(c_U)\}\) and \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}=\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)\).  Retain \(r(B)=\min\{g_B(c_L),g_B(c_U)\}\) and \(v^\star=\max_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}r(B)\) only as secondary regular-class maximin-gain diagnostics.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}gain-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}kappa-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:argmax-correspondences]\label{def:argmax-correspondences}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\_\allowbreak{}0\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\_\allowbreak{}R,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\_\allowbreak{}0}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} On the feasible covariance domain, where ass:product-coupling-zero and ass:frechet-covariance-domain give \(0\in\mathcal C(F_1,F_0)=[c_L,c_U]\), define the primary full-quotient equality correspondences
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+=\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:w^+(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\},
\qquad
\mathcal A_0^+=\operatorname*{argmax}_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}g_B^+(0),
\]
which may be empty.  On the compact fixed-threshold rank-two restriction retain \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)\), \(\mathcal A_0=\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}g_B(0)\), and \(\mathcal A_R=\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}r(B)\) as regular deployable correspondences.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}frechet-\allowbreak{}covariance-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}product-\allowbreak{}coupling-\allowbreak{}zero}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:certified-input-interface]\label{def:certified-input-interface}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathfrak I\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{cert\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Encode \(\Pi,\Omega,Q(c_L),Q(c_U),c_L,c_U,\kappa,\varepsilon\) either by exact rational or real-algebraic data, or by outward-rounded inclusion oracles supplied with effective refinement moduli and an exact decision oracle for sphere feasibility and the inequality \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega B)\ge\kappa\), including equality at the \(\kappa\)-boundary.  Equivalently, the endpoint moment matrices may be encoded through \(\mu_1,\mu_0,\sigma_1^2,\sigma_0^2,c_L,c_U\).  Require \(\varepsilon\) itself to have an exact positive rational or real-algebraic encoding.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}kappa-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}moment-\allowbreak{}matrix}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:certified-output]\label{def:certified-output}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathscr K\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} A corrected minimax certificate \(\mathscr K_{\varepsilon}\) consists of a finite quotient-chart cover, an exactly feasible incumbent \(B_{\varepsilon}\), rational bounds \(L_{\varepsilon},U_{\varepsilon}\), outward-rounded endpoint-loss and constraint bounds for every retained box, a checkable exact-infeasibility or valid lower-bound pruning record for every discarded box, retained boxes whose union is \(\mathcal C_\varepsilon\), and their maximum chart diameter \(h_{\varepsilon}\).  The exactly feasible incumbent may be represented by exact rational or real-algebraic coordinates, or by a certified inclusion-oracle name whose every requested precision terminates.  For the latter representation, exact feasibility is certified by nested closed quotient-chart boxes, each meeting \(\mathcal B_\kappa\), whose diameters tend to zero and whose intersection is the singleton \(\{B_{\varepsilon}\}\).  The checker requires \(L_{\varepsilon}\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}\le w(B_{\varepsilon})\le U_{\varepsilon}\), \(U_{\varepsilon}-L_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon\), \(h_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon\), and \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\subseteq\mathcal C_\varepsilon\); it never accepts deletion of a box meeting the exact \(\kappa\)-boundary without an exact infeasibility certificate or a valid objective lower bound exceeding the incumbent upper bound.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}certified-\allowbreak{}input-\allowbreak{}interface}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}argmax-\allowbreak{}correspondences}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:branch-bound-handle]\label{def:branch-bound-handle}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{projective interval branch-\allowbreak{}and-\allowbreak{}bound procedure}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Given the moment-complete \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\), the projective interval branch-and-bound procedure covers each normalized projective factor by the finite pivot-coordinate charts \(\{|b_j|\ge n^{-1/2}\}\), bisects retained boxes, handles the sphere equations and the exact \(\kappa\)-boundary through the feasibility interface, and bounds both endpoint residual losses and every constraint by outward-rounded interval extensions, optionally tightened by interval-Hessian underestimators.  For any chart box whose feasible intersection is nonempty, repeated bisection and exact feasible-child selection produce nested closed feasible boxes with diameters tending to zero; their singleton intersection supplies the certified inclusion-oracle name of an exactly feasible incumbent authorized by the certificate definition.  The procedure prunes only by exact infeasibility or by a certified box lower bound exceeding the incumbent upper bound, and it refines the finite quotient-chart cover and arithmetic enclosures until \(U_{\varepsilon}-L_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon\) and \(h_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon\), at which point it returns the full certificate \(\mathscr K_{\varepsilon}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}kappa-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}certified-\allowbreak{}input-\allowbreak{}interface}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}certified-\allowbreak{}output}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:dominance-gap]\label{def:dominance-gap}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Delta(\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}xi)}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} \(\Delta(\xi)=\inf_{B_0\in\mathcal A_0(\xi)}w_{\xi}(B_0)-\sup_{B_{\mathrm{mm}}\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)}w_{\xi}(B_{\mathrm{mm}})\).  Since every member of \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)\) has loss \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)\), the second term equals \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}argmax-\allowbreak{}correspondences}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:separation-search-handle]\label{def:separation-search-handle}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{finite lawful minimax separation handle}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Start from a three-vertex network under independent Bernoulli assignment with probability \(1/3\), enumerate the mutually exclusive exposure truth tables exactly, derive exact \(\Pi\) and \(\Omega\), use Bernoulli \(1/2\) pilot marginals, and analyze simultaneously the full minimax-residual and zero-covariance-surrogate correspondences.  The instance-specific certificate consists of exact optimizer equality sets, a rational lower bound \(\delta>0\) on \(\inf_{B_0\in\mathcal A_0}w(B_0)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}\), and a rational optimizer-wide regularity slack \(\eta>0\) over \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\cup\mathcal A_0\).  This finite lawful separation handle is distinct from, and does not assert, generic finite-\(\varepsilon\) termination of the moment-complete projective interval branch-and-bound procedure.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}branch-\allowbreak{}bound-\allowbreak{}handle}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}dominance-\allowbreak{}gap}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:observable-coefficient-map]\label{def:observable-coefficient-map}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}gamma\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{HT\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}gamma\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{SN\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}tau\_\allowbreak{}B\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CV\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Define the realized outcome \(Y_i^{\mathrm{obs}}=Y_i(W)\) and Zhao's signed armwise Horvitz--Thompson stack
\[
\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}=
\left(\frac{D_{1,1}Y_1^{\mathrm{obs}}}{\pi_{1,1}},\ldots,
\frac{D_{n,1}Y_n^{\mathrm{obs}}}{\pi_{n,1}},
-\frac{D_{1,0}Y_1^{\mathrm{obs}}}{\pi_{1,0}},\ldots,
-\frac{D_{n,0}Y_n^{\mathrm{obs}}}{\pi_{n,0}}\right)^{\top}.
\]
For a representative of \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\), define Zhao's published pseudoinverse coefficient
\[
\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)
=(B^{\top}\Omega B)^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}
\]
and the observable control-variate estimator
\[
\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}
=n^{-1}\mathbf1^{\top}\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}
-\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)^{\top}
\{n^{-1}B^{\top}D-\mathbb E_\nu[n^{-1}B^{\top}D]\}.
\]
On \(\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\), \((B^{\top}\Omega B)^+=(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}\), so these maps reduce to the ordinary-inverse rank-two formulas used by the finite-rate results.  Separately define \(\widehat1_a=n^{-1}\sum_iD_{i,a}/\pi_{i,a}\), \(\widehat\gamma_a^{\mathrm{SN}}(B)=\widehat\gamma_a^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)/\widehat1_a\) when \(\widehat1_a>0\) and zero otherwise, and obtain \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV,SN}}\) by substitution.  No first-order equivalence for the self-normalized variant is asserted.  The expectation identity needed for the oracle comparison is not built into this definition; it is derived from ass:known-exposure in lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}known-\allowbreak{}exposure}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}rate-\allowbreak{}admissible-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:ordinary-intercept-slope-image]\label{def:ordinary-intercept-slope-image}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{a\_\allowbreak{}B,\allowbreak{}b\_\allowbreak{}B,\allowbreak{}p\_\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}xi,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Delta\_\allowbreak{}Z}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Let
\[
E=\begin{bmatrix}0&I_n\\I_n&0\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
A_B=\Pi^{-1}\Omega B(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}.
\]
For \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\), define the ordinary-domain intercept and slope
\[
a_B=g_B(0),
\qquad
b_B=-n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(A_BE),
\qquad g_B(c)=a_B+b_Bc,
\]
and define the endpoint drift penalty
\[
p_\xi(b)=\max\{(2/n-b)c_L,(2/n-b)c_U\}.
\]
Whenever the relevant ordinary-domain extrema are attained, put
\[
a^\star=\max_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}a_B,
\quad
\mathcal A_{0,Z}=\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}a_B,
\quad
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm},Z}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B),
\]
\[
\Delta_Z=
\inf_{B_0\in\mathcal A_{0,Z}}w(B_0)
-\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B).
\]
These objects use only the positive-definite ordinary-inverse domain; no singular or Moore--Penrose class is included.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}gain-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}moment-\allowbreak{}matrix}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}well-\allowbreak{}defined-\allowbreak{}ordinary-\allowbreak{}domain}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:rank-adaptive-stability-strata]\label{def:rank-adaptive-stability-strata}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{Z,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathfrak M\_\allowbreak{}\{3,\allowbreak{}n\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathfrak M\_\allowbreak{}\{4,\allowbreak{}n\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}s\_\allowbreak{}n,\allowbreak{}d\_\allowbreak{}n,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal S\_\allowbreak{}\{r,\allowbreak{}n\}\allowbreak{}(\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}lambda,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}overline a),\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal K\_\allowbreak{}n\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{st\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}n\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{st\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}b\_\allowbreak{}n\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{st\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Put \(N=2n\), write \(\pi=\mathbb E_\nu D\), and define the centered design vector
\[
Z=D-\pi.
\]
The exact finite-design moment moduli are
\[
\mathfrak M_{4,n}
=\max_{q\in\{-1,1\}^{N}}\mathbb E_\nu(q^{\top}Z)^4
\]
and
\[
\mathfrak M_{3,n}
=\max_{q,r,s\in\{-1,1\}^{N}}
\left|\mathbb E_\nu\{(q^{\top}Z)(r^{\top}Z)(s^{\top}Z)\}\right|.
\]
They are computable from the known finite assignment law by respectively \(2^N\) and \(2^{3N}\) finite evaluations.  Convexity for the fourth moment and separate linearity for the third mixed moment give, for arbitrary real vectors,
\[
\mathbb E_\nu(q^{\top}Z)^4
\le\mathfrak M_{4,n}\lVert q\rVert_\infty^4,
\]
\[
\left|\mathbb E_\nu\{(q^{\top}Z)(r^{\top}Z)(s^{\top}Z)\}\right|
\le\mathfrak M_{3,n}\lVert q\rVert_\infty
\lVert r\rVert_\infty\lVert s\rVert_\infty.
\]
For the directed local neighborhoods in \(\texttt{ass:zhao-dependency-neighborhood-rate}\), retain \(\mathcal G_n=\{(\ell,j):j\in N_\ell\}\) only as a directed relation and define
\[
s_n=\max_{1\le \ell\le N}\#\{k:\Omega_{k\ell}\ne0\},
\qquad
d_n=\max_{1\le \ell\le N}\lvert N_\ell\rvert.
\]
No separated-set independence or undirected dependency-graph property is included in these definitions.

For a normalized representative \(B\) of a class in \(\mathcal B_Z^+\), put
\[
\lVert B\rVert_{\max}
=\max_{1\le i\le n,\,a\in\{1,0\}}|b_{a,i}|.
\]
For \(r\in\{1,2\}\), \(\lambda>0\), and \(\overline a\ge1\), define
\[
\mathcal S_{r,n}(\lambda,\overline a)
=\left\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:
\operatorname{rank}(S_B)=r,\ 
\lambda_+(S_B)\ge n\lambda,\ 
\lVert B\rVert_{\max}\le\overline a\right\},
\]
where \(\lambda_+(S_B)\) is the smallest strictly positive eigenvalue.  Define
\[
\mathcal S_{0,n}(\overline a)
=\left\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:S_B=0,\ 
\lVert B\rVert_{\max}\le\overline a\right\}.
\]
No positive-eigenvalue condition is imposed at rank zero.  These strata neither identify nearby classes across ranks nor impose stability as \(\lambda_+(S_B)\downarrow0\).

Fix displayed constants \(\lambda_{1,n}>0\), \(\lambda_{2,n}>0\), and \(\overline a\ge1\), and define the stable carrier
\[
\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}
=\mathcal S_{0,n}(\overline a)
\cup\mathcal S_{1,n}(\lambda_{1,n},\overline a)
\cup\mathcal S_{2,n}(\lambda_{2,n},\overline a).
\]
It is nonempty because the all-zero class belongs to \(\mathcal S_{0,n}(\overline a)\), and it is compact in the normalized quotient.  Define the attained stable value and its exact approximation price relative to the unrestricted published carrier by
\[
v_n^{\mathrm{st}}
=\min_{[B]_+\in\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}}w^+(B),
\qquad
b_n^{\mathrm{st}}=v_n^{\mathrm{st}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\ge0.
\]
Rank zero is exactly the uncontrolled Horvitz--Thompson fallback.  No equality \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}=0\) and no rate for \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) is part of the definition.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:pilot-plugin]\label{def:pilot-plugin}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat w\_\allowbreak{}m\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat B\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\_\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat B\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\_\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{st\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat w,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat v\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal A\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Compute the empirical means, variances, and two empirical quantile-coupling covariances from \(\widehat F_1,\widehat F_0\), and let \(\widehat Q_L,\widehat Q_U\) be the resulting endpoint matrices.  On the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain, define the distinct full-class empirical objective
\[
\widehat w_m^+(B)=
\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\!\left[
\left\{\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
-\Pi^{-1}\Omega B S_B^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\right\}\widehat Q_e
\right].
\]
Let
\[
r_\Omega=\max_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\operatorname{rank}(B^{\top}\Omega B)\in\{0,1,2\}.
\]
A block class has rational support when each nonzero arm column is the squared-norm-\(n\) normalization of a nonzero vector in \(\mathbb Q^n\), while a zero arm remains zero.  In a fixed enumeration of the finitely many signed pivot charts, enumerate all rational-support block classes of rank \(r_\Omega\), remove duplicate quotient classes, and call the resulting sequence \((C_k)_{k\ge1}\).  If \(r_\Omega=0\), take \(C_k=[0]_+\) for every \(k\).

The following countable rule is a classical nonconstructive measurable existence construction, not a finite evaluation algorithm.  For every finite strictly positive deterministic or pilot-measurable tolerance \(\varepsilon_m\), define
\[
 \rho_m=\inf_{j\ge1}\widehat w_m^+(C_j),
 \qquad
 \widehat k_m=
 \min\{k\ge1:\widehat w_m^+(C_k)\le\rho_m+\varepsilon_m\},
 \qquad
 \widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+=C_{\widehat k_m}.
\]
The countable infimum and first qualifying index supply no finite stopping test for arbitrary real-valued pilot observations.  A separate rank-stratified cylindrical-algebraic rule is defined only for exact rational or real-algebraic endpoint inputs; agreement between that rule and \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+\) is not asserted.

For the stable carrier \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\), fix once and for all a countable dense sequence \((C_k^{\mathrm{st}})_{k\ge1}\).  Compactness makes such a sequence exist and continuity of the rank-stratified objective gives
\[
\widehat v_m^{\mathrm{st}}
=\min_{[B]_+\in\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}}\widehat w_m^+(B)
=\inf_{k\ge1}\widehat w_m^+(C_k^{\mathrm{st}}).
\]
For every finite strictly positive deterministic or pilot-measurable \(\varepsilon_m\), define
\[
\widehat k_m^{\mathrm{st}}
=\min\left\{k\ge1:
\widehat w_m^+(C_k^{\mathrm{st}})
\le\widehat v_m^{\mathrm{st}}+\varepsilon_m\right\},
\qquad
\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+}
=C_{\widehat k_m^{\mathrm{st}}}^{\mathrm{st}}.
\]
This stable selector is pilot measurable and obeys
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+})
\le\widehat v_m^{\mathrm{st}}+\varepsilon_m.
\]
For arbitrary real pilot values it is, like the unrestricted countable rule, a nonconstructive measurable existence selector with no finite stopping test.  It is distinct from the unrestricted selector and from the exact-input stable cylindrical-algebraic selector.

On the compact fixed-threshold rank-two restriction, separately retain
\[
\widehat w(B)=\max\{\widehat\ell_B(\widehat c_L),\widehat\ell_B(\widehat c_U)\},
\quad
\widehat v_{\mathrm{mm}}=\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}\widehat w(B),
\quad
\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}\widehat w(B),
\]
and retain \(\widehat r(B)\), \(\widehat v\), and \(\widehat{\mathcal A}_R\) only as secondary diagnostics.  The classical unrestricted countable selector has maximal achievable rank \(r_\Omega\); it is rank two exactly when rank two is achievable.  The stable selector instead trades unrestricted value against the certified positive-eigenvalue and coordinate constraints through \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\).
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}moment-\allowbreak{}matrix}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}gain-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}robust-\allowbreak{}objective}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}rank-\allowbreak{}adaptive-\allowbreak{}stability-\allowbreak{}strata}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:jiangxi-picc-robust-reanalysis]\label{def:jiangxi-picc-robust-reanalysis}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathcal J\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{PICC\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} The exploratory record \(\mathcal J_{\mathrm{PICC}}\) is the reproducible but unexecuted procedure implemented by \(\texttt{discovery/jiangxi\_robust\_reanalysis.py}\).  Its input directory must contain \(\texttt{survey.tab}\) and \(\texttt{network.tab}\).  The procedure cleans identifiers, sentinel \(99\), self-loops, and duplicate directed nominations while retaining cross-village nominations; constructs the three target populations for \(\tau_{10},\tau_{21},\tau_{32}\); and uses the disclosed independent Bernoulli \((1/2)\) reconstruction necessitated by the unreleased original strata.  It splits connected components of the exposure-overlap graph without cutting components and estimates the two marginal laws and Fr\'echet endpoints on each training fold.  The checked script is a heuristic positive-definite rank-two procedure: it normalizes two nonzero columns, evaluates ordinary inverses with \(\texttt{torch.linalg.solve}\) or \(\texttt{np.linalg.solve}\), penalizes small eigenvalues, and conducts local Adam searches under common initialization and iteration budgets.  It does not admit singular strata, use a Moore--Penrose or CAD solver, certify epsilon-global optimality, or check a declared coordinate envelope or \(\kappa\)-feasibility restriction.  Its held-out deployment uses the armwise self-normalized coefficient \(\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{SN}}(B)\) and hence the implementation variant \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV,SN}}\).  Output fields report point estimates, both endpoint losses, worst loss, a minimum-eigenvalue diagnostic, and plug-in standard-error and objective diagnostics.  The standard-error field is not asserted to be an exact fixed-array design-randomization standard error, and the objective diagnostic is not a certified optimization gap.  Authenticated replication data were unavailable in the solve environment, so the protocol was not executed and no numerical table is asserted.  Its internal cross-fitting is distinct from the independent unpaired pilot of ass:unpaired-pilot, so neither the external-pilot first-order transfer theorem nor the full-class CAD guarantee is claimed for this application protocol.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}known-\allowbreak{}exposure}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}coefficient-\allowbreak{}map}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}pilot-\allowbreak{}plugin}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:feasible-ht-pilot-selected-estimator]\label{def:feasible-ht-pilot-selected-estimator}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat B\_\allowbreak{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{mm\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat B\_\allowbreak{}0}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Under ass:known-exposure and ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility, and using only the independent unpaired pilot, define the regular rank-two selectors
\[
\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}}
\in\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}\widehat w(B),
\qquad
\widehat B_0
\in\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}\widehat g_B(0)
\]
by the fixed lexicographic quotient-chart tie-breaking rule.  In the independent main experiment deploy \(\widehat\tau_{\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}}}^{\mathrm{CV}}\) and \(\widehat\tau_{\widehat B_0}^{\mathrm{CV}}\) through def:observable-coefficient-map.  On this class Zhao's published pseudoinverse equals the ordinary inverse.  The pilot chooses the basis, while the main assignment and main potential-outcome array enter only the displayed estimator.  The existence and measurability of these selectors are derived in thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer; no out-of-class fallback is part of the construction.  This construction is the regular deployable restriction, not the primary full-class epsilon-minimizer rule.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}known-\allowbreak{}exposure}; \texttt{ass:\allowbreak{}coordinate-\allowbreak{}bounded-\allowbreak{}kappa-\allowbreak{}feasibility}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}rate-\allowbreak{}admissible-\allowbreak{}basis-\allowbreak{}class}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}pilot-\allowbreak{}plugin}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}observable-\allowbreak{}coefficient-\allowbreak{}map}.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[def:encoded-cad-pilot-selector]\label{def:encoded-cad-pilot-selector}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Defined object.} \texttt{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathfrak I\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{alg\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}+\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathscr K\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\_\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CAD\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat B\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\_\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CAD\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathfrak I\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{alg\}\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{st\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}widehat\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathscr K\}\allowbreak{}\_\allowbreak{}\{m,\allowbreak{}\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}varepsilon\_\allowbreak{}m\}\allowbreak{}\textasciicircum{}\{\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}mathrm\{CAD,\allowbreak{}st\}\allowbreak{},\allowbreak{}+\allowbreak{}\}\allowbreak{}}
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Construction.} Call a realized pilot input algebraically encoded when \(\Pi,\Omega,\widehat Q_L,\widehat Q_U\), all scalar endpoint fields needed to construct the empirical moment matrices, and a strictly positive tolerance \(\varepsilon_m\) have exact rational or real-algebraic encodings.  Denote it by \(\widehat{\mathfrak I}_{m,\mathrm{alg}}^+\).  Fix once and for all the variable, projection, rank, signed-pivot-chart, cell, and algebraic-sample orders in the rank-stratified cylindrical-algebraic procedure.  Run that procedure with \(Q(c_L),Q(c_U)\) replaced by \(\widehat Q_L,\widehat Q_U\), and write the augmented finite output as
\[
\widehat{\mathscr K}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
=\left(
\widehat r_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD}},
[\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}]_+,
\widehat L_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+},
\widehat U_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+},
\widehat{\mathscr S}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD}},
\widehat{\mathscr P}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
\right).
\]
Here \(\widehat{\mathscr P}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}\) is the finite CAD sign, sample, and value-isolation record, and the selected basis has normalized rational or real-algebraic coordinates.  Put
\[
\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}}
=(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+})^{\top}
\Omega\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}.
\]
The spectral record contains the characteristic polynomial
\[
\chi_m(t)=t^2-\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})t
+\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}}),
\]
exact real-algebraic representations of its distinct roots with multiplicities, rational isolating intervals for every positive root, exact zero tags, and exact sign certificates for
\[
\widehat r_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD}}=
\begin{cases}
0,&\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})=0,\\
1,&\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})>0
\text{ and }\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})=0,\\
2,&\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})>0.
\end{cases}
\]
The basis component is the encoded CAD pilot selector.  This construction is defined only on the exact rational or real-algebraic input domain and is distinct from the classical countable selector \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+\); equality is not asserted.

For the stable rule, augment the exact input by exact rational or real-algebraic encodings of \(\lambda_{1,n}>0\), \(\lambda_{2,n}>0\), \(\overline a\ge1\), and the population endpoint input \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\); denote the augmented input by \(\widehat{\mathfrak I}_{m,\mathrm{alg}}^{\mathrm{st},+}\).  Impose, in addition to the normalized signed-pivot charts and coordinate inequalities \(|b_{a,i}|\le\overline a\), exactly one of
\[
\operatorname{tr}(S_B)=0,
\]
\[
\det(S_B)=0,\qquad \operatorname{tr}(S_B)\ge n\lambda_{1,n},
\]
or
\[
S_B-n\lambda_{2,n}I_2\succeq0.
\]
These are the rank-zero, rank-one, and rank-two pieces of \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\).  Run the empirical rank-stratified CAD minimization over their union and, separately on the exact population endpoint input, isolate \(v_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) and \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  The finite stable record \(\widehat{\mathscr K}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD,st},+}\) contains an algebraic stable basis \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD,st},+}\), empirical stable-value bounds, exact rank and coordinate-envelope signs, the spectral-isolation record, and numbers \(\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}\) and \(\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}\) satisfying
\[
0\le\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}
\le\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}},
\qquad
\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
-\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
\le\varepsilon_m.
\]
The population endpoint augmentation is necessary only for the enclosure of the population stability price; empirical endpoint input alone cannot certify that price.  The exact-input stable CAD selector is distinct from both countable selectors, and no agreement, zero-tolerance selector, uniform complexity bound, or finite arbitrary-real algorithm is asserted.
\par\smallskip\noindent\emph{Inputs.} \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}certified-\allowbreak{}input-\allowbreak{}interface}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}pilot-\allowbreak{}plugin}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}zhao-\allowbreak{}published-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}domain}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}completion-\allowbreak{}pseudoinverse-\allowbreak{}loss}; \texttt{def:\allowbreak{}rank-\allowbreak{}adaptive-\allowbreak{}stability-\allowbreak{}strata}.
\end{definition}

\section{Main results}

\begin{theorem}[thm:fixed-basis-endpoints]\label{thm:fixed-basis-endpoints}
Under ass:mutually-exclusive-exposures and ass:exposure-positivity, every \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\) has affine functions \(c\mapsto g_B(c)\) and \(c\mapsto\ell_B(c)\) on the imported feasible domain \(\mathcal C(F_1,F_0)=[c_L,c_U]\). Consequently, \(\inf_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}g_B(\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\})=\min\{g_B(c_L),g_B(c_U)\}\) and \(\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}\ell_B(\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\})=\max\{\ell_B(c_L),\ell_B(c_U)\}\), with both extrema attained. The product coupling gives \(c_L\le0\le c_U\), so \(g_B(0)\) is an ordinarily typed feasible-covariance gain. If \(c_L<0<c_U\), \(g_B(0)=\inf_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}g_B(\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\})\) if and only if the affine slope of \(g_B\) is zero.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) well defined.  For \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\), positive definiteness of \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\) makes \(S_B^{-1}\) well defined.  The product coupling and the imported Fr\'echet covariance domain give
\[
0\in\mathcal C(F_1,F_0)=[c_L,c_U].
\tag{1}
\]
Define
\[
E=\begin{bmatrix}0&I_n\\ I_n&0\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
A_B=\Pi^{-1}\Omega B S_B^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}.
\tag{2}
\]
By def:moment-matrix,
\[
Q(c)=Q(0)-cE.
\tag{3}
\]
Linearity and cyclicity of trace in def:gain-loss therefore give
\[
\begin{aligned}
g_B(c)
&=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\{A_BQ(c)\}\\
&=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\{A_BQ(0)\}
-c n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(A_BE).
\end{aligned}
\tag{4}
\]
Thus \(g_B\) is affine on \([c_L,c_U]\).

It remains to verify directly that the uncontrolled term is affine.  Put \(M=\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\).  For every unit \(i\), mutual exclusivity gives
\[
\operatorname{Cov}_{\nu}(D_{i,1},D_{i,0})
=\mathbb E_{\nu}(D_{i,1}D_{i,0})-\pi_{i,1}\pi_{i,0}
=-\pi_{i,1}\pi_{i,0}.
\tag{5}
\]
Because both exposure probabilities are positive, the \(i\)-th diagonal entry of either off-diagonal block of \(M\) is \(-1\).  Hence
\[
\operatorname{tr}(ME)=-2n.
\tag{6}
\]
Using (3), (6), and def:gain-loss,
\[
h(c)-h(0)
=-c n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(ME)
=\frac{2c}{n}.
\tag{7}
\]
Consequently \(h\) is affine, and \(\ell_B(c)=h(c)-g_B(c)\) is affine by (4) and (7).

Assumption ass:frechet-covariance-domain states the equality of sets
\[
\left\{\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\}:
H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)\right\}=[c_L,c_U].
\tag{8}
\]
Every affine real-valued function on the compact interval in (8) attains its minimum and maximum at endpoints.  Because (8) is equality of sets, both endpoints are attained by couplings in \(\Gamma(F_1,F_0)\).  Applying this fact to \(g_B\) and \(\ell_B\) proves
\[
\inf_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}
g_B\!\left(\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\}\right)
=\min\{g_B(c_L),g_B(c_U)\}
\tag{9}
\]
and
\[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}
\ell_B\!\left(\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\}\right)
=\max\{\ell_B(c_L),\ell_B(c_U)\},
\tag{10}
\]
with both extrema attained.  Equation (1) also proves \(c_L\le0\le c_U\) and shows that \(g_B(0)\) is evaluated at a feasible covariance.

Finally, write (4) as \(g_B(c)=\alpha_B+\beta_Bc\).  If \(\beta_B=0\), then \(g_B\) is constant, so its value at zero equals its infimum over (8).  Conversely, suppose \(c_L<0<c_U\).  If \(\beta_B>0\), then \(g_B(c_L)<g_B(0)\); if \(\beta_B<0\), then \(g_B(c_U)<g_B(0)\).  Therefore \(g_B(0)\) equals the worst-coupling gain if and only if \(\beta_B=0\).  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem reduces every fixed ordinary basis's copula adversary to two computable endpoint evaluations using only the algebraic design conditions and imported covariance interval. \textit{Gap.} It combines Zhao's covariance-dependent gain with Aronow--Green--Lee's sharp scalar endpoints; known exposure and i.i.d. pairs are not part of its algebraic proof basis. \textit{Consumer.} It feeds both robust optimization and the sequential pilot-to-worst-copula variance bridge.

\begin{proposition}[prop:kappa-exhaustion]\label{prop:kappa-exhaustion}
For every \(\kappa>0\), the quotient \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) is compact and satisfies \(\mathcal B_\kappa\subseteq\mathcal B_Z\).  Conversely, every \([B]\in\mathcal B_Z\) has normalized representatives belonging to \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) for some basis-dependent \(\kappa>0\), so
\[
\bigcup_{\kappa>0}\mathcal B_\kappa=\mathcal B_Z,
\qquad
q_Z^{-1}\!\left(\bigcup_{\kappa>0}\mathcal B_\kappa\right)=\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z.
\]
Thus the threshold classes exhaust the positive-definite rank-two projective restriction of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain, modulo the exact independent nonzero column-rescaling fibers of \(q_Z\).  Each fixed \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) is threshold restricted, and ranks zero and one in \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) are outside this proposition.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Let \(K_\kappa\) be the set of pairs \((b_1,b_0)\) satisfying the two sphere normalizations and \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}B^\top\Omega B)\ge\kappa\).  It is closed in a product of compact spheres.  Its quotient by the residual independent sign changes is therefore compact and equals \(\mathcal B_\kappa\).  Every member satisfies \(B^\top\Omega B\succeq n\kappa I_2\succ0\), hence lies in the positive-definite rank-two restriction \(\mathcal B_Z\) of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain.

Conversely, if \([B]\in\mathcal B_Z\), both arm columns are nonzero.  With
\[
T=\operatorname{diag}\left(\frac{\sqrt n}{\lVert b_1\rVert_2},
\frac{\sqrt n}{\lVert b_0\rVert_2}\right),
\]
the representative \(BT\) is normalized and \(T^\top B^\top\Omega BT\succ0\).  Thus \(\rho_B=\lambda_{\min}\{n^{-1}(BT)^\top\Omega(BT)\}>0\), and \([B]\in\mathcal B_{\rho_B/2}\).  Hence \(\bigcup_{\kappa>0}\mathcal B_\kappa=\mathcal B_Z\).  Surjectivity and the exact fibers of \(q_Z\), proved in thm:zhao-ordinary-domain-quotient-transfer, give the inverse-image equality.  This exhausts only the positive-definite rank-two restriction; ranks zero and one remain in \(\mathcal B_Z^+\). \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The compact threshold classes exhaust every ordinary-invertible block-separable matrix after quotienting independent nonzero arm-column rescaling. \textit{Gap.} Zhao2026OptimalControlVariates regulates supplied bases but does not construct this compact exhaustion or its quotient certificate. \textit{Consumer.} Certified optimizers can report \(\kappa\) while retaining an exact full ordinary-domain interpretation modulo rescaling.

\begin{theorem}[thm:relative-open-persistence]\label{thm:relative-open-persistence}
If \(\xi_0\in\mathfrak X_\kappa\) satisfies \(\Delta(\xi_0)>0\) and there is \(\eta>0\) such that \(\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)\cup\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)}\{\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}S_B)-\kappa\}\ge\eta\), then there is a relative-open neighborhood \(\mathcal U\subset\mathfrak X_\kappa\) of \(\xi_0\) such that \(\Delta(\xi)>0\) for every \(\xi\in\mathcal U\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
The optimizer-wide slack in the hypothesis is precisely the premise of lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity.  Suppose that no relative-open neighborhood of \(\xi_0\) had positive gap.  The relative topology in def:lawful-instance-class is metrizable, so for every integer \(j\ge1\) there would exist \(\xi_j\in\mathfrak X_\kappa\) at distance less than \(1/j\) from \(\xi_0\) with \(\Delta(\xi_j)\le0\).  Then \(\xi_j\to\xi_0\), whereas lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity gives
\[
0\ge\liminf_{j\to\infty}\Delta(\xi_j)
\ge\Delta(\xi_0)>0,
\]
a contradiction.  Hence some relative-open neighborhood \(\mathcal U\) of \(\xi_0\) satisfies \(\Delta(\xi)>0\) for every \(\xi\in\mathcal U\).  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Positive full-correspondence separation plus optimizer-wide regularity slack is sufficient for strict dominance to persist locally. \textit{Gap.} General dependence-robust optimization results do not establish this moving-feasible-set statement for Zhao-type projective bases. \textit{Consumer.} Any exact lawful witness satisfying the two displayed certificates activates a relative-open dominance region.

\begin{theorem}[thm:plugin-correspondence]\label{thm:plugin-correspondence}
Fix \(G,\nu,(f_i)_{i=1}^n\) satisfying ass:exposure-positivity and ass:kappa-feasibility, so \(\mathcal B_\kappa\neq\varnothing\).  Under ass:bounded-outcomes and ass:unpaired-pilot, as \(m_1\wedge m_0\to\infty\), \(\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|\to0\) almost surely, \(\widehat v_{\mathrm{mm}}\to v_{\mathrm{mm}}\) almost surely, and every subsequential limit of \(B_m\in\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\) belongs to \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) almost surely.  Equivalently, \(\limsup_{m_1,m_0\to\infty}\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\subseteq\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) almost surely in the projective quotient.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
By \(\texttt{ass:kappa-feasibility}\) and \(\texttt{prop:kappa-exhaustion}\), \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) is a nonempty compact metrizable projective quotient.  For every normalized representative \(B\in\mathcal B_\kappa\), block separability and projective normalization give
\[
B^{\top}B=nI_2,
\qquad
\lVert B\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\sqrt n,
\qquad
\lVert S_B^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le (n\kappa)^{-1}.
\tag{1}
\]
The last inequality follows from \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\succeq n\kappa I_2\), so every inverse used below exists uniformly over the feasible class.

We first prove convergence of the two endpoint moment matrices from the pilot primitives.  For arm \(a\), write \(F_{a,m_a}\) for the distribution function of \(\widehat F_a\).  At every fixed rational \(t\in[0,1]\), the variables
\[
X_{j,a}(t)=\mathbf 1\{Y^P_{j,a}\le t\}
\]
are independent Bernoulli variables with mean \(F_a(t)\) by \(\texttt{ass:unpaired-pilot}\).  If \(X\) is Bernoulli with mean \(p\), then
\[
\mathbb E\exp\{\lambda(X-p)\}
=e^{-\lambda p}(1-p+pe^\lambda)\le e^{\lambda^2/8}.
\tag{2}
\]
Indeed, the second derivative of \(\log(1-p+pe^\lambda)\) is the variance of a tilted Bernoulli variable and is at most \(1/4\), while the centered logarithm and its first derivative vanish at zero.  Exponential Markov inequality, independence, and optimization over \(\lambda\) therefore yield
\[
P\!\left(\left|F_{a,m_a}(t)-F_a(t)\right|>s\right)
\le 2e^{-2m_as^2},
\qquad s>0.
\tag{3}
\]
For rational \(s>0\), the right-hand side is summable in \(m_a\).  The union bound on tail unions, followed by intersection over the countable rational pairs \((t,s)\), proves simultaneous almost-sure convergence at every rational \(t\).

Fix \(K\ge1\) and put \(x_j=j/K\).  Monotonicity of the empirical and population distribution functions implies, for \(y\in[x_j,x_{j+1}]\),
\[
|F_{a,m_a}(y)-F_a(y)|
\le
\max_{r\in\{j,j+1\}}|F_{a,m_a}(x_r)-F_a(x_r)|
+F_a(x_{j+1})-F_a(x_j).
\tag{4}
\]
Integrating, summing over \(j\), taking the limit superior, and telescoping the population increments give
\[
\limsup_{m_a\to\infty}
\int_0^1|F_{a,m_a}(y)-F_a(y)|\,dy\le K^{-1}
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{5}
\]
Letting \(K\to\infty\) makes the integral converge to zero.  For distribution functions \(R,S\) supported on \([0,1]\), Tonelli's theorem applied to threshold indicators gives the one-dimensional identity
\[
\int_0^1|R^{-1}(u)-S^{-1}(u)|\,du
=\int_0^1|R(y)-S(y)|\,dy.
\tag{6}
\]
To verify (6), express the absolute difference of two numbers in \([0,1]\) as the integral of the absolute difference of their threshold indicators and use
\(\mathbf 1\{R^{-1}(u)\le y\}=\mathbf 1\{u\le R(y)\}\) outside a null set.  Consequently,
\[
\int_0^1|\widehat F_a^{-1}(u)-F_a^{-1}(u)|\,du\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely},
\qquad a\in\{1,0\}.
\tag{7}
\]
The support condition used in this step is exactly \(\texttt{ass:bounded-outcomes}\).

Since \(y\mapsto y\) and \(y\mapsto y^2\) are respectively one- and two-Lipschitz on \([0,1]\), (7) gives convergence of the empirical means, second moments, and variances.  Moreover,
\[
|xy-x'y'|\le |x-x'|+|y-y'|,
\qquad x,x',y,y'\in[0,1].
\tag{8}
\]
Applying (8) first to the comonotone quantiles and then to the antimonotone quantiles proves convergence of both empirical Fr\'echet cross moments and hence
\[
\widehat c_L\to c_L,
\qquad
\widehat c_U\to c_U
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{9}
\]
By the block formula in \(\texttt{def:moment-matrix}\) and the endpoint construction in \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\), every block of \(\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\) is a convergent scalar multiplying either \(I_n\) or \(\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^{\top}\).  Since \(n\) is fixed,
\[
R_m:=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}
\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{10}
\]

It remains to transfer (10) uniformly through the design criterion.  Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) a fixed finite matrix.  For the uncontrolled term, the trace inequality \(|\operatorname{tr}(MZ)|\le\lVert M\rVert_*\lVert Z\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\) gives
\[
n^{-2}\left|
\operatorname{tr}\!\left[\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
\{\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\}\right]\right|
\le {2\over n}\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}R_m.
\tag{11}
\]
For the gain term, cyclicity of trace, (1), and
\(\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega B\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
\le\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\sqrt n\) give, uniformly in \(B\),
\[
\begin{split}
&n^{-2}\left|\operatorname{tr}\!\left[
S_B^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
\{\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\}
\Pi^{-1}\Omega B\right]\right|\\
&\hspace{25mm}\le
{2\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^2
\over n^2\kappa}R_m.
\end{split}
\tag{12}
\]
The elementary inequality
\(\left|\max_e x_e-\max_e y_e\right|\le\max_e|x_e-y_e|\), together with (11)--(12), supplies a finite constant \(K_0\), depending only on the fixed design and \(\kappa\), such that
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|
\le K_0R_m\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{13}
\]

On \(\mathcal B_\kappa\), the map \(B\mapsto S_B^{-1}\) is continuous because of the uniform spectral lower bound in (1).  All remaining matrix operations and the maximum over the two endpoints are continuous and invariant under the projective sign representatives.  Thus \(w\) and every \(\widehat w\) are continuous on the compact quotient, and their minima are attained.  The elementary comparison of two minima gives
\[
|\widehat v_{\mathrm{mm}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}|
\le\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|
\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{14}
\]

Finally, work on the probability-one event on which (13) holds.  Let the sample sizes tend to infinity along an arbitrary sequence, let \(B_m\in\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\), and suppose a subsequence converges to \(B_\infty\).  Choose any \(B^\star\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\), which exists by compactness.  Empirical optimality and (13) imply
\[
w(B_m)
\le w(B^\star)+2\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}
|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|.
\tag{15}
\]
Continuity and passage to the convergent subsequence give \(w(B_\infty)\le w(B^\star)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}\).  The reverse inequality follows from the definition of the minimum, so \(B_\infty\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\).  Since the sequence and convergent subsequence were arbitrary, this is exactly
\[
\limsup_{m_1,m_0\to\infty}\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}
\subseteq\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}
\]
in the projective quotient.  The argument concerns only the fixed-\(\kappa\), positive-definite rank-two class and makes no assertion across singular pseudoinverse strata. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Uniform convergence over the compact regular projective class yields value consistency and the strongest generic set-valued optimizer guarantee. \textit{Gap.} Marginal endpoint consistency alone does not imply uniform global objective or argmin-correspondence consistency. \textit{Consumer.} An independent unpaired pilot may select a robust basis without claiming to observe the cross-world copula.

\begin{proposition}[prop:unique-saddle-point-convergence]\label{prop:unique-saddle-point-convergence}
If \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\{B^\star\}\) in the projective quotient, then every measurable selection \(\widehat B\in\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\) converges almost surely to \(B^\star\).  Without the singleton condition, there exist bounded two-marginal instances satisfying the maintained assumptions for which the outer-set conclusion of thm:plugin-correspondence holds but some measurable plug-in selections fail to converge to a point.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Equip the compact metrizable quotient \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) with any compatible metric \(d\), and work on the probability-one event furnished by \(\texttt{thm:plugin-correspondence}\).  Suppose first that \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\{B^\star\}\).  If a selection \(\widehat B\in\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\) did not converge to \(B^\star\), there would be a number \(\epsilon_0>0\) and a sequence of pilot-size pairs with \(m_1\wedge m_0\to\infty\) such that
\[
d(\widehat B,B^\star)\ge\epsilon_0.
\tag{16}
\]
Compactness supplies a convergent subsequence, with limit \(B_\infty\).  The outer-limit conclusion of \(\texttt{thm:plugin-correspondence}\) gives \(B_\infty\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\{B^\star\}\), whereas continuity of \(d\) and (16) give \(d(B_\infty,B^\star)\ge\epsilon_0\), a contradiction.  Thus every such selection converges almost surely.  Measurability ensures that the selected basis is a legitimate pilot statistic; the pathwise compactness argument itself needs no stronger selection condition.

We next give an explicit bounded counterexample showing why singletonness cannot be omitted.  Let \(n=3\), take the empty fixed network, put \(f_i(w)=w_i\), and let \(W_1,W_2,W_3\) be independent Bernoulli variables with success probability \(1/2\).  Then
\[
D=(W_1,W_2,W_3,1-W_1,1-W_2,1-W_3)^{\top},
\qquad
\Pi={1\over2}I_6,
\]
and direct covariance calculation gives
\[
\Omega={1\over4}
\begin{bmatrix}
I_3&-I_3\\
-I_3&I_3
\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{17}
\]
Both exposure labels therefore have probability \(1/2\) for every unit.  Set every potential outcome equal to zero under every assignment, take
\[
H=\delta_{(0,0)},
\qquad
F_1=F_0=\delta_0,
\tag{18}
\]
and let both pilot samples consist of their corresponding independent degenerate draws.  This instance has bounded outcomes, known exposures, mutually exclusive exposure events, identically distributed potential-outcome pairs, fixed marginals, exposure positivity, and an unpaired pilot independent of the main assignment.  Degenerate random variables are independent, so the independence clauses are satisfied literally rather than only vacuously.

Let \(e_1,e_2,e_3\) denote the coordinate vectors in \(\mathbb R^3\), and consider
\[
B^{(2)}=
\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt3e_1&0\\0&\sqrt3e_2\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
B^{(3)}=
\begin{bmatrix}\sqrt3e_1&0\\0&\sqrt3e_3\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{19}
\]
Their columns satisfy the required squared-norm-three normalization.  By (17) and orthogonality of the displayed arm vectors,
\[
3^{-1}S_{B^{(2)}}=3^{-1}S_{B^{(3)}}={1\over4}I_2.
\tag{20}
\]
Thus, for example, both classes belong to \(\mathcal B_{1/8}\).  They are distinct in the projective quotient because their comparison-arm lines are respectively \(\operatorname{span}(e_2)\) and \(\operatorname{span}(e_3)\).

Under (18), \(\mu_1=\mu_0=\sigma_1^2=\sigma_0^2=c_L=c_U=0\), and hence \(Q(0)=0\).  The pilot empirical laws equal the same point masses for every positive pair of sample sizes, so also \(\widehat Q_L=\widehat Q_U=0\).  The definitions of the population and plug-in losses therefore give, for every \(B\in\mathcal B_{1/8}\),
\[
w(B)=0,
\qquad
\widehat w(B)=0,
\qquad
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}
=\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}
=\mathcal B_{1/8}.
\tag{21}
\]
In particular, the outer-set conclusion holds identically.  Define a deterministic, hence measurable, plug-in selection by choosing \(B^{(2)}\) when \(m_1\) is even and \(B^{(3)}\) when \(m_1\) is odd.  It belongs to \(\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\mathrm{mm}}\) for every pilot sample by (21), but along \(m_1=m_0\to\infty\) it alternates between the two distinct quotient points in (19) and therefore has no point limit.  This proves the asserted existential failure without singletonness. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The corrected statement shows that uniqueness on the compact quotient automatically creates every required neighborhood gap; endpoint uniqueness is unnecessary. \textit{Gap.} Outer-set consistency cannot imply convergence of arbitrary selections under ties, as the explicit degenerate bounded instance demonstrates. \textit{Consumer.} Applied reports should claim point convergence only after verifying a unique projective minimizer.

\begin{lemma}[lem:restricted-marginal-extrema-continuity]\label{lem:restricted-marginal-extrema-continuity}
Let \(E_1,E_0\) be compact metric spaces.  Under weak convergence of an ordered pair of marginal probability measures, the fixed-marginal coupling correspondence is continuous in the weak topology.  Consequently, for every continuous function \(\varphi:E_1\times E_0\to\mathbb R\), the minimum and maximum of \(H\mapsto\int\varphi\,dH\) over the coupling set vary continuously with the ordered marginal pair, and the associated optimizer correspondences are upper hemicontinuous.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[lem:real-closed-field-quantifier-elimination]\label{lem:real-closed-field-quantifier-elimination}
For Collins's EVAL/ELIM construction applied to a first-order formula \(\Phi\) over a real closed field, the returned standard quantifier-free formula \(\psi\) is equivalent to \(\Phi\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{theorem}[thm:zhao-ordinary-domain-quotient-transfer]\label{thm:zhao-ordinary-domain-quotient-transfer}
Let \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\) be the unquotiented positive-definite rank-two restriction of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain, and let \(q_Z(B)=[B]\).  Then \(q_Z:\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\to\mathcal B_Z\) is surjective, and
\[
q_Z(B)=q_Z(C)
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
C=B\operatorname{diag}(t_1,t_0)
\quad\text{for some }t_1t_0\ne0.
\]
For every such diagonal \(T\), every \(c\in\mathcal C(F_1,F_0)\), and every \(B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\),
\[
g_{q_Z(BT)}(c)=g_{q_Z(B)}(c),\qquad
\ell_{q_Z(BT)}(c)=\ell_{q_Z(B)}(c),\qquad
w(q_Z(BT))=w(q_Z(B)).
\]
Consequently, for every nonempty \(K\subseteq\mathcal B_Z\), the infima and suprema of each displayed pullback criterion over \(q_Z^{-1}(K)\) equal those over \(K\), and every attained optimizer correspondence is the inverse image of its quotient correspondence.  This theorem concerns only the rank-two restriction; the published pseudoinverse strata are carried by \(\mathcal B_Z^+\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Positive definiteness makes both arm columns nonzero, so the quotient map from \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\) onto \(\mathcal B_Z\) is well defined and surjective.  The projective equivalence relation gives
\[
q_Z(B)=q_Z(C)\Longleftrightarrow C=BT,
\qquad T=\operatorname{diag}(t_1,t_0),\quad t_1t_0\ne0.
\tag{1}
\]
For such \(T\),
\[
S_{BT}=T^\top S_BT,
\qquad S_{BT}^{-1}=T^{-1}S_B^{-1}(T^\top)^{-1}.
\tag{2}
\]
Substitution in the trace formula and cyclicity cancel every factor of \(T\), proving \(g_{q_Z(BT)}(c)=g_{q_Z(B)}(c)\).  The uncontrolled term is basis independent, so the residual and worst-endpoint loss are invariant as well.  Any invariant function is constant on every fiber of a surjection; its extrema over an inverse image equal those over the quotient set, and an attained optimizer pulls back exactly to the inverse image of the quotient optimizer set.  This is an exact transfer for the positive-definite rank-two restriction of Zhao's larger published pseudoinverse class. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Exact rescaling-fiber transfer for the positive-definite rank-two restriction. \textit{Gap.} The unquotiented object here is only the ordinary rank-two restriction used for the note's comparison construction; it is not the note's rank-stratified projective completion. \textit{Consumer.} Fixed-threshold exhaustion and triangle rank-two separation use this exact quotient map.

\begin{lemma}[lem:ht-affine-drift]\label{lem:ht-affine-drift}
Let \(h(c)=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}[\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Q(c)]\).  Under ass:mutually-exclusive-exposures, ass:exposure-positivity, ass:frechet-covariance-domain, and ass:product-coupling-zero,
\[
h(c)=h(0)+\frac{2c}{n}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
w(B)=h(0)+\max\left\{\frac{2c_L}{n}-g_B(c_L),\frac{2c_U}{n}-g_B(c_U)\right\}.
\]
If \(c_L<c_U\), minimization of \(w(B)\) is not algebraically equivalent to maximization of \(r(B)=\min\{g_B(c_L),g_B(c_U)\}\): as functions of a formal endpoint-gain pair \((x_L,x_U)\), the two loss criteria are not related by a constant independent of \((x_L,x_U)\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Put \(E=\begin{bmatrix}0&I_n\\I_n&0\end{bmatrix}\) and \(A_{\mathrm{HT}}=\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\).  Def:moment-matrix gives \(Q(c)=Q(0)-cE\).  Mutual exclusivity implies
\[
\Omega_{i,n+i}=\operatorname{Cov}_\nu(D_{i,1},D_{i,0})
=-\pi_{i,1}\pi_{i,0}.
\]
Exposure positivity permits division, so symmetry gives \(\operatorname{tr}(A_{\mathrm{HT}}E)=-2n\).  Therefore
\[
h(c)=h(0)-cn^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(A_{\mathrm{HT}}E)
=h(0)+2c/n.
\]
Subtracting the endpoint gains yields the displayed formula for \(w(B)\).

To prove non-equivalence, put \(a_e=2c_e/n\) and define on a formal endpoint-gain pair \(x\)
\[
\Phi(x)=\max\{a_L-x_L,a_U-x_U\},
\qquad \Psi(x)=-\min\{x_L,x_U\}.
\]
If \(\Phi=\Psi+K\) for all \(x\), evaluating at \((t,0)\) gives \(K=a_U\), while evaluating at \((0,t)\) for \(t\ge a_U-a_L\) gives \(K=a_L\).  This contradicts \(c_L<c_U\). \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The drift identity is the algebraic reason the primary decision must minimize residual variance rather than maximize guaranteed gain. \textit{Gap.} Neither the imported covariance bounds nor the covariance-dependent gain formula accounts for the endpoint-dependent uncontrolled variance term in the robust decision. \textit{Consumer.} The formula lets a downstream reader compute the endpoint residual losses and detect which endpoint is adversarial.

\begin{theorem}[thm:moment-complete-finite-epsilon-solver]\label{thm:moment-complete-finite-epsilon-solver}
For every moment-complete input \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\) satisfying exposure positivity and \(\kappa\)-feasibility, and every exactly encoded \(\varepsilon>0\), the candidate projective interval branch-and-bound procedure terminates after finitely many subdivisions and returns a checkable certificate \(\mathscr K_{\varepsilon}\) such that
\[
L_{\varepsilon}\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}\le w(B_{\varepsilon})\le U_{\varepsilon},
\qquad
U_{\varepsilon}-L_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon,
\qquad
h_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon,
\qquad
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\subseteq\mathcal C_{\varepsilon}.
\]
Here \(B_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal B_\kappa\) is represented exactly, including when the feasible set has no relative interior and lies on \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega B)=\kappa\).  A chart box meeting that equality boundary is never discarded as infeasible; it may be discarded only when its certified objective lower bound exceeds the incumbent upper bound.  Consequently, along any sequence \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\), the certified value gaps and retained-box mesh diameters converge to zero.  This is a finite, instance-dependent termination result; it asserts neither a uniform complexity bound nor a semidefinite-program reduction.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Let
\[
K_\kappa=\{(b_1,b_0):\lVert b_1\rVert_2^2=\lVert b_0\rVert_2^2=n,
\ B^{\top}\Omega B-n\kappa I_2\succeq0\}.
\tag{1}
\]
Ass:kappa-feasibility makes \(K_\kappa\) nonempty.  It is closed in a product of two spheres and hence compact; prop:kappa-exhaustion identifies its finite sign quotient with \(\mathcal B_\kappa\).  On (1),
\[
\lVert(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le(n\kappa)^{-1}.
\tag{2}
\]
The trace formulas in def:gain-loss, the resolvent identity, and (2) make both endpoint losses, and hence their maximum \(w\), continuous on \(K_\kappa\).  The refinement moduli in \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\) turn this compactness argument into an effective uniform-continuity modulus: for every positive rational \(\rho\), the inclusion oracles terminate with a rational chart diameter \(d(\rho)>0\) such that two feasible representatives in the same box of diameter at most \(d(\rho)\) have losses differing by at most \(\rho\).

Cover each normalized projective factor by the finitely many signed pivot charts in def:branch-bound-handle.  Fix a rational \(\rho>0\) with \(4\rho<\varepsilon\), and bisect all retained boxes until their diameters are at most \(\min\{\varepsilon,d(\rho)\}\).  This is a finite depth and produces finitely many boxes.  The exact feasibility interface decides every box, including boxes whose feasible intersection lies entirely on
\(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega B)=\kappa\).  In each feasible box, exact feasible-child selection and repeated nested refinement give the certified inclusion-oracle name of an exactly feasible point \(B_C\).  The endpoint inclusion oracles and their effective arithmetic moduli then terminate with outward rational bounds \(\underline q_C,\overline q_C\) satisfying
\[
w(B_C)-\rho\le\underline q_C\le w(B_C)
\le\overline q_C\le w(B_C)+\rho.
\tag{3}
\]
Uniform continuity supplies the valid box lower bound
\[
\lambda_C:=\underline q_C-\rho
\le\inf_{B\in C\cap K_\kappa}w(B).
\tag{4}
\]
Set \(L_\varepsilon=\min_C\lambda_C\), \(U_\varepsilon=\min_C\overline q_C\), and choose \(B_\varepsilon\) from a box attaining the latter minimum.  A box containing a compact-class minimizer, (3), and (4) give
\[
v_{\mathrm{mm}}-2\rho\le L_\varepsilon\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}
\le w(B_\varepsilon)\le U_\varepsilon\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}+2\rho.
\tag{5}
\]
Thus \(U_\varepsilon-L_\varepsilon<\varepsilon\).  Prune only boxes certified infeasible or having a valid lower bound strictly above the incumbent upper bound.  No box meeting \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) can satisfy either condition, so the union \(\mathcal C_\varepsilon\) of unpruned quotient boxes contains \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\).  The prescribed refinement gives \(h_\varepsilon\le\varepsilon\).

Every stage terminates: the chart cover and subdivision depth are finite, and every requested feasibility, arithmetic, and inclusion-oracle refinement terminates by def:certified-input-interface.  The finite record is exactly \(\mathscr K_\varepsilon\) of def:certified-output.  Letting \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) in (5) and the mesh bound proves convergence of both certified gaps.  The construction is instance dependent and supplies neither a uniform complexity bound nor an SDP reduction. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} From compact quotient charts, effective loss moduli, exact feasibility decisions, and nested feasible-box extraction, the theorem derives finite termination and a checkable \(\varepsilon\)-global value and outer-minimizer certificate. \textit{Gap.} The guarantee is finite and instance-dependent; it provides neither a uniform complexity bound nor a semidefinite-program reduction. \textit{Consumer.} It is the global optimality certificate for choosing a minimax-residual basis from the fixed normalized \(\kappa\)-regular projective class.

\begin{lemma}[lem:zhao-rank-two-stratum-identification]\label{lem:zhao-rank-two-stratum-identification}
Define \(\iota:\mathcal B_Z\to\mathcal B_Z^+\) by \(\iota([B])=[B]_+\).  This map is a well-defined bijection from \(\mathcal B_Z\) onto
\[
 \{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:\operatorname{rank}(B^{\top}\Omega B)=2\}.
\]
For every \([B]\in\mathcal B_Z\) and every \(c\in\mathcal C(F_1,F_0)\),
\[
 g_{\iota(B)}^+(c)=g_B(c),
 \qquad \ell_{\iota(B)}^+(c)=\ell_B(c),
 \qquad w^+(\iota(B))=w(B).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
If \([B]\in\mathcal B_Z\), then \(S_B\succ0\), both arm columns are nonzero, and \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)=2\).  On this stratum the equivalence relation of def:zhao-published-pseudoinverse-domain is exactly the independent nonzero rescaling relation defining \(\mathcal B_Z\).  Hence \(\iota([B])=[B]_+\) is well defined and injective.  Conversely, a rank-two positive-semidefinite \(S_B\) is positive definite, so its class has a unique preimage in \(\mathcal B_Z\).  On rank two, \(S_B^+=S_B^{-1}\).  Substitution into the two gain definitions proves equality of gains; subtraction from the same \(h(c)\) and endpoint maximization prove equality of losses. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Identifies the note's ordinary rank-two quotient with the rank-two stratum in this note's constructed rank-stratified projective completion. \textit{Gap.} It does not call the quotient itself a class published by Zhao. \textit{Consumer.} Projector approximation and all full-class triangle transfers.

\begin{lemma}[lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction]\label{lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction}
For \(e\in\{L,U\}\), set \(A_e=\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}Q(c_e)\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}\) and \(Z_B=\Omega^{1/2}B\).  Every \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\) satisfies
\[
 g_B^+(c_e)=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(P_BA_e),
 \qquad P_B=Z_B(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)^+Z_B^{\top},
\]
where \(P_B\) is the orthogonal projector onto \(\operatorname{range}(Z_B)\), \(A_e\succeq0\), and \(\operatorname{rank}(P_B)=\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\in\{0,1,2\}\).  On rank two, \([B]_+=\iota([C])\) for a unique \([C]\in\mathcal B_Z\), and the gains and losses agree.  Define
\[
r_\Omega=\max_{[C]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\operatorname{rank}(C^{\top}\Omega C).
\]
For every \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\), there is a sequence \(([B_j]_+)_{j\ge1}\subseteq\mathcal B_Z^+\) with \(\operatorname{rank}(S_{B_j})=r_\Omega\) and a projector \(P_\infty\) such that
\[
P_{B_j}\longrightarrow P_\infty,
\qquad P_\infty-P_B\succeq0.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\liminf_{j\to\infty}g_{B_j}^+(c_e)\ge g_B^+(c_e)
\quad(e\in\{L,U\}),
\qquad
\limsup_{j\to\infty}w^+(B_j)\le w^+(B).
\]
If \(r_\Omega=2\), the approximating sequence may be chosen in \(\iota(\mathcal B_Z)\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) well defined.  Since each endpoint moment matrix is positive semidefinite, congruence gives
\[
A_e=\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}Q(c_e)\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}\succeq0.
\tag{1}
\]
Put \(Z_B=\Omega^{1/2}B\).  Then \(S_B=Z_B^{\top}Z_B\), and cyclicity of trace yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
n^2g_B^+(c_e)
&=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)^+Z_B^{\top}A_eZ_B\right]\\
&=\operatorname{tr}\!\left[Z_B(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)^+Z_B^{\top}A_e\right]
=\operatorname{tr}(P_BA_e).
\end{aligned}
\tag{2}
\]
The singular-value decomposition of \(Z_B\) shows that
\[
P_B=Z_B(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)^+Z_B^{\top}
\tag{3}
\]
is the orthogonal projector onto \(\operatorname{range}(Z_B)\).  Thus
\[
\operatorname{rank}(P_B)=\operatorname{rank}(Z_B)
=\operatorname{rank}(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)
=\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\in\{0,1,2\}.
\tag{4}
\]
The rank-two identification and equality of the ordinary and pseudoinverse gains and losses are exactly \(\texttt{lem:zhao-rank-two-stratum-identification}\).

The zero class belongs to \(\mathcal B_Z^+\), and the ranks in (4) take values in the finite set \(\{0,1,2\}\); hence \(r_\Omega\) exists.  We construct the asserted sequence by cases.

Suppose first that \(r_\Omega=2\).  Choose \([C]_+\) with \(Z_C\) of rank two and, for a representative of \([B]_+\), put
\[
Z(t)=Z_B+tZ_C.
\tag{5}
\]
The function
\[
p(t)=\det\{Z(t)^{\top}Z(t)\}
\tag{6}
\]
is a polynomial.  Its coefficient of \(t^4\) is \(\det(Z_C^{\top}Z_C)>0\), so it is not the zero polynomial.  Choose \(t_j\downarrow0\) outside its finitely many roots.  Then \(Z(t_j)\) has rank two.  Independent normalization of its two nonzero arm columns does not alter its quotient class or projector, so it defines \([B_j]_+\in\iota(\mathcal B_Z)\).

The set of rank-two orthogonal projectors in the finite-dimensional matrix space is compact.  After taking a subsequence and relabeling, let
\[
P_{B_j}\longrightarrow P_\infty.
\tag{7}
\]
For any \(v\in\operatorname{range}(Z_B)\), write \(v=Z_Bx\).  Since \(Z(t_j)x\in\operatorname{range}\{Z(t_j)\}\),
\[
\operatorname{dist}\!\left(v,\operatorname{range}\{Z(t_j)\}\right)
\le |t_j|\,\lVert Z_Cx\rVert_2\longrightarrow0.
\tag{8}
\]
Taking the limit in (8) gives \(P_\infty v=v\).  Therefore
\(
\operatorname{range}(P_B)\subseteq\operatorname{range}(P_\infty)
\), and for orthogonal projectors this is equivalent to
\[
P_\infty-P_B\succeq0.
\tag{9}
\]

Suppose next that \(r_\Omega=1\).  If \(B\) has rank one, take the constant sequence \(B_j=B\).  If \(B\) has rank zero, choose any rank-one class \([C]_+\), take \(B_j=C\), and put \(P_\infty=P_C\); then \(P_B=0\), so (9) again holds.  Finally, if \(r_\Omega=0\), every class has projector zero and the constant sequence suffices.  These cases prove the projector approximation for every design, including designs with no rank-two class.

By (1), (2), (7), (9), and trace monotonicity,
\[
\lim_{j\to\infty}g_{B_j}^+(c_e)
=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(P_\infty A_e)
\ge n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(P_BA_e)
=g_B^+(c_e)
\tag{10}
\]
for \(e=L,U\).  The uncontrolled endpoint terms do not depend on the basis.  Subtracting (10) from those terms and taking the maximum of the two endpoint residuals gives
\[
\limsup_{j\to\infty}w^+(B_j)\le w^+(B).
\tag{11}
\]
This proves every assertion. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The projector representation isolates the only rank-dependent part of every endpoint gain and proves domination by maximal achievable rank without assuming rank two exists. \textit{Gap.} A rank-two-only perturbation cannot support full-carrier selection on designs whose attainable rank is one or zero. \textit{Consumer.} The maximal-rank countable selector and both full-carrier pilot regret results use the projector convergence and positive-semidefinite trace monotonicity.

\begin{theorem}[thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-rank-stratified-epsilon-solver]\label{thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-rank-stratified-epsilon-solver}
For every rational or real-algebraic moment-complete input \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\) satisfying ass:exposure-positivity and every exactly encoded \(\varepsilon>0\), a rank-stratified cylindrical-algebraic procedure terminates and returns rational or real-algebraic bounds and a basis class \([B_\varepsilon]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\) in the note's quotient of Zhao's published carrier satisfying
\[
L_\varepsilon^+\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\le U_\varepsilon^+,
\qquad U_\varepsilon^+-L_\varepsilon^+\le\varepsilon,
\qquad w^+(B_\varepsilon)\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon.
\]
The procedure splits \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\in\{0,1,2\}\), represents the pseudoinverse objective by rational semialgebraic formulas on each stratum, and returns a finite checkable CAD sign, sample, and value-isolation certificate covering all strata.  The set \(\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:w^+(B)\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon\}\) is nonempty and semialgebraic.  No exact minimizer, outer convergence to \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\), uniform complexity bound, or semidefinite-program reduction is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Let \(K_0\) be the ordered field generated over \(\mathbb Q\) by the finitely many entries of \(\Pi\), \(\Omega\), \(Q(c_L)\), and \(Q(c_U)\), together with the other exactly encoded input constants.  The rational or real-algebraic input condition in def:certified-input-interface makes every element of \(K_0\) real algebraic.  By ass:exposure-positivity, every diagonal entry of \(\Pi\) is strictly positive.  Hence \(\Pi^{-1}\) exists and its entries belong to \(K_0\).  The endpoint matrices \(Q(c_L)\) and \(Q(c_U)\), as well as the design covariance \(\Omega\), are positive semidefinite by their declared spaces.  These facts provide every support, division, and positivity condition used below.

\emph{Normalized semialgebraic representation of the quotient.}
By def:zhao-published-pseudoinverse-domain, every class in \(\mathcal B_Z^+\), the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published all-block-separable carrier, has a representative for which each arm column is either zero or has squared Euclidean norm \(n\).  It therefore suffices to work on
\[
\mathcal K=
\left\{(b_1,b_0):
\bigl(b_1=0\ \vee\ b_1^{\top}b_1=n\bigr),\quad
\bigl(b_0=0\ \vee\ b_0^{\top}b_0=n\bigr)
\right\}.
\tag{1}
\]
This is a union of four compact semialgebraic sets over \(K_0\).
