For literal quotient coordinates, for each nonzero arm choose its first nonzero coordinate and require that coordinate to be positive.  The finitely many resulting semialgebraic sign charts form a one-to-one cross-section of the independent-rescaling quotient.

The objective is constant on quotient fibers, including at singular ranks.  Let \(C=\Omega^{1/2}B\), and, for \(e\in\{L,U\}\), let
\[
R_e=\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}Q(c_e)\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}.
\]
The square roots are proof intermediates only; the algorithm below uses polynomial formulas in the encoded entries.  Since \(S_B=C^{\top}C\), cyclicity of trace and def:completion-pseudoinverse-loss give
\[
g_B^+(c_e)
=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\!\left[
C(C^{\top}C)^+C^{\top}R_e
\right].
\tag{2}
\]
If \(C=U\Sigma V^{\top}\) is a singular-value decomposition, then
\[
C(C^{\top}C)^+C^{\top}
=U\operatorname{diag}\!\bigl(\mathbf 1\{\sigma_j>0\}\bigr)U^{\top},
\]
which is the orthogonal projector onto \(\operatorname{col}(C)\).  Independent nonzero rescaling of either nonzero arm column preserves that column space.  Hence (2), and therefore \(w^+(B)\), is quotient invariant at ranks zero, one, and two.  Moreover,
\[
0\preceq C(C^{\top}C)^+C^{\top}\preceq I,
\qquad R_e\succeq0.
\]
Taking traces after multiplication by \(R_e\), and using \(n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(R_e)=h(c_e)\), yields
\[
0\le g_B^+(c_e)\le h(c_e),
\qquad
0\le \ell_B^+(c_e)\le h(c_e).
\tag{3}
\]
Equation (3) implies \(0\le w^+(B)\le H:=\max\{h(c_L),h(c_U)\}<\infty\) for every normalized representative.  Hence the attainable loss set is nonempty because the all-zero representative lies in \(\mathcal K\), and it is bounded below by \(0\) (indeed contained in \([0,H]\)); no finiteness-in-cardinality assertion is used.

\emph{Exact rank-stratified graph.}
Write \(S=S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\).  Since \(\Omega\succeq0\), the matrix \(S\) is a \(2\times2\) positive-semidefinite matrix.  Its possible ranks and spectral pseudoinverses are exactly
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
\operatorname{rank}(S)&\text{semialgebraic condition}&S^+\\ \hline
0&\operatorname{tr}(S)=0&0,\\[2mm]
1&\operatorname{tr}(S)>0,\ \det(S)=0&
\displaystyle\frac{S}{\{\operatorname{tr}(S)\}^2},\\[3mm]
2&\det(S)>0&
\displaystyle\frac{\operatorname{adj}(S)}{\det(S)}.
\end{array}
\tag{4}
\]
Indeed, positive semidefiniteness makes trace zero equivalent to rank zero.  At rank one, if the nonzero eigenvalue is \(\lambda=\operatorname{tr}(S)\) and \(S=\lambda uu^{\top}\) with \(\lVert u\rVert_2=1\), then \(S/\{\operatorname{tr}(S)\}^2=\lambda^{-1}uu^{\top}=S^+\).  At rank two, positive semidefiniteness and \(\det(S)>0\) imply positive definiteness, so the last expression is the ordinary inverse.  Every denominator in (4) is strictly positive on its stated stratum; at a boundary the appropriate lower-rank row applies.

For each row of (4), substitute the displayed rational expression into the two endpoint gains.  Introduce variables \(z_L,z_U,y\) and impose
\[
z_e=h(c_e)-g_B^+(c_e)\quad(e\in\{L,U\}),
\qquad y\ge z_L,
\qquad y\ge z_U,
\qquad (y=z_L\ \vee\ y=z_U).
\tag{5}
\]
On rank one multiply the gain equalities by the strictly positive quantity \(\{\operatorname{tr}(S)\}^2\); on rank two multiply them by the strictly positive quantity \(\det(S)\); no denominator occurs on rank zero.  Consequently (1), the applicable row of (4), and (5) form a finite Boolean combination of polynomial equalities and strict or weak inequalities over \(K_0\).  Their union is the semialgebraic graph
\[
\mathcal G=
\{(b_1,b_0,y):(b_1,b_0)\in\mathcal K,\ y=w^+(B)\}.
\tag{6}
\]
It covers every normalized representative and all three ranks, including equality boundaries, without evaluating an inverse at a zero denominator.

\emph{Exact value isolation.}
Define
\[
\mathcal T=
\left\{t\in\mathbb R:
\exists(b_1,b_0,y)\in\mathcal G\ \text{such that}\ y\le t
\right\}.
\tag{7}
\]
By (3), \(\mathcal T\) is nonempty and bounded below, and by the definitions of \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) and \(\mathcal G\),
\[
\inf\mathcal T
=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B)
=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+.
\tag{8}
\]
The formula in (7) is first order over a real closed field.  Apply lem:real-closed-field-quantifier-elimination to eliminate \(b_1,b_0,z_L,z_U,y\).  The resulting quantifier-free description of \(\mathcal T\) uses finitely many polynomial sign conditions over \(K_0\).  A one-dimensional polynomial sign pattern is constant on every interval avoiding the finitely many real roots of its nonzero polynomials.  Every finite boundary point of \(\mathcal T\) is therefore a root of one of those polynomials.  By (3) and (8), \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) is such a finite boundary point.  Hence \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) is algebraic over \(K_0\), and thus is a real-algebraic number.

The corresponding cylindrical algebraic decomposition terminates: it begins with finitely many variables and polynomials; every projection stage forms finitely many coefficients, discriminants, and resultants; exact real-algebraic root isolation produces finitely many sign cells; and lifting repeats a finite operation through finitely many coordinates.  The equivalence in lem:real-closed-field-quantifier-elimination certifies the sign decomposition.  Refine an isolating interval for \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) until its exactly checked width is at most the exactly encoded positive number \(\varepsilon\).  This returns rational or real-algebraic numbers \(L_\varepsilon^+\) and \(U_\varepsilon^+\) such that
\[
L_\varepsilon^+\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\le U_\varepsilon^+,
\qquad
U_\varepsilon^+-L_\varepsilon^+\le\varepsilon.
\tag{9}
\]
Strict positivity and exact encoding of \(\varepsilon\) make the refinement test terminate.

\emph{Algebraic incumbent and finite certificate.}
Attainment is unnecessary.  By the defining property of an infimum and \(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\exists [B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+
\quad
w^+(B)<v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon.
\tag{10}
\]
Adjoin the real-algebraic constant \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) to the coefficient field and apply CAD lifting to (10), represented by (1), (4), and (5).  Because the formula is nonempty, CAD returns a sample point with real-algebraic coordinates.  Its normalized representative defines a basis class \([B_\varepsilon]_+\) in the note's quotient \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) of Zhao's published carrier, and
\[
w^+(B_\varepsilon)<v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon,
\qquad\text{hence}\qquad
w^+(B_\varepsilon)\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon.
\tag{11}
\]
Likewise, in the finite quotient charts following (1), the weak sublevel set
\[
\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:w^+(B)\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++\varepsilon\}
\]
is semialgebraic by the same rank-stratified formulas and is nonempty by (10).

Finally, retain the normalized quotient-chart list, the rank condition and pseudoinverse identity from (4) on every CAD cell, the projection polynomials and sign table, the algebraic sample establishing (11), and the root-isolation data establishing (9).  Exact algebraic arithmetic checks each item.  This is a finite sign, sample, and value-isolation certificate covering ranks zero, one, and two.  It supplies an \(\varepsilon\)-incumbent whether or not the infimum is attained and asserts neither outer-set convergence to \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\), a uniform complexity bound, nor a semidefinite-program reduction.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Computable epsilon-value certificate over every rank in this note's constructed projective completion.  GaoKleywegt2017DependenceRobust studies dependence-robust ambiguity sets and dual reformulations; this theorem instead fixes the two marginals, uses their sharp Fréchet covariance endpoints, and certifies a nonconvex control-variate-basis problem by rank-stratified CAD rather than by a DRO dual reduction. \textit{Gap.} It certifies an infimum and epsilon sample even when the equality set is empty.  The fixed-marginal endpoint certificate is distinct from the dependence-ambiguity-set duality studied by GaoKleywegt2017DependenceRobust. \textit{Consumer.} Design preparation can audit a full-class robust value without an SDP or exact-attainment claim.

\begin{lemma}[lem:algebraic-box-feasibility-cad]\label{lem:algebraic-box-feasibility-cad}
For every rational signed pivot-chart box, intersection with the normalized feasible set \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) is an existential semialgebraic question.  More explicitly, sphere feasibility, chart and box restrictions, and \(S_B-n\kappa I_2\succeq0\) are finitely many polynomial equalities and weak inequalities with rational or real-algebraic coefficients; for
\[
 S_B-n\kappa I_2=\begin{bmatrix}u&v\\v&z\end{bmatrix},
\]
the matrix inequality is exactly \(u\ge0\), \(z\ge0\), and \(uz-v^2\ge0\).  Cylindrical algebraic decomposition therefore terminates with either an exact real-algebraic feasible sample or a finite infeasibility certificate, including when feasibility occurs only on the equality boundary.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Fix a rational signed pivot-chart box and write the \(2n\) coordinates of \((b_1,b_0)\) as \(x\).  By \(\operatorname{def{:}kappa\mbox{-}basis\mbox{-}class}\), the normalization conditions are the polynomial equations
\[
\sum_{i=1}^n b_{1i}^2=n,
\qquad
\sum_{i=1}^n b_{0i}^2=n.
\tag{1}
\]
Every box restriction and signed pivot restriction is a polynomial weak inequality with rational coefficients.  Since \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\), each entry of \(S_B\) is a quadratic polynomial in \(x\), with coefficients in the real-algebraic coefficient field supplied by \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\).

Write
\[
S_B-n\kappa I_2=
\begin{bmatrix}u&v\\v&z\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{2}
\]
If the matrix in (2) is positive semidefinite, its principal minors give
\[
u\ge0,
\qquad z\ge0,
\qquad uz-v^2\ge0.
\tag{3}
\]
Conversely, suppose (3) holds.  If \(u>0\), then for every \((s,t)\in\mathbb R^2\),
\[
us^2+2vst+zt^2
=u\left(s+\frac vu t\right)^2
+\left(z-\frac{v^2}{u}\right)t^2\ge0.
\tag{4}
\]
If \(u=0\), the determinant inequality in (3) forces \(v=0\), and the quadratic form is \(zt^2\ge0\).  Thus (3) is necessary and sufficient for the matrix inequality in (2).  In particular, the use of weak inequalities retains the equality boundary.

Intersection of the chart box with the normalized feasible set is therefore represented by one existential first-order formula
\[
\exists x\;\Phi(x),
\tag{5}
\]
where \(\Phi\) is a finite conjunction of the polynomial equalities (1), the chart and box inequalities, and the three weak polynomial inequalities (3).  By \(\operatorname{lem{:}real\mbox{-}closed\mbox{-}field\mbox{-}quantifier\mbox{-}elimination}\), cylindrical algebraic decomposition returns a quantifier-free formula equivalent to (5), and exact real-algebraic sign isolation decides that formula.

It remains to verify the asserted form of the output.  During cylindrical lifting, each projection polynomial specializes to a univariate polynomial with real-algebraic coefficients.  Exact root isolation partitions the line into finitely many open sectors and root sections.  Choosing a rational point in a sector, or the isolated real-algebraic root in a section, and proceeding recursively through the finitely many coordinates produces a real-algebraic sample in every sign-invariant cell.  If one such cell satisfies \(\Phi\), its sample is an exactly feasible point.  If none does, the finite sign-invariant cell list together with the elimination trace certifies infeasibility.  Root sections are retained because every constraint is tested with its stated weak inequality, so the conclusion also holds when feasibility occurs only on a sphere boundary or on \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}S_B)=\kappa\). \(\square\)
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[prop:kappa-value-exhaustion]\label{prop:kappa-value-exhaustion}
Suppose ass:exposure-positivity holds and \(\mathcal B_Z\ne\varnothing\).  If \(0<\kappa_1\le\kappa_2\) and both regular classes are nonempty, then
\[
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_1}}w(B)
\le
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_2}}w(B).
\]
Moreover,
\[
\inf_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing}}
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)
=
\inf_{[B]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)
=
\inf_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}w(q_Z(B)).
\]
If the middle infimum is attained by some \(B_Z^\star\in\mathcal B_Z\), then there is \(\kappa_0>0\) such that, for every \(0<\kappa\le\kappa_0\),
\[
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=\inf_{[B]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B).
\]
Thus the fixed-threshold values exhaust the entire maximal well-defined ordinary-inverse matrix domain in value through the quotient; singular or Moore--Penrose bases remain outside this conclusion.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Exposure positivity makes every diagonal entry of \(\Pi\) strictly positive, so the inverse-probability matrices occurring in \(w\) are finite.  For every nonempty \(\mathcal B_\kappa\), the compactness proposition makes \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) compact.  On its normalized representatives,
\[
 S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\succeq n\kappa I_2.
\]
Consequently, \(B\mapsto S_B^{-1}\) is continuous there.  The trace formulas defining \(g_B(c_e)\) and \(\ell_B(c_e)\), for \(e\in\{L,U\}\), are therefore continuous in \(B\), and so is their finite maximum \(w(B)\).  These functions are invariant under the residual independent sign changes, hence descend continuously to the quotient.  Thus every displayed minimum over a nonempty \(\mathcal B_\kappa\) is attained.

If \(0<\kappa_1\le\kappa_2\), the eigenvalue constraints give
\[
 \mathcal B_{\kappa_2}\subseteq\mathcal B_{\kappa_1}.
\]
Minimizing the same function over these nested nonempty sets yields
\[
 \min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_1}}w(B)
 \le
 \min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_2}}w(B).
\]

The exhaustion proposition gives the set identity
\[
 \mathcal B_Z=\bigcup_{\kappa>0}\mathcal B_\kappa,
\]
where empty threshold classes do not affect the union.  Therefore the elementary identity for an infimum over a union gives
\[
 \inf_{[B]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)
 =
 \inf_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing}}
 \inf_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)
 =
 \inf_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing}}
 \min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B).
\tag{1}
\]
The quotient-transfer theorem states that \(q_Z\) is surjective and that \(w\circ q_Z\) is constant on every fiber.  Hence
\[
 \inf_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}w(q_Z(B))
 =\inf_{[B]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B),
\tag{2}
\]
which proves all three value equalities.

Finally, suppose \(B_Z^\star\) attains the middle infimum.  By exhaustion there is \(\kappa_0>0\) such that \(B_Z^\star\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_0}\).  For every \(0<\kappa\le\kappa_0\), nesting gives \(B_Z^\star\in\mathcal B_\kappa\), while \(\mathcal B_\kappa\subseteq\mathcal B_Z\).  If \(V_Z=\inf_{[B]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)\), these two inclusions imply
\[
 V_Z
 \le \min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)
 \le w(B_Z^\star)
 =V_Z.
\]
Thus equality holds for every such \(\kappa\).  All arguments take place on the positive-definite rank-two ordinary-inverse restriction and its exact rescaling quotient; no singular pseudoinverse class is included. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The fixed-threshold minima exhaust the positive-definite rank-two projective restriction in value. \textit{Gap.} Its full unquotiented pullback is still only the rank-two restriction, not the note's constructed all-rank projective completion. \textit{Consumer.} The separate full-class value-transfer theorem supplies equality with the rank-stratified infimum.

\begin{theorem}[thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate]\label{thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate}
There is a positive, fully explicit triangle witness.  Let \(n=3\), let
\[
G=\begin{bmatrix}0&1&1\\1&0&1\\1&1&0\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\nu(w)=\left(\frac13\right)^{\sum_iw_i}
       \left(\frac23\right)^{3-\sum_iw_i},
\]
and, writing \(\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}\), define
\[
f_i(w)=
\begin{cases}
1,&w_i=1\text{ and }w_j=w_k=0,\\
0,&w_i=0\text{ and }w_j+w_k\ge1,\\
\star,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
Take \(F_1=F_0=\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2)\) and \(\kappa=1/8\).  With \(J_3=\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^{\top}\) and \(A=(I_3+J_3)/4\), the exact design and endpoint inputs are
\[
\Pi=\operatorname{diag}\!\left(\frac4{27}I_3,\frac{10}{27}I_3\right),
\qquad
\Omega=\frac1{729}
\begin{bmatrix}
108I_3-16J_3&68J_3-108I_3\\
68J_3-108I_3&162I_3+8J_3
\end{bmatrix},
\]
\[
c_L=-\frac14,
\qquad c_U=\frac14,
\qquad
Q(c_L)=
\begin{bmatrix}A&(I_3-J_3)/4\\(I_3-J_3)/4&A\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
Q(c_U)=
\begin{bmatrix}A&-A\\-A&A\end{bmatrix}.
\]
For normalized representatives \(B(x,y)=\begin{bmatrix}x&0\\0&y\end{bmatrix}\), the full optimizer correspondences are
\[
\mathcal A_0=
\left\{[B(x,y)]:
\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y\rVert_2^2=3,\ 
\mathbf 1^{\top}x=\mathbf 1^{\top}y=x^{\top}y=0
\right\},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=
\left\{[B(x,y)]:
\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y\rVert_2^2=3,\ 
\mathbf 1^{\top}x=x^{\top}y=0,\ 
(\mathbf 1^{\top}y)^2=\frac{216}{55}
\right\}.
\]
They are nonempty, and their exact objective values satisfy
\[
\max_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}g_B(0)=\frac{31}{80},
\qquad
w(B_0)=\frac{521}{1200}\quad(B_0\in\mathcal A_0),
\qquad
w(B_{\mathrm{mm}})=v_{\mathrm{mm}}=\frac{2613}{8432}
\quad(B_{\mathrm{mm}}\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}).
\]
Moreover every member of \(\mathcal A_0\cup\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) obeys
\[
\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_B)=\frac4{27}.
\]
Consequently \(\xi_0=(\nu,F_1,F_0)\) belongs to \(\mathfrak X_\kappa\), and the rational choices
\[
\delta=\frac{4912}{39525},
\qquad
\eta=\frac5{216}
\]
give the full-correspondence certificates
\[
\inf_{B_0\in\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)}w_{\xi_0}(B_0)
-\sup_{B_{\mathrm{mm}}\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)}w_{\xi_0}(B_{\mathrm{mm}})
=\delta>0
\]
and
\[
\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)\cup\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)}
\left\{\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_B)-\kappa\right\}
=\eta>0.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
For a fixed vertex \(i\), the event \(D_{i,1}=1\) is the assignment in which \(i\) alone is treated, whereas \(D_{i,0}=1\) means that \(i\) is untreated and at least one of the other two vertices is treated.  Enumeration of the eight assignments therefore gives
\[
\pi_{i,1}=\frac4{27},
\qquad
\pi_{i,0}=\frac{10}{27},
\]
and
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc}
&i=j&i\ne j\\ \hline
\operatorname{Cov}(D_{i,1},D_{j,1})&92/729&-16/729\\
\operatorname{Cov}(D_{i,1},D_{j,0})&-40/729&68/729\\
\operatorname{Cov}(D_{i,0},D_{j,0})&170/729&8/729.
\end{array}
\tag{1}
\]
This proves the displayed \(\Pi\) and \(\Omega\), as well as exposure positivity and mutual exclusivity.  The Bernoulli \((1/2)\) quantile is zero on \((0,1/2]\) and one on \((1/2,1]\), so the countermonotone and comonotone products give \(c_L=-1/4\) and \(c_U=1/4\).  Substitution of \(\mu_a=1/2\) and \(\sigma_a^2=1/4\) in def:moment-matrix gives the two displayed endpoint matrices.

For a normalized representative \(B(x,y)\), put
\[
s=\mathbf1^{\top}x,
\qquad t=\mathbf1^{\top}y,
\qquad q=x^{\top}y.
\]
Direct block multiplication using (1) yields
\[
S_B=
\begin{bmatrix}
4/9-16s^2/729&-4q/27+68st/729\\
-4q/27+68st/729&2/3+8t^2/729
\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{2}
\]
With
\[
d=729+12t^2-36s^2-54q^2+68qst-22s^2t^2,
\tag{3}
\]
one has \(\det S_B=8d/19683\), so \(d>0\) on \(\mathcal B_{1/8}\).  Exact substitution in def:gain-loss gives
\[
\frac{31}{80}-g_B(0)=\frac{N_0}{1200d},
\tag{4}
\]
where
\[
N_0=5022q^2-4352qst+670s^2t^2+1629s^2+2148t^2.
\tag{5}
\]
The following completion makes its sign and equality set explicit:
\[
N_0
=5022\left(q-\frac{1088}{2511}st\right)^2
+1629s^2+2148t^2-\frac{685118}{2511}s^2t^2.
\tag{6}
\]
The last three terms are bilinear in \((s^2,t^2)\in[0,9]^2\).  Their values at the four vertices are
\[
0,\qquad14661,\qquad19332,\qquad\frac{368665}{31},
\]
respectively.  Bilinear interpolation therefore proves nonnegativity, with equality only at \(s=t=0\).  Equation (6) then requires \(q=0\).  Thus (4) is an equality exactly when \(s=t=q=0\).  Substitution at this equality set gives
\[
g_B(0)=\frac{31}{80},
\qquad
w(B)=\frac{521}{1200}.
\tag{7}
\]

For the minimax loss, let \(\lambda=1407/2635\in(0,1)\) and \(v=2613/8432\).  Since a maximum dominates every convex combination,
\[
\begin{aligned}
w(B)-v
&\ge \lambda\ell_B(c_L)+(1-\lambda)\ell_B(c_U)-v\\
&=\frac{3N_{\mathrm{mm}}}{210800d},
\end{aligned}
\tag{8}
\]
where exact reduction gives
\[
N_{\mathrm{mm}}
=274734\left(q-\frac{70411}{137367}st\right)^2
+s^2\left(34527-\frac{439017980}{137367}t^2\right).
\tag{9}
\]
Because \(t^2\le9\), the second coefficient is bounded below by
\[
34527-9\frac{439017980}{137367}
=\frac{87967621}{15263}>0.
\tag{10}
\]
Hence equality in (9) requires \(s=q=0\).  Since \(\lambda\in(0,1)\), equality in the first line of (8) additionally requires \(\ell_B(c_L)=\ell_B(c_U)\).  Under \(s=q=0\), exact substitution gives
\[
\ell_B(c_L)-\ell_B(c_U)
=-\frac{3(55t^2-216)}{10(4t^2+243)},
\tag{11}
\]
so equality holds exactly when \(t^2=216/55\).  This proves the displayed minimizer equality set and \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}=2613/8432\).

Both equality sets are nonempty.  For the nominal set choose an orthogonal pair of norm \(\sqrt3\) in \(\mathbf1^\perp\).  For the minimax set choose a norm-\(\sqrt3\) vector \(y\) with \((\mathbf1^{\top}y)^2=216/55<9\), and then choose a norm-\(\sqrt3\) vector \(x\) perpendicular to both \(\mathbf1\) and \(y\).  On either equality set, (2) is diagonal and
\[
\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_B)=\frac4{27}.
\tag{12}
\]
Thus the equality sets lie in \(\mathcal B_{1/8}\), proving \(\kappa\)-feasibility and membership of \(\xi_0\) in \(\mathfrak X_\kappa\).  Finally,
\[
\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{4912}{39525},
\qquad
\frac4{27}-\frac18=\frac5{216},
\tag{13}
\]
which are the claimed full-correspondence gap and regularity slack.  Assumption ass:known-exposure supplies the causal interpretation of the two exposure labels.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The exact triangle witness certifies every equality case on both optimizer correspondences, the positive loss gap, and optimizer-wide regularity slack. \textit{Gap.} Zhao2026OptimalControlVariates supplies the zero-covariance surrogate but not this nondegenerate full-equality-set strict-separation certificate. \textit{Consumer.} The completion-relative-open theorem turns its separation and slack into a lawful neighborhood result.

\begin{lemma}[lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity]\label{lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity}
Let \(\xi_0\in\mathfrak X_\kappa\) and suppose that, for some \(\eta>0\),
\[
\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)\cup\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)}
\left\{\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}S_B)-\kappa\right\}\ge\eta.
\]
For every sequence \((\xi_j)_{j\ge1}\subset\mathfrak X_\kappa\) converging to \(\xi_0\) in the relative topology of def:lawful-instance-class,
\[
\liminf_{j\to\infty}\Delta(\xi_j)\ge\Delta(\xi_0).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Let \(\xi_j=(\nu_j,F_{1j},F_{0j})\to\xi_0\).  Total-variation convergence on the finite assignment space gives
\[
\Pi_j\to\Pi_0,
\qquad
\Omega_j\to\Omega_0.
\tag{1}
\]
Exposure positivity at \(\xi_0\) makes \(\Pi_j^{-1}\to\Pi_0^{-1}\) after discarding finitely many indices.  Wasserstein convergence on \([0,1]\) gives convergence of the first and second marginal moments.  Applying lem:restricted-marginal-extrema-continuity to the continuous product map \((y_1,y_0)\mapsto y_1y_0\) gives
\[
c_{Lj}\to c_{L0},
\qquad
c_{Uj}\to c_{U0}.
\tag{2}
\]
Equations (1)--(2) and def:moment-matrix imply convergence of both endpoint matrices.

Work with the normalized representatives on the product of the two radius-\(\sqrt n\) spheres, modulo the finite residual sign action.  This ambient quotient is compact.  The function
\[
\rho(\xi,B)=\lambda_{\min}\{n^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega(\xi)B\}
\tag{3}
\]
is jointly continuous, and \(\mathcal B_\kappa(\xi)=\{B:\rho(\xi,B)\ge\kappa\}\).  On this closed region, \(S_B^{-1}\) has norm at most \((n\kappa)^{-1}\).  Hence (1)--(2), def:gain-loss, and the maximum over the two endpoints show that
\[
(\xi,B)\longmapsto g_{B,\xi}(0)
\quad\text{and}\quad
(\xi,B)\longmapsto w_\xi(B)
\tag{4}
\]
are jointly continuous wherever \(B\in\mathcal B_\kappa(\xi)\).

Put \(v_j=\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa(\xi_j)}w_{\xi_j}(B)\).  Choose \(B_*\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)\).  The assumed slack gives \(\rho(\xi_0,B_*)\ge\kappa+\eta\); by (3), \(B_*\) is feasible at \(\xi_j\) for all large \(j\).  Therefore
\[
\limsup_{j\to\infty}v_j
\le\lim_{j\to\infty}w_{\xi_j}(B_*)
=v_0.
\tag{5}
\]

Next define
\[
q_j=\min_{B\in\mathcal A_0(\xi_j)}w_{\xi_j}(B).
\]
Compactness makes the minimum well defined.  For a subsequence realizing \(\liminf_jq_j\), choose \(B_j\in\mathcal A_0(\xi_j)\) with \(w_{\xi_j}(B_j)=q_j\).  Compactness gives a further subsequence, not relabeled, with \(B_j\to\bar B\).  Closedness of the feasibility inequalities gives \(\bar B\in\mathcal B_\kappa(\xi_0)\).  Choose any \(C_*\in\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)\).  Its assumed slack makes it feasible at \(\xi_j\) eventually.  Since \(B_j\) maximizes the nominal gain,
\[
g_{B_j,\xi_j}(0)\ge g_{C_*,\xi_j}(0).
\]
Passing to the limit by (4) gives
\[
g_{\bar B,\xi_0}(0)\ge g_{C_*,\xi_0}(0)
=\max_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa(\xi_0)}g_{B,\xi_0}(0),
\]
so \(\bar B\in\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)\).  A final use of (4) yields
\[
\liminf_{j\to\infty}q_j
=w_{\xi_0}(\bar B)
\ge q_0.
\tag{6}
\]
Since def:dominance-gap gives \(\Delta(\xi_j)=q_j-v_j\), (5)--(6) imply
\[
\liminf_{j\to\infty}\Delta(\xi_j)
\ge\liminf_jq_j-\limsup_jv_j
\ge q_0-v_0
=\Delta(\xi_0).
\]
This is the claimed lower semicontinuity.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The lemma propagates marginal and design continuity through the moving \(\kappa\)-feasible set and both optimizer correspondences. \textit{Gap.} Fixed-marginal coupling continuity alone does not control minimax and surrogate optimizers when feasibility also moves with the assignment law. \textit{Consumer.} The relative-open triangle theorems use it to retain a quantitative fraction of the exact separation margin.

\begin{theorem}[thm:triangle-relative-open-dominance]\label{thm:triangle-relative-open-dominance}
For the explicit triangle instance \(\xi_0\) and \(\kappa=1/8\) in thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate, there is a relative-open neighborhood \(\mathcal U\subset\mathfrak X_\kappa\) containing \(\xi_0\) such that
\[
\Delta(\xi)>\frac{\delta}{2}=\frac{2456}{39525}
\qquad(\xi\in\mathcal U),
\]
where \(\delta=4912/39525\).  Consequently, for every \(\xi\in\mathcal U\), every \(B_{\mathrm{mm}}\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)\), and every \(B_0\in\mathcal A_0(\xi)\),
\[
w_\xi(B_{\mathrm{mm}})<w_\xi(B_0).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate gives
\[
\Delta(\xi_0)=\delta=\frac{4912}{39525}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi_0)\cup\mathcal A_0(\xi_0)}
\{\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_B)-\kappa\}=\frac5{216}>0.
\tag{1}
\]
Lemma lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity therefore implies that for every sequence \(\xi_j\to\xi_0\),
\[
\liminf_{j\to\infty}\Delta(\xi_j)\ge\delta.
\tag{2}
\]
If no relative-open neighborhood satisfied \(\Delta(\xi)>\delta/2\), metrizability of \(\mathfrak X_\kappa\) would provide a sequence \(\xi_j\to\xi_0\) with \(\Delta(\xi_j)\le\delta/2\), contradicting (2).  Thus such a neighborhood \(\mathcal U\) exists.  For \(B_{\mathrm{mm}}\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)\) and \(B_0\in\mathcal A_0(\xi)\), def:dominance-gap and constancy of \(w_\xi\) on the minimizer set give
\[
w_\xi(B_0)-w_\xi(B_{\mathrm{mm}})
\ge\Delta(\xi)>\frac\delta2>0.
\]
This proves the asserted strict ordering.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The exact triangle certificate is strengthened to a neighborhood guarantee retaining more than half its rational margin. \textit{Gap.} A single strict example would not by itself show that the improvement is robust to perturbing the design and pilot marginals. \textit{Consumer.} Nearby lawful instances inherit a strict comparison between every minimax optimizer and every zero-covariance optimizer.

\begin{theorem}[thm:certified-global-basis-input-obstruction]\label{thm:certified-global-basis-input-obstruction}
Endpoint covariances alone do not determine the minimax worst-copula residual value.  In particular, let \(n=3\), let \(W_1,W_2,W_3\) be independent Bernoulli \(1/2\) variables under \(\nu\), and set \(f_i(w)=w_i\).  With \(\kappa=1/8\) and \(\varepsilon=1/100\), the marginal pairs \((F_1,F_0)=(\delta_0,\delta_0)\) and \((F_1,F_0)=(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2),\delta_0)\) produce the same moment-incomplete tuple
\[
\Pi=\tfrac12 I_6,\qquad
\Omega=\tfrac14\begin{bmatrix}I_3&-I_3\\-I_3&I_3\end{bmatrix},
\qquad c_L=c_U=0,
\]
but their endpoint moment matrices differ and their minimax residual values are, respectively,
\[
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=0
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=\frac1{36}.
\]
Consequently, no algorithm whose only input is \((\Pi,\Omega,c_L,c_U,\kappa,\varepsilon)\) can always return rational bounds
\[
L_{\varepsilon}\le \min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)\le U_{\varepsilon},
\qquad U_{\varepsilon}-L_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon.
\]
Thus endpoint moment matrices such as \(Q(c_L)\) and \(Q(c_U)\), or equivalent marginal moments, are necessary inputs for the proposed certification problem.  This obstruction makes no claim that the corrected moment-complete branch-and-bound solver terminates at every positive tolerance.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Write \(J_3=\mathbf 1\mathbf 1^{\top}\) and
\[
K=\begin{bmatrix}I_3&-I_3\\-I_3&I_3\end{bmatrix}.
\]
We first verify the design, support, and rank conditions.  Since \(f_i(W)=W_i\),
\[
D_{i,1}=W_i,
\qquad
D_{i,0}=1-W_i.
\]
Thus the two exposure events are complementary, hence mutually exclusive, for every unit and every assignment.  The three independent Bernoulli coordinates give probability \(2^{-3}=1/8\) to each assignment in \(\{0,1\}^3\).  Entrywise covariance calculation, using \(\mathbb E_\nu W_i=1/2\), \(\operatorname{Var}_\nu(W_i)=1/4\), and independence across distinct units, yields
\[
\Pi=\tfrac12 I_6,
\qquad
\Omega=\operatorname{Var}_\nu(D)=\tfrac14K.
\tag{1}
\]
In particular, all six exposure probabilities equal \(1/2>0\), so \(\Pi^{-1}=2I_6\) exists.

The normalized regular class is nonempty, including away from its regularity boundary.  Let
\[
x=\begin{bmatrix}1&1&1\end{bmatrix}^{\top},
\qquad
y=\sqrt{\tfrac32}\begin{bmatrix}1&-1&0\end{bmatrix}^{\top},
\qquad
B_\circ=\begin{bmatrix}x&0\\0&y\end{bmatrix}.
\]
Then \(\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y\rVert_2^2=3\) and \(x^{\top}y=0\).  By (1),
\[
S_{B_\circ}=B_\circ^{\top}\Omega B_\circ
=\tfrac14\begin{bmatrix}3&-x^{\top}y\\-y^{\top}x&3\end{bmatrix}
=\tfrac34 I_2,
\]
and hence
\[
\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_{B_\circ})=\tfrac14>\tfrac18=\kappa.
\tag{2}
\]
For every \(B\in\mathcal B_\kappa\), including every point on the boundary where the defining inequality is an equality,
\[
\lambda_{\min}(S_B)\ge 3\kappa=\tfrac38>0.
\tag{3}
\]
Thus every inverse used by the fixed-\(\kappa\) criterion is defined; no boundary point is discarded.

For the first marginal pair, \((F_1,F_0)=(\delta_0,\delta_0)\), both laws have support in \([0,1]\), all four marginal means and variances are zero, and both quantile-product integrals are zero.  Therefore \(c_L=c_U=0\) and \(Q(0)=0\).  The definitions of the uncontrolled variance, gain, and loss give
\[
h(0)=g_B(0)=\ell_B(0)=0
\qquad\text{for every }B\in\mathcal B_\kappa.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=0.
\tag{4}
\]

For the second marginal pair, \((F_1,F_0)=(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2),\delta_0)\), both laws again have support in \([0,1]\).  Since \(F_0^{-1}(u)=0\) for Lebesgue-almost every \(u\in(0,1)\), both endpoint quantile products vanish; since \(\mu_0=0\),
\[
c_L=c_U=0.
\tag{5}
\]
The marginal moments are
\[
\mu_1=\tfrac12,
\qquad
\sigma_1^2=\tfrac14,
\qquad
\mu_0=\sigma_0^2=0.
\]
The endpoint moment matrix is therefore
\[
Q(0)=\begin{bmatrix}A&0\\0&0\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
A=\tfrac14(I_3+J_3).
\tag{6}
\]
Equations (1) and (5) show that the two instances have the same moment-incomplete input \((\Pi,\Omega,c_L,c_U,\kappa,\varepsilon)\), while (6) differs from the zero endpoint matrix in the first instance.

It remains to optimize the second loss exactly.  Fix \(B\in\mathcal B_\kappa\), write its normalized arm blocks as \(x_B=b_1\) and \(y_B=b_0\), and define
\[
C_B=\begin{bmatrix}x_B&-y_B\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb R^{3\times2}.
\]
Direct block multiplication using (1) gives
\[
S_B=\tfrac14C_B^{\top}C_B.
\tag{7}
\]
By (3), \(C_B\) has rank two.  A second direct multiplication, now using (1) and (6), gives
\[
B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Q(0)\Pi^{-1}\Omega B
=\tfrac14C_B^{\top}AC_B.
\tag{8}
\]
Let
\[
P_B=C_B(C_B^{\top}C_B)^{-1}C_B^{\top}.
\]
This is the rank-two orthogonal projector onto \(\operatorname{col}(C_B)\).  Substituting (7)--(8) into the gain formula and using cyclicity of trace yields
\[
g_B(0)
=\tfrac19\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(C_B^{\top}C_B)^{-1}C_B^{\top}AC_B\right]
=\tfrac19\operatorname{tr}(P_BA).
\tag{9}
\]
The uncontrolled term is, again by (1) and (6),
\[
h(0)
=\tfrac19\operatorname{tr}\!\left[\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Q(0)\right]
=\tfrac19\operatorname{tr}(A)
=\tfrac16.
\tag{10}
\]

Put \(u=3^{-1/2}\mathbf1\).  Since \(J_3=3uu^{\top}\),
\[
A=\tfrac14I_3+\tfrac34uu^{\top}.
\]
For every rank-two orthogonal projector \(P_B\),
\[
\operatorname{tr}(P_BA)
=\tfrac14\operatorname{tr}(P_B)+\tfrac34u^{\top}P_Bu
=\tfrac12+\tfrac34\lVert P_Bu\rVert_2^2
\le\tfrac54.
\tag{11}
\]
Combining (9)--(11) proves the global lower bound
\[
\ell_B(0)=h(0)-g_B(0)
\ge\tfrac16-\tfrac19\cdot\tfrac54
=\tfrac1{36}.
\tag{12}
\]
The feasible basis \(B_\circ\) from (2) attains equality: its first arm column is \(x=\sqrt3u\), so \(u\in\operatorname{col}(C_{B_\circ})\) and \(P_{B_\circ}u=u\).  Since (5) makes the covariance interval a singleton, \(w(B)=\ell_B(0)\).  Hence (12) is sharp and
\[
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=\tfrac1{36}.
\tag{13}
\]

Finally, an algorithm restricted to \((\Pi,\Omega,c_L,c_U,\kappa,\varepsilon)\) receives identical input on the two instances.  Any interval that is always valid for that input must therefore contain both values (4) and (13).  Its width must satisfy
\[
U_\varepsilon-L_\varepsilon\ge\tfrac1{36}>\tfrac1{100}=\varepsilon,
\]
contradicting the demanded tolerance.  Thus the endpoint moment matrices, or equivalent marginal moments, are necessary inputs.  This is an input-indistinguishability obstruction only and proves no termination claim for a moment-complete solver.

The causal estimand remains the fixed finite-population contrast \(\tau\).  The optimized quantity \(w(B)\) is an ex ante basis-design criterion and is not an identifying definition of \(\tau\).  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The exact two-law witness proves that \(\Pi,\Omega,c_L,c_U,\kappa,\varepsilon\) alone cannot determine the minimax residual value: the same displayed tuple yields values zero and \(1/36\). \textit{Gap.} This theorem establishes necessity of endpoint moment information.  Sufficiency and finite instance-dependent termination are supplied separately by the corrected moment-complete solver theorem. \textit{Consumer.} It justifies the moment-complete certified input interface and prevents a certificate from silently omitting the marginal second moments.

\begin{theorem}[thm:algebraic-finite-epsilon-solver]\label{thm:algebraic-finite-epsilon-solver}
For every rational or real-algebraic input \(\mathfrak I_{\mathrm{cert}}\) satisfying ass:exposure-positivity and ass:kappa-feasibility, and every exactly encoded \(\varepsilon>0\), the algebraic projective interval branch-and-bound procedure terminates after finitely many subdivisions and returns a locally checkable certificate \(\mathscr K_{\varepsilon}\) such that
\[
 L_{\varepsilon}\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}\le w(B_{\varepsilon})\le U_{\varepsilon},
 \qquad U_{\varepsilon}-L_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon,
 \qquad h_{\varepsilon}\le\varepsilon,
 \qquad \mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\subseteq\mathcal C_{\varepsilon}.
\]
The incumbent \(B_{\varepsilon}\) has finite real-algebraic coordinates, including when every feasible point lies on \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega B)=\kappa\).  The checker verifies only CAD sign certificates, algebraic feasibility, outward objective bounds, rational norm and diameter records, chart cover, pruning inequalities, and mesh; the displayed facts about \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}\) and \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) are mathematical consequences.  Along every \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\), the certified value gaps and meshes converge to zero.  No uniform complexity bound or semidefinite-program reduction is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Use normalized representatives
\[
K_\kappa=
\left\{(b_1,b_0):
\lVert b_1\rVert_2^2=\lVert b_0\rVert_2^2=n,
\quad S_B-n\kappa I_2\succeq0
\right\}.
\tag{1}
\]
Assumption \(\operatorname{ass{:}kappa\mbox{-}feasibility}\) makes \(K_\kappa\) nonempty.  It is closed in the product of two radius-\(\sqrt n\) spheres and hence compact.  Proposition \(\operatorname{prop{:}kappa\mbox{-}exhaustion}\) identifies its projective quotient with \(\mathcal B_\kappa\).  Moreover, (1) gives
\[
S_B\succeq n\kappa I_2,
\qquad
\lVert S_B^{-1}\rVert_2\le (n\kappa)^{-1}
\quad(B\in K_\kappa).
\tag{2}
\]
Thus every inverse used by \(w\) exists, including on the \(\kappa\)-equality boundary.  Assumption \(\operatorname{ass{:}exposure\mbox{-}positivity}\) makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) well defined.

For \(e\in\{L,U\}\), put
\[
R_e=\Omega\Pi^{-1}Q(c_e)\Pi^{-1}\Omega,
\qquad
S(B)=B^{\top}\Omega B,
\qquad
T_e(B)=B^{\top}R_eB.
\tag{3}
\]
The exact algebraic input permits computation of rational bounds \(M_\Omega\ge\lVert\Omega\rVert_2\) and \(M_e\ge\lVert R_e\rVert_2\).  If \(B,C\in K_\kappa\) and \(d=\lVert B-C\rVert_F\), then \(\lVert B\rVert_2,\lVert C\rVert_2\le\sqrt{2n}\), whence
\[
\lVert S(B)-S(C)\rVert_2
\le 2\sqrt{2n}\,M_\Omega d,
\qquad
\lVert T_e(B)-T_e(C)\rVert_2
\le 2\sqrt{2n}\,M_e d.
\tag{4}
\]
The resolvent identity gives
\[
S(B)^{-1}-S(C)^{-1}
=S(B)^{-1}\{S(C)-S(B)\}S(C)^{-1}.
\tag{5}
\]
Combining (2)--(5), \(\lVert T_e(B)\rVert_2\le2nM_e\), and \(|\operatorname{tr}(UV)|\le2\lVert U\rVert_2\lVert V\rVert_2\) for two-by-two matrices yields
\[
\left|
n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\{S(B)^{-1}T_e(B)\}
-n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\{S(C)^{-1}T_e(C)\}
\right|
\le L_e d,
\tag{6}
\]
where, for example, the following effective rational upper bound is valid:
\[
L_e=
n^{-2}\left\{
\frac{8\sqrt{2n}\,M_\Omega M_e}{n\kappa^2}
+\frac{4\sqrt{2n}\,M_e}{n\kappa}
\right\}.
\tag{7}
\]
Increasing algebraic square-root bounds outward if necessary makes (7) rational.  The terms \(h(c_e)\) do not depend on \(B\), and the maximum of finitely many functions with common Lipschitz bound has that bound.  Hence an effectively computable positive rational \(\overline L\) satisfies
\[
|w(B)-w(C)|\le\overline L\lVert B-C\rVert_F
\quad(B,C\in K_\kappa).
\tag{8}
\]

Every normalized arm vector has a coordinate of absolute value at least \(1\), so the finitely many signed pivot charts with threshold \(n^{-1/2}\) cover the quotient.  Fix a rational \(\rho>0\) such that \(4\rho<\varepsilon\).  Uniformly bisect this finite chart cover until every resulting box \(C\) has diameter \(d_C\) satisfying
\[
d_C\le\varepsilon,
\qquad
\overline Ld_C\le\rho.
\tag{9}
\]
Only finitely many bisections are required.  By \(\operatorname{lem{:}algebraic\mbox{-}box\mbox{-}feasibility\mbox{-}cad}\), every box is then either deleted with a finite exact infeasibility certificate or supplied with a feasible point \(B_C\) having finite real-algebraic coordinates.  Exact algebraic evaluation and root isolation return rational numbers \(\underline q_C,\overline q_C\) satisfying
\[
w(B_C)-\rho\le\underline q_C\le w(B_C)
\le\overline q_C\le w(B_C)+\rho.
\tag{10}
\]
For every feasible box define
\[
\lambda_C=\underline q_C-\overline Ld_C.
\tag{11}
\]
For \(B\in C\cap K_\kappa\), equations (8), (10), and (11) imply
\[
\lambda_C
\le w(B_C)-\overline Ld_C
\le w(B).
\tag{12}
\]
Thus \(\lambda_C\) is a certified rational lower bound on the objective throughout the feasible part of \(C\).

Set
\[
L_\varepsilon=\min_C\lambda_C,
\qquad
U_\varepsilon=\min_C\overline q_C,
\tag{13}
\]
and let \(B_\varepsilon=B_C\) for a box attaining the second minimum.  The minima in (13) are over a nonempty finite family.  Compactness and continuity give a minimizer \(B^\star\in K_\kappa\) with \(w(B^\star)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}\).  Equations (9)--(12) give, for every feasible box, \(\lambda_C\ge v_{\mathrm{mm}}-2\rho\); applying (8) and (10) in a box containing \(B^\star\) gives \(\overline q_C\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}+2\rho\).  Consequently,
\[
v_{\mathrm{mm}}-2\rho
\le L_\varepsilon
\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}
\le w(B_\varepsilon)
\le U_\varepsilon
\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}+2\rho.
\tag{14}
\]
In particular, \(U_\varepsilon-L_\varepsilon\le4\rho<\varepsilon\).

Prune a feasible box only when its certified lower bound \(\lambda_C\) is strictly larger than \(U_\varepsilon\).  By (12)--(14), no box containing a global minimizer can be pruned.  Therefore the union \(\mathcal C_\varepsilon\) of the unpruned quotient-chart boxes satisfies
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\subseteq\mathcal C_\varepsilon.
\tag{15}
\]
Equation (9) gives \(h_\varepsilon\le\varepsilon\).  The incumbent is one of the CAD samples, so it is exactly feasible and has finite real-algebraic coordinates even if all feasible points satisfy the regularity constraint with equality.

The finite record consists precisely of the chart cover, CAD sign and feasibility certificates, the algebraic incumbent, outward rational objective bounds, rational norm and diameter records, pruning comparisons, and the mesh.  A local checker verifies those records; (12)--(15) then give the asserted statements about the unknown value and minimizer set.  For any sequence \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\), the certified gap and mesh are bounded above by \(\varepsilon\), and therefore both converge to zero.  The argument supplies neither a uniform subdivision-complexity bound nor a semidefinite-program reduction. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Locally checkable compact fixed-kappa rank-two branch-and-bound certificate. \textit{Gap.} The separate full-class theorem uses rank-stratified CAD and does not return an exact outer-argmin set. \textit{Consumer.} Regular deployable basis optimization.

\begin{theorem}[thm:ordinary-inverse-strict-improvement-characterization]\label{thm:ordinary-inverse-strict-improvement-characterization}
For every \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\), the ordinary-inverse loss has the exact intercept--slope form
\[
w(B)=h(0)-a_B+p_\xi(b_B).
\]
Suppose the ordinary-domain zero-covariance and minimax extrema are attained.  Then
\[
\Delta_Z=
\max_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}\{a_B-p_\xi(b_B)\}
-\left[a^\star-\inf_{B:\,a_B=a^\star}p_\xi(b_B)\right].
\]
Consequently strict ordinary-domain improvement holds if and only if \(\Delta_Z>0\), equivalently if and only if there is \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\) for which
\[
\inf_{C:\,a_C=a^\star}p_\xi(b_C)-p_\xi(b_B)
>a^\star-a_B.
\]
Thus some slope-penalty advantage must strictly exceed the sacrificed zero-covariance gain.  Through \(q_Z\), all displayed values are unchanged on \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\), and every attained optimizer correspondence there is exactly the inverse image of its projective correspondence.  No universal strictness, minimax interchange, singular completion, or semidefinite reduction is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
From \(Q(c)=Q(0)-cE\), def:ordinary-intercept-slope-image, and cyclicity of trace,
\[
g_B(c)=a_B+b_Bc.
\tag{1}
\]
Lemma lem:ht-affine-drift gives \(h(c)=h(0)+2c/n\), and hence
\[
\ell_B(c)=h(0)-a_B+(2/n-b_B)c.
\tag{2}
\]
Taking the maximum of (2) at \(c_L,c_U\) proves
\[
w(B)=h(0)-a_B+p_\xi(b_B).
\tag{3}
\]

Under the assumed attainments, put
\[
M=\max_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}\{a_B-p_\xi(b_B)\}.
\]
Equation (3) gives
\[
\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)=h(0)-M.
\tag{4}
\]
On the zero-covariance optimizer face \(a_B=a^\star\), the same equation gives
\[
\inf_{B_0\in\mathcal A_{0,Z}}w(B_0)
=h(0)-a^\star+inf_{B:\,a_B=a^\star}p_\xi(b_B).
\tag{5}
\]
Subtracting (4) from (5) proves the displayed formula for \(\Delta_Z\).  Because \(M\) is attained, \(\Delta_Z>0\) is equivalent to the existence of \(B\in\mathcal B_Z\) such that
\[
a_B-p_\xi(b_B)
>a^\star-\inf_{C:\,a_C=a^\star}p_\xi(b_C),
\]
which rearranges exactly to
\[
\inf_{C:\,a_C=a^\star}p_\xi(b_C)-p_\xi(b_B)
>a^\star-a_B.
\]
Finally, thm:zhao-ordinary-domain-quotient-transfer makes all criteria constant on the fibers of \(q_Z\), so it preserves the values and pulls every attained optimizer correspondence back to its exact inverse image in \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\).  No step invokes singular ranks, a minimax interchange, or a semidefinite reduction.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem gives the exact ordinary-inverse frontier for when copula-robust residual minimization strictly improves on the zero-covariance optimizer set. \textit{Gap.} The penalty over the noncompact zero-covariance equality face is correctly represented by an infimum; no penalty minimizer, singular completion, or universal strictness is assumed. \textit{Consumer.} prop:ordinary-domain-feasible-ht-gap-transfer

\begin{theorem}[thm:completion-domain-value-and-optimizer-transfer]\label{thm:completion-domain-value-and-optimizer-transfer}
Suppose ass:exposure-positivity and ass:frechet-covariance-domain hold and \(\mathcal B_Z\ne\varnothing\).  On the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain,
\[
v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
:=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B)
=\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)
=\inf_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing}}
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B).
\]
The possibly empty full-quotient equality set is
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
=\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:w^+(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\}.
\]
Writing \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\kappa)\) for the fixed-threshold argmin, its rank-two stratum is exactly
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\cap\iota(\mathcal B_Z)
=\iota\!\left(
\bigcup_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,
\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing,\ 
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+}}
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\kappa)\right).
\]
If the rank-two infimum is attained at \(C\in\mathcal B_Z\), then \(\iota(C)\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  Conversely, every rank-two point \(\iota(C)\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) is an ordinary-domain minimizer and belongs to a fixed-threshold argmin whose value equals \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  The singular equality stratum is
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\setminus\iota(\mathcal B_Z)
=\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1,\ w^+(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\}.
\]
A singular equality point need not imply rank-two attainment.  No unconditional full-class attainment or singular equality point is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
By exposure positivity all inverse-probability expressions are defined.  The sharp covariance-domain assumption ensures that \(c_L\) and \(c_U\) are the endpoints of the entire feasible cross-world covariance interval, so the endpoint maximum in \(w^+\) has its stated worst-copula interpretation.  Put
\[
 V_+=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B),
 \qquad
 V_Z=\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B).
\]

The rank-two stratum-identification lemma maps every \(C\in\mathcal B_Z\) to \(\iota(C)\in\mathcal B_Z^+\) and gives
\[
 w^+(\iota(C))=w(C).
\]
It follows at once that \(V_+\le V_Z\).  For the reverse inequality, fix any \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\).  If \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)=2\), the same lemma supplies a unique \(C\in\mathcal B_Z\) with \([B]_+=\iota(C)\), and hence
\[
 V_Z\le w(C)=w^+(B).
\tag{1}
\]
If \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1\), the rank-stratified projector-reduction lemma supplies a sequence \(B_j\in\mathcal B_Z\) satisfying
\[
 \limsup_{j\to\infty}w(B_j)\le w^+(B).
\]
Since \(V_Z\le w(B_j)\) for every \(j\),
\[
 V_Z
 \le\liminf_{j\to\infty}w(B_j)
 \le\limsup_{j\to\infty}w(B_j)
 \le w^+(B).
\tag{2}
\]
Equations \((1)\) and \((2)\) show that \(V_Z\) is a lower bound for \(w^+\) on all of \(\mathcal B_Z^+\).  Thus \(V_Z\le V_+\), and consequently
\[
 V_+=V_Z=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+.
\tag{3}
\]
The fixed-threshold value-exhaustion proposition then gives
\[
 v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
 =\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B)
 =\inf_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing}}
 \min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B).
\tag{4}
\]

The displayed description of \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) is precisely the equality set associated with the infimum in \((3)\); it may be empty because \((3)\) establishes a value equality, not attainment.  It remains to identify its rank-two part.  Suppose first that \(\iota(C)\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  Rank-two loss equality and \((3)\) yield
\[
 w(C)=w^+(\iota(C))=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+=V_Z.
\tag{5}
\]
The exhaustion proposition places \(C\) in some nonempty \(\mathcal B_{\kappa_C}\).  Because \(\mathcal B_{\kappa_C}\subseteq\mathcal B_Z\), equation \((5)\) gives
\[
 V_Z
 \le\min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa_C}}w(B)
 \le w(C)
 =V_Z.
\]
Therefore the threshold value equals \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) and \(C\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\kappa_C)\).

Conversely, suppose that for some nonempty \(\mathcal B_\kappa\),
\[
 C\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\kappa),
 \qquad
 \min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+.
\]
Then rank-two loss equality gives
\[
 w^+(\iota(C))=w(C)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+,
\]
so \(\iota(C)\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  The two implications prove
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\cap\iota(\mathcal B_Z)
=\iota\!\left(
\bigcup_{\substack{\kappa>0:\,
\mathcal B_\kappa\ne\varnothing,\ 
\min_{B\in\mathcal B_\kappa}w(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+}}
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\kappa)\right).
\tag{6}
\]
In particular, any ordinary-domain minimizer \(C\) satisfies \((5)\), hence maps into the full equality set; and every rank-two full equality point satisfies the converse conclusion just proved.

Finally, the stratum-identification lemma says that the complement of \(\iota(\mathcal B_Z)\) in \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) consists exactly of the classes with \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1\).  Intersecting that complement with the defining equality for \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) yields
\[
 \mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\setminus\iota(\mathcal B_Z)
 =\{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+:\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1,\ w^+(B)=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\}.
\]
The singular approximation used in \((2)\) produces only a rank-two minimizing sequence.  It does not assert that the rank-two infimum is attained, nor that either the full equality set or its singular stratum is nonempty. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Canonical full-carrier theorem on the note's projective quotient: it proves infimum equality by one-sided rank-two projector approximation and states conditional equality-set transfer without assuming attainment. \textit{Gap.} Zhao supplies the block parameterization and spectral convention, but not the note's endpoint-robust rank-two density and value transfer on its constructed completion. \textit{Consumer.} The full-class solver, plug-in value theorem, and triangle certificate use this hierarchy.

\begin{lemma}[lem:triangle-completion-singular-strata-certificate]\label{lem:triangle-completion-singular-strata-certificate}
For the triangle matrices displayed in thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate, the rank-zero and rank-one strata induced by Zhao's published unquotiented pseudoinverse carrier and expressed in the note's quotient obey
\[
\sup_{[B]_+\,:\,\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}g_B^+(0)
=\frac{87}{400}<\frac{31}{80}
\]
and
\[
\inf_{[B]_+\,:\,\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}w^+(B)
=\frac{39}{80}>\frac{2613}{8432}.
\]
More precisely, rank zero has \(g_B^+(0)=0\) and \(w^+(B)=171/200\).  In rank one, after normalizing the unique nonzero arm vector to squared norm three and writing \(u\) for the squared sum of its coordinates, \(0\le u\le9\), the focal-only stratum has
\[
g_x^+(c_L)=\frac{1195u+6561}{1200(81-4u)},
\qquad g_x^+(c_U)=\frac{243(49-5u)}{400(81-4u)},
\]
and the comparison-only stratum has
\[
g_y^+(c_L)=\frac{1240u+729}{150(4u+243)},
\qquad g_y^+(c_U)=\frac{81(48-5u)}{50(4u+243)}.
\]
These formulas and \(0\le u\le9\) are an exact semialgebraic certificate.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Simultaneous decomposition into \(\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf1\}\) and \(\mathbf1^\perp\) reduces \(729\Omega\) to
\[
\begin{bmatrix}60&96\\96&186\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{bmatrix}108&-108\\-108&162\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{1}
\]
Their determinants are \(1944\) and \(5832\), with positive leading entries, so \(\Omega\succ0\).  Therefore rank zero has both columns zero, while rank one has exactly one nonzero arm column.

For a focal-only normalized vector \(x\), put \(u=(\mathbf1^{\top}x)^2\).  Cauchy--Schwarz and \(\lVert x\rVert_2^2=3\) give \(0\le u\le9\).  Substitution in the scalar projector quotient from lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction gives
\[
g_x^+(c_L)=\frac{1195u+6561}{1200(81-4u)},
\qquad
g_x^+(c_U)=\frac{243(49-5u)}{400(81-4u)}.
\tag{2}
\]
Since \(0\) is the midpoint of the two endpoints and the gain is affine,
\[
g_x^+(0)=\frac{21141-1225u}{1200(81-4u)}.
\tag{3}
\]
Using the two uncontrolled endpoint variances in def:gain-loss and taking the larger residual gives
\[
w^+(x)=\frac{9(1755-17u)}{400(81-4u)}.
\tag{4}
\]
The derivative numerator in (3) is \(-14661\), while that in (4) is \(50787\), after deletion of their positive squared denominators.  Thus (3) decreases and (4) increases on \([0,9]\), giving
\[
\sup_xg_x^+(0)=\frac{87}{400},
\qquad
\inf_xw^+(x)=\frac{39}{80},
\tag{5}
\]
both at \(u=0\).

For a comparison-only normalized vector \(y\), the same calculation gives
\[
g_y^+(c_L)=\frac{1240u+729}{150(4u+243)},
\qquad
g_y^+(c_U)=\frac{81(48-5u)}{50(4u+243)},
\tag{6}
\]
and hence
\[
g_y^+(0)=\frac{25u+12393}{300(4u+243)},
\qquad
w^+(y)=\frac{9(256u+2889)}{200(4u+243)}.
\tag{7}
\]
The derivative numerators are \(-43497\) and \(50652\), respectively.  Therefore
\[
\sup_yg_y^+(0)=\frac{17}{100},
\qquad
\inf_yw^+(y)=\frac{107}{200}.
\tag{8}
\]
At rank zero the projector is zero, so the gain is zero and the larger uncontrolled endpoint loss is \(171/200\).  Comparing (5), (8), and rank zero proves
\[
\sup_{\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}g_B^+(0)=\frac{87}{400},
\qquad
\inf_{\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}w^+(B)=\frac{39}{80}.
\]
Finally,
\[
\frac{31}{80}-\frac{87}{400}=\frac{17}{100}>0,
\qquad
\frac{39}{80}-\frac{2613}{8432}=\frac{468}{2635}>0,
\]
which proves both strict comparisons.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Computes exact rank-zero and rank-one bounds for the note-constructed rank-stratified projective completion using Zhao's spectral convention. \textit{Gap.} The singular strata are part of this note's explicit projective rank completion; they are not attributed to a projective all-rank domain published by Zhao. \textit{Consumer.} The full-carrier triangle theorem uses the strict singular margins to exclude ties.

\begin{theorem}[thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate]\label{thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate}
There is a positive, fully explicit triangle witness whose strict separation survives every singular stratum in the note's quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented pseudoinverse domain.  Let \(n=3\), \(G\), \(\nu\), and \(f_i\) be the triangle network, Bernoulli \((1/3)\) assignment, and exposure maps displayed in thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate, and take \(F_1=F_0=\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2)\), \(\kappa=1/8\).  The exact matrices and endpoints are
\[
\Pi=\operatorname{diag}\!\left(\frac4{27}I_3,\frac{10}{27}I_3\right),
\quad
\Omega=\frac1{729}
\begin{bmatrix}108I_3-16J_3&68J_3-108I_3\\68J_3-108I_3&162I_3+8J_3\end{bmatrix},
\quad c_L=-\frac14,\quad c_U=\frac14,
\]
with the two endpoint matrices displayed in thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate.  The complete rank-two equality sets are
\[
\mathcal A_0=\{[B(x,y)]:\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y\rVert_2^2=3,
\ \mathbf1^\top x=\mathbf1^\top y=x^\top y=0\},
\]
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\{[B(x,y)]:\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y\rVert_2^2=3,
\ \mathbf1^\top x=x^\top y=0,\ (\mathbf1^\top y)^2=216/55\}.
\]
They lie in \(\mathcal B_\kappa\), have normalized smallest eigenvalue \(4/27\), and satisfy
\[
\max g_B(0)=\frac{31}{80},
\qquad w(B_0)=\frac{521}{1200},
\qquad w(B_{\mathrm{mm}})=v_{\mathrm{mm}}=\frac{2613}{8432}.
\]
On the note's quotient of the full published carrier,
\[
\mathcal A_0^+=\iota(\mathcal A_0),
\qquad \mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+=\iota(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}),
\]
\[
\max_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}g_B^+(0)=\frac{31}{80},
\qquad v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+=\min_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}w^+(B)=\frac{2613}{8432},
\]
and there are no singular optimizer ties.  Consequently \(\delta=4912/39525\) and \(\eta=5/216\) give the full-correspondence separation and optimizer-wide regularity slack.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
First cover the entire positive-definite rank-two restriction, not only the fixed-\(\kappa\) subset.  Normalize an arbitrary rank-two representative as \(B(x,y)\), put \(s=\mathbf1^{\top}x\), \(t=\mathbf1^{\top}y\), and \(q=x^{\top}y\), and define
\[
d=729+12t^2-36s^2-54q^2+68qst-22s^2t^2.
\tag{1}
\]
The matrix identity in the proof of thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate gives \(\det S_B=8d/19683\), so positive definiteness is equivalent to \(d>0\).  The same exact trace calculation, which did not use \(\kappa\), gives
\[
\frac{31}{80}-g_B(0)=\frac{N_0}{1200d},
\tag{2}
\]
where
\[
N_0=5022\left(q-\frac{1088}{2511}st\right)^2
+1629s^2+2148t^2-\frac{685118}{2511}s^2t^2\ge0.
\tag{3}
\]
As proved by the four-vertex bilinear certificate there, equality holds exactly when \(s=t=q=0\).  Likewise, with \(\lambda=1407/2635\),
\[
w(B)-\frac{2613}{8432}
\ge\lambda\ell_B(c_L)+(1-\lambda)\ell_B(c_U)-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{3N_{\mathrm{mm}}}{210800d},
\tag{4}
\]
where
\[
N_{\mathrm{mm}}
=274734\left(q-\frac{70411}{137367}st\right)^2
+s^2\left(34527-\frac{439017980}{137367}t^2\right)\ge0.
\tag{5}
\]
The coefficient in (5) is positive for \(t^2\le9\).  Equality requires \(s=q=0\), and equality in the convex-combination bound further requires \(55t^2=216\).  Thus (2)--(5) prove the displayed nominal and minimax equality sets and values over every rank-two class in \(\mathcal B_Z\), independently of \(\kappa\).

For completeness, the displayed triangle covariance is positive definite.  On \(\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf1\}\) and \(\mathbf1^\perp\), respectively, \(729\Omega\) reduces to
\[
\begin{bmatrix}60&96\\96&186\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{bmatrix}108&-108\\-108&162\end{bmatrix}.
\tag{6}
\]
Their determinants are \(1944\) and \(5832\), and their leading entries are positive; hence \(\Omega\succ0\).  Consequently rank zero has both arm columns zero, rank one has exactly one nonzero arm column, and two nonzero arm columns give rank two.

Lemma lem:triangle-completion-singular-strata-certificate proves
\[
\sup_{\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}g_B^+(0)
=\frac{87}{400}<\frac{31}{80},
\qquad
\inf_{\operatorname{rank}(S_B)\le1}w^+(B)
=\frac{39}{80}>\frac{2613}{8432}.
\tag{7}
\]
Thus no rank-zero or rank-one class in Zhao's published pseudoinverse carrier ties either rank-two optimum.  Lemma lem:zhao-rank-two-stratum-identification transfers the complete rank-two equality sets through \(\iota\), proving the two full-carrier optimizer identities and attainment of \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).

Finally, thm:triangle-minimax-separation-certificate shows that these equality sets lie in \(\mathcal B_{1/8}\) and have normalized smallest eigenvalue \(4/27\).  Therefore
\[
\delta=\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{4912}{39525},
\qquad
\eta=\frac4{27}-\frac18=\frac5{216}.
\]
This proves the full-correspondence separation, the absence of singular ties, and the optimizer-wide regularity slack.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The exact triangle computation covers all rank-two bases and the note-constructed singular strata of its projective completion, excluding every singular optimizer tie. \textit{Gap.} It supplies the explicit strict witness and regularity slack needed for neighborhood persistence; the quotient and its singular strata are the note's construction, while Zhao supplies the block parameterization and spectral pseudoinverse convention. \textit{Consumer.} thm:triangle-completion-relative-open-dominance

\begin{theorem}[thm:triangle-completion-relative-open-dominance]\label{thm:triangle-completion-relative-open-dominance}
For the explicit triangle instance \(\xi_0\) and \(\kappa=1/8\) in thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate, there is a relative-open neighborhood \(\mathcal U\subset\mathfrak X_\kappa\) containing \(\xi_0\) such that
\[
\Delta(\xi)>\frac{\delta}{2}=\frac{2456}{39525}
\qquad(\xi\in\mathcal U).
\]
Throughout \(\mathcal U\), the optimizer correspondences on the note's quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented pseudoinverse domain contain no singular classes and satisfy
\[
\mathcal A_0^+(\xi)=\iota_\xi(\mathcal A_0(\xi)),
\qquad
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+(\xi)=\iota_\xi(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)).
\]
Consequently every fixed-\(\kappa\) rank-two minimax optimizer has lower worst-copula loss than every fixed-\(\kappa\) zero-covariance optimizer, and the same holds for every pair of optimizers on that full quotient, with each difference exceeding \(\delta/2\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
At the triangle instance, lem:triangle-completion-singular-strata-certificate and thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate give the strict singular-stratum margins
\[
\frac{31}{80}-\frac{87}{400}=\frac{17}{100}
\quad\text{and}\quad
\frac{39}{80}-\frac{2613}{8432}=\frac{468}{2635}.
\tag{1}
\]
The block reduction
\[
729\Omega_0\sim
\begin{bmatrix}60&96\\96&186\end{bmatrix}
\oplus
\begin{bmatrix}108&-108\\-108&162\end{bmatrix}
\]
has positive leading entries and determinants \(1944\) and \(5832\), so \(\Omega_0\succ0\).  Total-variation and Wasserstein convergence, together with lem:restricted-marginal-extrema-continuity, make \(\Pi\), \(\Omega\), \(Q(c_L)\), and \(Q(c_U)\) continuous at \(\xi_0\).  Hence \(\Omega(\xi)\) remains uniformly positive definite on a sufficiently small relative-open neighborhood.

Represent the note's full quotient by the four compact normalized support components: both columns zero, focal only, comparison only, and both columns nonzero.  On the one-column components the pseudoinverse is a scalar reciprocal bounded away from zero.  On the two-column component, block separation and normalization give
\[
\lVert Bz\rVert_2^2=n\lVert z\rVert_2^2,
\]
so \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\succeq n\lambda_{\min}(\Omega)I_2\).  Thus all endpoint gains and losses are jointly continuous, uniformly over each compact component.  The two strict margins in (1) persist after shrinking the neighborhood.  Consequently no full-carrier nominal or minimax optimizer is singular there.

On the compact two-column component, the optimizer correspondences of the continuous nominal and worst-endpoint criteria are upper hemicontinuous by the elementary subsequence argument: any convergent sequence of optimizers has a limit that beats every fixed competitor after passing the defining inequality to the limit.  At \(\xi_0\), every such optimizer has normalized smallest eigenvalue \(4/27=\kappa+\eta\).  Continuity of \(\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}S_B)\) and compactness of the triangle optimizer sets therefore yield, after another shrinkage,
\[
\lambda_{\min}(n^{-1}S_B)>\kappa
\tag{2}
\]
for every nearby full-carrier optimizer.  Hence each full-carrier optimizer lies in \(\mathcal B_\kappa\).  Conversely, any \(\mathcal B_\kappa\)-optimizer attains the same global value because a global optimizer is already feasible in \(\mathcal B_\kappa\).  Lemma lem:zhao-rank-two-stratum-identification now gives
\[
\mathcal A_0^+(\xi)=\iota_\xi(\mathcal A_0(\xi)),
\qquad
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}^+(\xi)=\iota_\xi(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}(\xi)).
\tag{3}
\]

Finally, lem:dominance-gap-lower-semicontinuity and \(\Delta(\xi_0)=\delta\) imply, by the same sequential contradiction used in thm:triangle-relative-open-dominance, that the neighborhood may be chosen so that
\[
\Delta(\xi)>\frac\delta2.
\tag{4}
\]
Definition def:dominance-gap gives the fixed-\(\kappa\) pairwise loss difference in (4), and (3), together with preservation of loss under \(\iota\), gives the identical inequality for every pair of full-carrier optimizers.  \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem shows that the exact triangle advantage is stable to lawful perturbations of the design and pilot marginals.  Unlike HarshawMiddletonSavje2026, which concerns optimized variance estimation under interference and complex experimental designs, it fixes the network and assignment law and compares cross-world-copula-robust versus zero-covariance control-variate basis choices. \textit{Gap.} A positive gap at one isolated law would not establish a robust design phenomenon; persistence also requires optimizer-wide interior regularity and strict rank-stratum gaps.  The theorem does not claim to extend HarshawMiddletonSavje2026's variance-estimation target or optimization problem. \textit{Consumer.} It delivers the claimed relative-open neighborhood on which every robust optimizer beats every zero-covariance optimizer for this fixed-design basis problem.

\begin{theorem}[thm:triangle-zhao-ordinary-domain-separation]\label{thm:triangle-zhao-ordinary-domain-separation}
For the triangle instance of thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate, optimization over Zhao's unquotiented positive-definite rank-two ordinary-inverse restriction \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\) has
\[
\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}g_{q_Z(B)}(0)=q_Z^{-1}(\mathcal A_0),
\qquad
\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}w(q_Z(B))=q_Z^{-1}(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}),
\]
where \(\mathcal A_0\) and \(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}\) are exactly the displayed normalized equality sets in that theorem.  Their values are respectively \(31/80\) and \(2613/8432\); every zero-covariance optimizer has worst-copula loss \(521/1200\), so the exact worst-loss reduction is
\[
\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}=\frac{4912}{39525}>0.
\]
Thus the triangle separation holds on Zhao's entire unquotiented positive-definite rank-two ordinary-inverse restriction, with optimizer sets enlarged only by independent nonzero column rescaling.  The separate rank-zero and rank-one conclusions concern Zhao's published all-block-separable pseudoinverse carrier expressed on the note's projective quotient \(\mathcal B_Z^+\).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
By \texttt{def:zhao-well-defined-ordinary-domain}, \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\) is precisely Zhao's unquotiented positive-definite rank-two ordinary-inverse restriction, and \(q_Z\) maps it onto \(\mathcal B_Z\).  The complete rank-two part of \texttt{thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate} gives
\[
\operatorname*{argmax}_{[C]\in\mathcal B_Z}g_C(0)=\mathcal A_0,
\qquad
\operatorname*{argmin}_{[C]\in\mathcal B_Z}w(C)=\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}},
\]
with respective optimal values \(31/80\) and \(2613/8432\), and it gives
\[
w(C_0)=\frac{521}{1200}
\qquad\text{for every }C_0\in\mathcal A_0.
\tag{1}
\]

The map \(q_Z\) is surjective by \texttt{thm:zhao-ordinary-domain-quotient-transfer}.  The same theorem proves that \(g_{q_Z(B)}(0)\) and \(w(q_Z(B))\) are constant on each fiber of \(q_Z\).  Therefore, for the gain criterion,
\[
\begin{aligned}
B\in\operatorname*{argmax}_{C\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}g_{q_Z(C)}(0)
&\Longleftrightarrow
q_Z(B)\in\operatorname*{argmax}_{[C]\in\mathcal B_Z}g_C(0)\\
&\Longleftrightarrow q_Z(B)\in\mathcal A_0,
\end{aligned}
\]
which is exactly
\[
\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}g_{q_Z(B)}(0)
=q_Z^{-1}(\mathcal A_0).
\tag{2}
\]
Applying the identical fiber argument to the loss criterion gives
\[
\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z}w(q_Z(B))
=q_Z^{-1}(\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}}).
\tag{3}
\]
Surjectivity and fiber invariance also preserve the corresponding objective values.  In particular, (1)--(3) imply that every unquotiented zero-covariance optimizer has worst-endpoint loss \(521/1200\), whereas every unquotiented minimax optimizer has loss \(2613/8432\).  Direct subtraction yields
\[
\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{4912}{39525}>0.
\tag{4}
\]

Equations (2)--(4) use only the positive-definite rank-two restriction.  Rank-zero and rank-one classes are not elements of \(\widetilde{\mathcal B}_Z\); the separate conclusions about them in the triangle completion theorem are conclusions on Zhao's published all-block-separable pseudoinverse carrier expressed through the note's projective quotient \(\mathcal B_Z^+\).  This proves the corrected domain attribution and all asserted optimizer and value identities. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Transfers the exact triangle equality sets only to the unquotiented positive-definite rank-two restriction. \textit{Gap.} It no longer calls that restriction Zhao's maximal ordinary domain. \textit{Consumer.} The full-carrier triangle theorem separately handles published ranks zero and one.

\begin{lemma}[lem:full-carrier-countable-measurable-selector]\label{lem:full-carrier-countable-measurable-selector}
Suppose ass:exposure-positivity holds.  Let \((C_k)_{k\ge1}\) be the fixed enumeration of normalized rational-support classes of maximal achievable rank \(r_\Omega\) specified in \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\).  Under ass:bounded-outcomes and ass:unpaired-pilot, this enumeration is nonempty and, for every realized pilot sample,
\[
 \inf_{k\ge1}\widehat w_m^+(C_k)
 =\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B).
\]
For every finite strictly positive deterministic or pilot-measurable \(\varepsilon_m\), the index
\[
 \widehat k_m=
 \min\left\{k\ge1:
 \widehat w_m^+(C_k)
 \le \inf_{j\ge1}\widehat w_m^+(C_j)+\varepsilon_m
 \right\}
\]
is finite and pilot-measurable.  Hence \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+=C_{\widehat k_m}\) is a measurable class of rank \(r_\Omega\) in the note's quotient of Zhao's published carrier and satisfies
\[
 \widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)
 \le
 \inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon_m.
\]
The selector is rank two exactly when rank two is achievable.  This is a classical nonconstructive measurable existence selector: no full-carrier minimizer or zero-tolerance selector is used, no finite method for evaluating the countable infimum or certifying the first qualifying index on arbitrary real pilot inputs is supplied, and agreement with the exact-input cylindrical-algebraic selector is not asserted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Fix the design.  Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) well defined.  For \(e\in\{L,U\}\), let \(\widehat H_e\) be the comonotone or antimonotone quantile coupling of the two empirical marginals.  If
\[
\mathcal Y=(Y_1(1),\ldots,Y_n(1),-Y_1(0),\ldots,-Y_n(0))^{\top}
\]
under \(\widehat H_e^{\otimes n}\), the endpoint construction in \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\) gives
\[
\widehat Q_e=\mathbb E_{\widehat H_e^{\otimes n}}(\mathcal Y\mathcal Y^{\top})\succeq0.
\tag{1}
\]
Consequently
\[
\widehat A_e=\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}\widehat Q_e\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}\succeq0.
\tag{2}
\]
For every carrier class, cyclicity of trace and \(\texttt{lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction}\) write the empirical endpoint residual as
\[
\widehat\ell_{B,e}^+
=\widehat h_e-n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(P_B\widehat A_e),
\qquad e\in\{L,U\},
\tag{3}
\]
where \(\widehat h_e=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}(\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\widehat Q_e)\) is independent of \(B\).

We first show density in objective value on the maximal-rank stratum.  If \(r_\Omega=2\), positive definiteness of \(B^{\top}\Omega B\) is open, rational directions are dense in every signed-pivot chart, and normalization is continuous away from zero.  Hence normalized rational-support rank-two classes exist and are dense in each rank-two chart.  On a fixed rank stratum the projector, and thus (3), is continuous.

If \(r_\Omega=1\), let \([B]_+\) have rank one.  At least one arm column has nonzero image \(z_a=\Omega^{1/2}B_ae_a\).  Choose nonzero rational vectors converging to that arm direction.  Their images remain nonzero eventually.  Put the normalized rational vector in arm \(a\) and zero in the other arm.  The resulting class has rank one because maximal rank is one, and its one-dimensional projector converges to the projector onto \(\operatorname{range}(\Omega^{1/2}B)\).  These classes occur in \((C_k)\).  If \(r_\Omega=0\), every projector is zero and the repeated zero class prescribed by \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\) is nonempty and has every carrier value.

Now fix any \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\).  The approximation clause of \(\texttt{lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction}\) supplies maximal-rank classes \([B_j]_+\) and a projector \(P_\infty\) such that
\[
P_{B_j}\longrightarrow P_\infty,
\qquad P_\infty-P_B\succeq0.
\tag{4}
\]
Equations (2)--(4) and trace monotonicity imply, endpoint by endpoint and then after taking the maximum,
\[
\limsup_{j\to\infty}\widehat w_m^+(B_j)\le\widehat w_m^+(B).
\tag{5}
\]
Approximate each fixed \(B_j\) by the rational-support classes just constructed.  It follows that, for every \(\delta>0\), some \(C_k\) satisfies
\[
\widehat w_m^+(C_k)\le\widehat w_m^+(B)+\delta.
\tag{6}
\]
Taking infima and letting \(\delta\downarrow0\) proves
\[
\inf_{k\ge1}\widehat w_m^+(C_k)
=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B),
\tag{7}
\]
the reverse inequality being immediate because every \(C_k\) belongs to the carrier.  No cross-rank continuity or attainment is used.

For measurability, empirical means, variances, and endpoint cross moments are measurable functions of finitely many pilot order statistics.  Hence \(X_k:=\widehat w_m^+(C_k)\) is pilot measurable for each fixed \(k\), and \(\rho_m=\inf_{j\ge1}X_j\) is measurable as a countable infimum.  Strict positivity and finiteness of \(\varepsilon_m\) make the qualifying set nonempty.  Moreover,
\[
\{\widehat k_m=k\}
=\{X_k\le\rho_m+\varepsilon_m\}
\cap\bigcap_{j<k}\{X_j>\rho_m+\varepsilon_m\},
\tag{8}
\]
so \(\widehat k_m\) is finite and measurable.  The selected class has rank \(r_\Omega\), obeys the asserted positive-tolerance inequality by (7), and has rank two exactly when rank two is achievable.  The argument supplies only a classical countable measurable selector; it supplies no finite arbitrary-real stopping rule, zero-tolerance selector, or agreement with the distinct exact-input CAD selector. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This lemma supplies the measurable arbitrary-real positive-tolerance rule on Zhao's full pseudoinverse carrier. \textit{Gap.} The countable infimum and first qualifying index are set-theoretic objects and have no finite stopping test for arbitrary real pilot observations. \textit{Consumer.} The unrestricted plug-in value theorem and objective-regret rate use this selector.

\begin{theorem}[thm:encoded-cad-pilot-selector-certificate]\label{thm:encoded-cad-pilot-selector-certificate}
Suppose ass:exposure-positivity holds and let \(\widehat{\mathfrak I}_{m,\mathrm{alg}}^+\) be an algebraically encoded empirical endpoint input from \(\texttt{def:encoded-cad-pilot-selector}\), with exactly encoded \(\varepsilon_m>0\).  Put
\[
\widehat v_m^+=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B).
\]
The fixed rank-stratified cylindrical-algebraic procedure terminates and returns \(\widehat{\mathscr K}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}\).  Its basis component has rational or real-algebraic coordinates, and
\[
\widehat L_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
\le\widehat v_m^+
\le\widehat U_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+},
\qquad
\widehat U_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
-\widehat L_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
\le\varepsilon_m,
\]
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+})
\le\widehat v_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\]
The selected-rank field is exact and belongs to \(\{0,1,2\}\), and \(\widehat{\mathscr S}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD}}\) gives exact zero-versus-positive eigenvalue decisions and rational isolating intervals for all distinct positive eigenvalues, with multiplicities.  All conclusions are locally checkable from the finite CAD, algebraic-value, rank, and spectral records.  No agreement with the countable selector, zero-tolerance selector, complexity bound, or finite algorithm for arbitrary real-valued pilot inputs is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
The algebraically encoded empirical input contains finitely many rational or real-algebraic entries of \(\Pi\), \(\Omega\), \(\widehat Q_L\), and \(\widehat Q_U\), together with an exactly encoded \(\varepsilon_m>0\).  Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) well defined over the same real-algebraic coefficient field.  Each empirical endpoint matrix is positive semidefinite: as in (1) of the selector proof, it is the second-moment matrix under the appropriate empirical quantile coupling.  Thus the empirical input satisfies every algebraic support and positivity condition used by \(\texttt{thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-rank-stratified-epsilon-solver}\), with \(Q(c_L),Q(c_U)\) replaced by \(\widehat Q_L,\widehat Q_U\).

Apply that theorem to the empirical endpoint pair.  Its rank-stratified graph uses, for \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\succeq0\), the exact formulas
\[
S_B^+=0\quad\text{if }\operatorname{tr}(S_B)=0,
\]
\[
S_B^+=\frac{S_B}{\{\operatorname{tr}(S_B)\}^2}
\quad\text{if }\operatorname{tr}(S_B)>0,\ \det(S_B)=0,
\]
\[
S_B^+=\frac{\operatorname{adj}(S_B)}{\det(S_B)}
\quad\text{if }\det(S_B)>0.
\tag{1}
\]
All denominators in (1) are strictly positive on their stated strata.  After clearing them, the normalized signed-pivot charts, ranks, two endpoint residuals, and their maximum are a finite semialgebraic graph.  Real-closed-field quantifier elimination therefore isolates the empirical infimum
\[
\widehat v_m^+=\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)
\]
and terminates with algebraic bounds satisfying
\[
\widehat L_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
\le\widehat v_m^+
\le\widehat U_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+},
\qquad
\widehat U_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}
-\widehat L_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}\le\varepsilon_m.
\tag{2}
\]
The defining property of the infimum and \(\varepsilon_m>0\) make the strict empirical \(\varepsilon_m\)-sublevel nonempty.  CAD lifting supplies a normalized rational or real-algebraic sample in it, giving
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+})
\le\widehat v_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\tag{3}
\]

It remains to verify the augmented rank and spectral record.  The selected matrix \(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}}\) is a \(2\times2\) positive-semidefinite matrix with real-algebraic entries.  Its characteristic polynomial is
\[
\chi_m(t)=t^2-\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})t
+\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}}).
\tag{4}
\]
Exact sign determination for the algebraic trace and determinant gives precisely rank zero, one, or two according as
\[
\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})=0,
\quad
\operatorname{tr}(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})>0=\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}}),
\quad\text{or}\quad
\det(\widehat S_m^{\mathrm{CAD}})>0.
\tag{5}
\]
Exact real-algebraic root isolation applied to (4) terminates and returns the distinct roots, multiplicities, exact zero tags, and disjoint rational isolating intervals for all positive roots.
