Retaining the finite CAD projection and lifting records, the value isolations in (2), the sample in (3), and the spectral data (4)--(5) gives exactly \(\widehat{\mathscr K}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD},+}\).  Each conclusion is checked by finite algebraic sign and interval arithmetic.  Nothing identifies this finite exact-input output with the arbitrary-real countable selector or supplies a complexity bound or zero-tolerance rule. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This is the finite, locally checkable counterpart to the unrestricted nonconstructive countable selector. \textit{Gap.} It requires exact rational or real-algebraic empirical endpoint input and does not by itself certify positive-rank deployment stability. \textit{Consumer.} Exact-input deployments use its rank and spectral record either directly or as the unrestricted benchmark for the stable CAD certificate.

\begin{lemma}[lem:full-carrier-plugin-objective-regret-rate]\label{lem:full-carrier-plugin-objective-regret-rate}
Under ass:bounded-outcomes, ass:unpaired-pilot, and ass:exposure-positivity, define
\[
 M_m=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},
 \qquad
 d_m^+=\sup_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}
 |\widehat w_m^+(B)-w^+(B)|,
\]
and let \(\pi_{\min,n}=\min_{i,a}\pi_{i,a}>0\).  For every rank zero, one, or two class \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\),
\[
 0\preceq A_B^+\preceq A_{\mathrm{HT}},
 \qquad
 A_B^+=\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}P_B\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1},
 \qquad
 A_{\mathrm{HT}}=\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1},
\]
and
\[
 d_m^+\le 4\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}n^{-1}M_m.
\]
Along a triangular sequence satisfying ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate, this yields
\[
 d_m^+\le4\underline\pi^{-1}n^{\beta-1}M_m
\tag{A}
\]
and, for every positive deterministic or pilot-measurable \(\varepsilon_m\),
\[
 \mathbb E_P\!\left[
 w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
 \right]
 \le
 8\underline\pi^{-1}n^{\beta-1}\mathbb E_P[M_m]
 +\mathbb E_P[\varepsilon_m].
\tag{B}
\]
Consequently,
\[
 \frac{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}{n^{1+\beta}}\longrightarrow\infty,
 \qquad n\mathbb E_P[\varepsilon_m]\longrightarrow0
\]
imply
\[
 n\mathbb E_P\!\left[
 w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
 \right]\longrightarrow0.
\]
The selected class has maximal achievable rank \(r_\Omega\).  This is a full-carrier basis-objective regret result on the note's quotient of Zhao's all-rank pseudoinverse domain, not a feasible-estimator variance bridge when the selected rank is singular.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
For \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\), \(\texttt{lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction}\) supplies the orthogonal projector
\[
P_B=\Omega^{1/2}B(B^{\top}\Omega B)^+B^{\top}\Omega^{1/2},
\]
so \(0\preceq P_B\preceq I_{2n}\).  Congruence by \(\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}\), licensed by exposure positivity, gives
\[
0\preceq A_B^+
=\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}P_B\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}
\preceq \Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}=A_{\mathrm{HT}}.
\tag{1}
\]
Both matrices are positive semidefinite, so their nuclear norms equal their traces.  Since \(D_{i,a}\) is Bernoulli of mean \(\pi_{i,a}\),
\[
\Omega_{(i,a),(i,a)}=\pi_{i,a}(1-\pi_{i,a}).
\tag{2}
\]
Thus, with \(\pi_{\min,n}=\min_{i,a}\pi_{i,a}>0\),
\[
\lVert A_B^+\rVert_*
\le\operatorname{tr}(A_{\mathrm{HT}})
=\sum_{i,a}\frac{1-\pi_{i,a}}{\pi_{i,a}}
\le2n\pi_{\min,n}^{-1},
\qquad
\lVert A_{\mathrm{HT}}\rVert_*le2n\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}.
\tag{3}
\]
For \(e\in\{L,U\}\), the empirical and population loss definitions give
\[
\widehat\ell_{B,e}^+-\ell_B^+(c_e)
=n^{-2}\operatorname{tr}\!\left[(A_{\mathrm{HT}}-A_B^+)
\{\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\}\right].
\tag{4}
\]
Trace duality, (3), and the one-Lipschitz property of the maximum in the supremum norm imply
\[
d_m^+\le4\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}n^{-1}M_m.
\tag{5}
\]
Under \(\texttt{ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate}\), \(\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}\le\underline\pi^{-1}n^\beta\), which proves (A).

For the expectation rate, put \(D_a=W_1(\widehat F_a,F_a)\).  On \([0,1]\), the distribution-function and quantile formulas for \(W_1\), Jensen's inequality, and the binomial variance identity give
\[
\mathbb E_PD_a
\le\int_0^1\sqrt{\frac{F_a(y)\{1-F_a(y)\}}{m_a}}\,dy
\le\frac1{2\sqrt{m_a}}.
\tag{6}
\]
The one- and two-Lipschitz moment maps and
\(|xy-x'y'|\le|x-x'|+|y-y'|\) give
\[
|\widehat\mu_a-\mu_a|\le D_a,
\quad |\widehat\sigma_a^2-\sigma_a^2|\le4D_a,
\quad |\widehat c_e-c_e|\le2(D_1+D_0).
\tag{7}
\]
Using the block formula for \(Q\), \(\lVert I_n\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1\), and \(\lVert\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\top}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=n\), there is a numerical \(K_M<\infty\) such that, for \(m=m_1\wedge m_0\),
\[
\mathbb E_PM_m\le K_M\frac n{\sqrt m}.
\tag{8}
\]

By \(\texttt{lem:full-carrier-countable-measurable-selector}\), pointwise in the pilot,
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)
\le\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon_m.
\tag{9}
\]
For two functions on the same nonempty domain,
\[
|\inf f-\inf g|\le\sup|f-g|.
\tag{10}
\]
The carrier is nonempty because it contains the zero class.  Applying (10), (9), and the definition of \(d_m^+\) yields
\[
0\le w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
\le2d_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\tag{11}
\]
Taking expectations and using (A) proves (B).  Finally, (8) gives
\[
n\mathbb E_P\!\left[w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\right]
\le8K_M\underline\pi^{-1}\frac{n^{1+\beta}}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}
+n\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m\longrightarrow0.
\tag{12}
\]
The rank claim follows from the selector construction.  The proof controls the all-rank basis objective and does not assert an observable-estimator bridge at an uncontrolled singular rank. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} A uniform all-rank projector modulus converts pilot endpoint-matrix error and optimization tolerance into an explicit full-carrier regret bound. \textit{Gap.} The former statement carried a rank-two-existence premise not used by the all-rank matrix inequality. \textit{Consumer.} The theorem on pilot value consistency uses its uniform modulus, while triangular-sequence readers can use its explicit pilot-size rate.

\begin{lemma}[lem:bounded-unpaired-endpoint-strong-convergence]\label{lem:bounded-unpaired-endpoint-strong-convergence}
Under ass:bounded-outcomes and ass:unpaired-pilot, fix \(n\), form the two empirical endpoint matrices in def:pilot-plugin, and put
\[
 M_m=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}
 \lVert \widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
As \(m_1\wedge m_0\to\infty\), \(M_m\to0\) almost surely under the pilot law \(P\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
For arm \(a\), write \(F_{a,m_a}\) for the distribution function of \(\widehat F_a\).  For every rational \(t\in[0,1]\), the variables
\[
X_{j,a}(t)=\mathbf 1\{Y^P_{j,a}\le t\}
\]
are independent Bernoulli variables with mean \(F_a(t)\) by \(\texttt{ass:unpaired-pilot}\).  Hoeffding's elementary exponential argument gives, for \(s>0\),
\[
P\!\left(\left|F_{a,m_a}(t)-F_a(t)\right|>s\right)
\le 2e^{-2m_as^2}.
\tag{1}
\]
Indeed, for a Bernoulli variable \(X\) of mean \(p\), convexity gives
\(\mathbb E e^{\lambda(X-p)}\le e^{\lambda^2/8}\); exponential Markov inequality, independence, and optimization in \(\lambda\) yield (1).  The right side is summable in \(m_a\).  Intersecting the resulting probability-one events over rational \(t\) and rational \(s>0\) shows that \(F_{a,m_a}(t)\to F_a(t)\) simultaneously at every rational \(t\).

Fix \(K\ge1\) and put \(x_j=j/K\).  Monotonicity of the two distribution functions implies, for \(y\in[x_j,x_{j+1}]\),
\[
|F_{a,m_a}(y)-F_a(y)|
\le \max_{r\in\{j,j+1\}}|F_{a,m_a}(x_r)-F_a(x_r)|
+F_a(x_{j+1})-F_a(x_j).
\tag{2}
\]
Integrating, summing, passing to the limit on the finite grid, and telescoping the last increments gives
\[
\limsup_{m_a\to\infty}\int_0^1|F_{a,m_a}(y)-F_a(y)|\,dy\le K^{-1}
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{3}
\]
Letting \(K\to\infty\) proves convergence of this integral to zero.  For probability laws \(R,S\) on \([0,1]\), the layer-cake representation and Tonelli's theorem give
\[
\int_0^1|R^{-1}(u)-S^{-1}(u)|\,du
=\int_0^1|R(y)-S(y)|\,dy.
\tag{4}
\]
Thus
\[
\int_0^1|\widehat F_a^{-1}(u)-F_a^{-1}(u)|\,du\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely},\qquad a\in\{1,0\}.
\tag{5}
\]
The bounded support used in (2)--(5) is exactly \(\texttt{ass:bounded-outcomes}\).

The functions \(y\) and \(y^2\) are respectively one- and two-Lipschitz on \([0,1]\), so (5) yields convergence of the empirical means, second moments, and variances.  Moreover,
\[
|xy-x'y'|\le |x-x'|+|y-y'|
\qquad(x,x',y,y'\in[0,1]).
\tag{6}
\]
Applying (6) to the comonotone and antimonotone quantile pairs proves convergence of both empirical endpoint cross moments and hence of both empirical covariance endpoints.  Every block of \(\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\) is therefore a convergent scalar multiplying either \(I_n\) or \(\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\top}\).  Since \(n\) is fixed and
\[
\lVert I_n\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1,
\qquad \lVert\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\top}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=n,
\tag{7}
\]
we obtain
\[
M_m=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely under }P.
\]
The additional stable and unrestricted selectors now present in \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\) do not enter this endpoint calculation. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This pilot-only lemma turns empirical marginal convergence into operator-norm convergence of both endpoint moment matrices. \textit{Gap.} Unpaired pilots identify the margins and sharp endpoints but not a cross-world copula. \textit{Consumer.} It supplies the almost-sure input convergence used in the full-carrier pilot value theorem and is unchanged by the selector-rank correction.

\begin{theorem}[thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-plugin-value-and-epsilon-regret]\label{thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-plugin-value-and-epsilon-regret}
Fix \(G,\nu,(f_i)_{i=1}^n\) satisfying ass:exposure-positivity.  Under ass:bounded-outcomes and ass:unpaired-pilot, let \(\widehat w_m^+(B)\) and, for \(\varepsilon_m>0\), \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+\) be the distinct full-class empirical objective and classical nonconstructive measurable first-qualifying maximal-rank selector in \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\), and put
\[
d_m^+=\sup_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}|\widehat w_m^+(B)-w^+(B)|.
\]
As \(m_1\wedge m_0\to\infty\), \(d_m^+\to0\) almost surely and
\[
\left|\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\right|
\le d_m^+\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]
For every positive deterministic or pilot-measurable \(\varepsilon_m\),
\[
w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)
\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++2d_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\]
Consequently, if \(\varepsilon_m\to0\) almost surely, then
\[
w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\]
The objective domain is the note's projective quotient of Zhao's published unquotiented all-block-separable pseudoinverse domain, and the classical positive-tolerance selector has maximal achievable rank \(r_\Omega\), which is rank two exactly when rank two is achievable.  No exact selector at zero tolerance, cross-rank outer-argmin convergence, point convergence, or uncontrolled singular-rank feasible-estimator variance bridge is asserted.  The theorem supplies no finite evaluation rule for the countable selector on arbitrary real pilot inputs and makes no agreement claim with the separately defined exact-input cylindrical-algebraic selector.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Put
\[
M_m=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.
\]
By \(\texttt{lem:bounded-unpaired-endpoint-strong-convergence}\),
\[
M_m\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely as }m_1\wedge m_0\to\infty.
\tag{1}
\]
Because the design and \(n\) are fixed and exposure positivity holds,
\(\pi_{\min,n}:=\min_{i,a}\pi_{i,a}>0\).  The uniform all-rank modulus in \(\texttt{lem:full-carrier-plugin-objective-regret-rate}\) therefore gives
\[
d_m^+\le4\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}n^{-1}M_m\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{2}
\]
The carrier is nonempty because it contains the zero class, and the elementary inequality
\[
|\inf f-\inf g|\le\sup|f-g|
\tag{3}
\]
applied on \(\mathcal B_Z^+\) yields
\[
\left|\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\right|
\le d_m^+\longrightarrow0
\quad\text{almost surely}.
\tag{4}
\]

For every finite positive deterministic or pilot-measurable \(\varepsilon_m\), \(\texttt{lem:full-carrier-countable-measurable-selector}\) gives a finite measurable index, a selected class of rank \(r_\Omega\), and
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)
\le\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon_m.
\tag{5}
\]
Twice applying the uniform error gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)
&\le\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)+d_m^+\\
&\le\inf_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon_m+d_m^+\\
&\le v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++2d_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\end{aligned}
\tag{6}
\]
Since \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) is the infimum on the same carrier,
\[
0\le w^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^+)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
\le2d_m^++\varepsilon_m.
\tag{7}
\]
Equations (2) and (7) prove the asserted regret convergence when \(\varepsilon_m\to0\) almost surely.  The selected rank is \(r_\Omega\), hence is two exactly when rank two is achievable.  The argument uses neither a zero-tolerance selector nor cross-rank argmin convergence, and the countable rule remains distinct from the exact-input CAD selector. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} Uniform endpoint-matrix convergence and the maximal-rank selector give full-carrier value consistency without assuming attainment across rank changes. \textit{Gap.} The result concerns the basis objective and deliberately gives no singular-rank observable-estimator bridge. \textit{Consumer.} The stable observable theorem uses its uniform full-carrier pilot modulus.

\begin{lemma}[lem:uniform-pilot-endpoint-matrix-rate]\label{lem:uniform-pilot-endpoint-matrix-rate}
Along a triangular sequence satisfying ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility and ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate, let \(\widehat Q_L,\widehat Q_U\) be the two endpoint matrices obtained from the unpaired empirical marginals, put \(M_n=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\), and define
\[
C_n=n^{-2}\left[
\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\rVert_*
+\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}
\left\lVert\Pi^{-1}\Omega B(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\right\rVert_*
\right].
\]
Under ass:bounded-outcomes and ass:unpaired-pilot,
\[
M_n=O_{L^1}\!\left(\frac{n}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}\right),
\qquad
C_n\le4\underline\pi^{-1}n^{\beta-1}.
\]
More precisely, with
\[
A_{\mathrm{HT}}=\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1},
\qquad
A_B=\Pi^{-1}\Omega B(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1},
\]
one has
\[
0\preceq A_B\preceq A_{\mathrm{HT}},
\qquad
\lVert A_B\rVert_*
\le\operatorname{tr}(A_{\mathrm{HT}})
=\sum_{i=1}^n\sum_{a\in\{1,0\}}\frac{1-\pi_{i,a}}{\pi_{i,a}}
\le2\underline\pi^{-1}n^{1+\beta}.
\]
The uniform plug-in inequalities are
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|
\le C_nM_n,
\]
and, writing \(\widehat Q(0)\) for the zero-covariance moment matrix formed from the empirical marginal moments,
\[
\lVert\widehat Q(0)-Q(0)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le2M_n,
\]
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat g_B(0)-g_B(0)|
\le2n^{-2}\sup_B\lVert A_B\rVert_*M_n
\le2C_nM_n.
\]
Hence the exact downstream premise
\[
nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\longrightarrow0
\]
implies first-order plug-in accuracy in the explicit sense that the pilot expectations of both displayed uniform criterion errors are \(o(n^{-1})\).  The condition
\[
\frac{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}{n^{1+\beta}}\longrightarrow\infty
\]
is sufficient.  Neither known exposure nor a dependency-neighborhood condition is used in this pilot-only matrix calculation.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Put \(m=m_1\wedge m_0\) and let \(W_1\) denote one-dimensional Wasserstein distance.  For laws \(R,S\) on \([0,1]\), layer cake and Tonelli give
\[
W_1(R,S)=\int_0^1|R^{-1}(u)-S^{-1}(u)|\,du
=\int_0^1|R(y)-S(y)|\,dy.
\tag{1}
\]
For each arm, Jensen's inequality and the binomial variance identity imply
\[
\mathbb E_PW_1(\widehat F_a,F_a)
\le\int_0^1\sqrt{\frac{F_a(y)\{1-F_a(y)\}}{m_a}}\,dy
\le\frac1{2\sqrt{m_a}}.
\tag{2}
\]
Because \(y\) and \(y^2\) are one- and two-Lipschitz on \([0,1]\),
\[
|\widehat\mu_a-\mu_a|\le W_1(\widehat F_a,F_a),
\qquad
|\widehat\sigma_a^2-\sigma_a^2|\le4W_1(\widehat F_a,F_a).
\tag{3}
\]
The inequality \(|xy-x'y'|\le|x-x'|+|y-y'|\), applied to the endpoint quantile products, gives
\[
|\widehat c_e-c_e|
\le2\{W_1(\widehat F_1,F_1)+W_1(\widehat F_0,F_0)\},
\qquad e\in\{L,U\}.
\tag{4}
\]
Every block of \(\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\) is a linear combination of \(I_n\) and \(\mathbf1\mathbf1^{\top}\), with coefficients controlled by (3)--(4).  Since their operator norms are \(1\) and \(n\), respectively, (2)--(4) yield a numerical constant \(K_M<\infty\) such that
\[
\mathbb E_PM_n\le K_M\frac n{\sqrt m}.
\tag{5}
\]

For \(B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\), let \(Z_B=\Omega^{1/2}B\).  Regularity makes \(Z_B^{\top}Z_B\) invertible, and
\[
P_B=Z_B(Z_B^{\top}Z_B)^{-1}Z_B^{\top}
\tag{6}
\]
is an orthogonal projector.  Hence \(0\preceq P_B\preceq I_{2n}\), and congruence gives
\[
0\preceq A_B
=\Pi^{-1}\Omega^{1/2}P_B\Omega^{1/2}\Pi^{-1}
\preceq A_{\mathrm{HT}}=\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}.
\tag{7}
\]
Both matrices are positive semidefinite.  Therefore their nuclear norms are their traces, and
\[
\lVert A_B\rVert_*
\le\operatorname{tr}(A_{\mathrm{HT}})
=\sum_{i,a}\frac{1-\pi_{i,a}}{\pi_{i,a}}
\le2\underline\pi^{-1}n^{1+\beta}.
\tag{8}
\]
The same last bound holds for \(A_{\mathrm{HT}}\).  Substitution in the definition of \(C_n\) proves
\[
C_n\le4\underline\pi^{-1}n^{\beta-1}.
\tag{9}
\]
Trace duality and the one-Lipschitz property of the endpoint maximum prove
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|\le C_nM_n.
\tag{10}
\]

For the zero-covariance matrix, write \(\Delta_e=\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\) and \(d_e=\widehat c_e-c_e\).  Affinity gives
\[
\widehat Q(0)-Q(0)=\Delta_e+d_eE.
\tag{11}
\]
Choose a unit vector \(z\perp\mathbf1\), possible because \(n\ge3\).  Evaluating the off-diagonal block of \(\Delta_e\) between \((z,0)\) and \((0,z)\) shows
\[
|d_e|\le\lVert\Delta_e\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}.
\tag{12}
\]
Since \(\lVert E\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=1\), (11)--(12) yield
\[
\lVert\widehat Q(0)-Q(0)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le2M_n.
\tag{13}
\]
Trace duality and (8) now give
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat g_B(0)-g_B(0)|
\le2n^{-2}\sup_B\lVert A_B\rVert_*M_n
\le2C_nM_n.
\tag{14}
\]
The exact premise \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\) makes the expectations of (10) and (14) \(o(n^{-1})\).  Finally, (5) and (9) show
\[
nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n
\le4\underline\pi^{-1}K_M\frac{n^{1+\beta}}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}},
\]
proving the stated sufficient pilot-size condition.  No known-exposure or dependency-neighborhood property was used. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The projector-order calculation isolates the true pilot sensitivity and supplies a dependency-degree-free first-order sufficient boundary. \textit{Gap.} It retains the exact condition \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\) and proves \(\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}/n^{1+\beta}\to\infty\) only as a sufficient specialization. \textit{Consumer.} thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer and thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-plugin-value-and-epsilon-regret

\begin{lemma}[lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence]\label{lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence}
Suppose ass:known-exposure, ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility, ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate, ass:zhao-dependency-neighborhood-rate, ass:zhao-first-order-rate, ass:bounded-outcomes, and ass:iid-potential-pairs hold.  Uniformly over the nonempty class \(B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\), let
\[
\gamma_B^*(Y)=(B^{\top}\Omega B)^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y
\]
and let \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}\) be the control-variate estimator using this fixed-array oracle coefficient.  There are finite constants \(K_\Delta,K_{\mathrm{cov}},K_R\), independent of \(n\), \(B\), the bounded array \(Y\), and \(H\), such that the deterministic finite-\(n\) envelope
\[
R_n=K_\Delta n^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
+2K_{\mathrm{cov}}n^{(7/2)\alpha+3\beta-2},
\qquad K_R=K_\Delta+2K_{\mathrm{cov}},
\]
satisfies, for every \(n\ge3\),
\[
0\le R_n\le K_Rn^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
\]
and the two uniform absolute bounds
\[
\sup_{\substack{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\\0\le Y_i(a)\le1}}
\left|
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid Y)
-\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}\mid Y)
\right|
\le R_n,
\]
\[
\sup_{\substack{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\\H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}}
\left|
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid Y)
-\ell_B(c_H)
\right|
\le R_n.
\]
The oracle ex ante identity is exact:
\[
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}\mid Y)
=\ell_B(c_H),
\qquad
c_H=\operatorname{Cov}_H\{Y_i(1),Y_i(0)\}.
\]
Since \(4\alpha+4\beta<1\), one has \(nR_n\to0\); hence the feasible and oracle conditional design variances differ by \(o(n^{-1})\), and the feasible ex ante variance equals \(\ell_B(c_H)+o(n^{-1})\), uniformly over the rate-admissible class.  The expectations over \(H^{\otimes n}\) are ex ante basis-performance averages, not additional randomness in the conditional design-based estimand.  The pseudoinverse coefficient equals the ordinary inverse on this class; no uniform coefficient-rate assertion is made on singular ranks.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Assumption \(\texttt{ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility}\) makes \(\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\) nonempty.  Put \(N=2n\), index the coordinates of \(D\) by \(\ell\), write \(Z_\ell=D_\ell-\pi_\ell\), and put \(d_n=\max_\ell|N_\ell|\).

If \(\ell\) occurs exactly once in an ordered triple or quadruple and every other distinct index lies outside \(N_\ell\), then \(Z_\ell\) is independent of the product of the remaining centered coordinates by \(\texttt{ass:zhao-dependency-neighborhood-rate}\); hence that mixed moment vanishes.  Charging every singleton in a nonvanishing triple to a directed neighbor gives at most \(8Nd_n^2\) contributing ordered triples.  Partitioning quadruples into multiplicity types \(4\), \(3+1\), \(2+2\), \(2+1+1\), and \(1+1+1+1\), and charging singleton indices in the same way, gives at most \(64N^2d_n^2\) contributing ordered quadruples.  Since \(|Z_\ell|\le1\),
\[
\mathfrak M_{3,n}\le8Nd_n^2,
\qquad
\mathfrak M_{4,n}\le64N^2d_n^2.
\tag{1}
\]
This count uses only coordinate-versus-complement independence.  If \(k\notin N_\ell\), then \(D_\ell\) is independent of \(D_k\), so \(\Omega_{k\ell}=0\).  Therefore
\[
s_n\le d_n\le\overline d n^\alpha.
\tag{2}
\]

Fix a bounded array and \(B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\).  Known exposure and the signed convention in \(Y\) imply
\[
\mathbb E_\nu\widehat Y_\ell^{\mathrm{obs}}=Y_\ell,
\qquad
\{\Pi^{-1}(\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}-Y)\}_\ell
=\frac{Y_\ell Z_\ell}{\pi_\ell^2}.
\tag{3}
\]
All divisions are licensed by exposure positivity.  With \(G_B=\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)-\gamma_B^*(Y)\), equation (3) gives
\[
G_B=\sum_{\ell=1}^Nu_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad
u_{B,\ell}=S_B^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega e_\ell\frac{Y_\ell}{\pi_\ell^2}.
\tag{4}
\]
Every covariance column has at most \(s_n\) nonzero entries, every covariance entry has absolute value at most one, \(\lVert S_B^{-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le(n\kappa)^{-1}\), and the basis coordinates are bounded by \(\overline a\).  From (2) and \(\pi_{\min,n}\ge\underline\pi n^{-\beta}\), there is a finite constant \(K_u\), independent of \(n,B,Y,H\), such that
\[
\sup_{B,\ell}\lVert u_{B,\ell}\rVert_2
\le K_un^{\alpha+2\beta-1}.
\tag{5}
\]
Also
\[
X_B=n^{-1}B^{\top}(D-\mathbb E_\nu D)
=\sum_{\ell=1}^Nx_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad
\sup_{B,\ell}\lVert x_{B,\ell}\rVert_2\le\overline a/n.
\tag{6}
\]
Applying the fourth-moment bound (1) coordinatewise to (4) and (6) gives constants \(K_G,K_X<\infty\) for which
\[
\sup_B\mathbb E_\nu\lVert G_B\rVert_2^4
\le K_Gn^{6\alpha+8\beta-2},
\qquad
\sup_B\mathbb E_\nu\lVert X_B\rVert_2^4
\le K_Xn^{2\alpha-2}.
\tag{7}
\]
The feasible and oracle estimators obey
\[
\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}=\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}+\Delta_B,
\qquad \Delta_B=-G_B^{\top}X_B.
\tag{8}
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz and (7) yield a finite \(K_\Delta\) such that
\[
\sup_B\mathbb E_\nu\Delta_B^2
\le K_\Delta n^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}.
\tag{9}
\]

Put \(T_B=\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}-\tau\).  It is centered and has representation
\[
T_B=\sum_{\ell=1}^Nt_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad
t_{B,\ell}=n^{-1}\{Y_\ell/\pi_\ell-B_{\ell\cdot}\gamma_B^*(Y)\}.
\tag{10}
\]
The oracle projection inequality is
\[
\gamma_B^*(Y)^{\top}S_B\gamma_B^*(Y)
\le Y^{\top}\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y.
\tag{11}
\]
At most \(Ns_n\) covariance entries are nonzero.  Bounded outcomes, (2), the exposure-probability lower bound, and \(S_B\succeq n\kappa I_2\) therefore give finite constants \(K_\gamma,K_t\) such that
\[
\sup_B\lVert\gamma_B^*(Y)\rVert_2\le K_\gamma n^{\alpha/2+\beta},
\qquad
\sup_{B,\ell}|t_{B,\ell}|\le K_tn^{\alpha/2+\beta-1}.
\tag{12}
\]
Expanding \(\operatorname{Cov}_\nu(T_B,\Delta_B\mid Y)\) and applying the third-moment bound (1), (5), (6), and (12) gives
\[
\sup_B|\operatorname{Cov}_\nu(T_B,\Delta_B\mid Y)|
\le K_{\mathrm{cov}}n^{(7/2)\alpha+3\beta-2}.
\tag{13}
\]
The variance identity for \(T_B+\Delta_B\), together with (9) and (13), proves the first uniform bound with
\[
R_n=K_\Delta n^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
+2K_{\mathrm{cov}}n^{(7/2)\alpha+3\beta-2}.
\tag{14}
\]
Because \(\alpha,\beta\ge0\), the second exponent is no larger than the first; hence
\[
0\le R_n\le K_Rn^{4\alpha+4\beta-2},
\qquad K_R=K_\Delta+2K_{\mathrm{cov}}.
\tag{15}
\]

Completing the square for the fixed oracle coefficient gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}\mid Y)
=n^{-2}Y^{\top}\{\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
-\Pi^{-1}\Omega BS_B^{-1}B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\}Y.
\tag{16}
\]
Under \(\texttt{ass:iid-potential-pairs}\), \(\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}(YY^{\top})=Q(c_H)\).  Taking the trace expectation in (16) and applying \(\texttt{def:gain-loss}\) yields
\[
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or}}\mid Y)
=\ell_B(c_H).
\tag{17}
\]
Integrating the first uniform bound proves the second.  Finally, \(4\alpha+4\beta<1\) gives \(nR_n\to0\).  All constants may depend on fixed \(\kappa,\overline a,\underline\pi,\overline d\), but not on \(n,B,Y\), or \(H\). \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This is the quantitative regular rank-two feasible-estimator bridge.  It derives the finite remainder from Zhao's directed coordinate-versus-complement neighborhoods using the singleton criterion and explicit multiplicity and directed-cycle counting with universal constants 8 and 64. \textit{Gap.} Uniformity additionally requires bounded outcomes, the coordinate envelope, normalized kappa-regularity, exposure-probability control, and the first-order exponent condition; no separated-set dependency graph or singular-rank coefficient rate is used. \textit{Consumer.} thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer

\begin{theorem}[thm:rank-adaptive-oracle-observable-variance-bridge]\label{thm:rank-adaptive-oracle-observable-variance-bridge}
Suppose ass:known-exposure, ass:exposure-positivity, and ass:bounded-outcomes hold.  For \([B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+\), define
\[
\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)=S_B^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y
\]
and let \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\) be the estimator in \(\texttt{def:observable-coefficient-map}\) with \(\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)\) in place of \(\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)\).

If \(\operatorname{rank}(S_B)=0\), then
\[
S_B^+=0,
\qquad
\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)=\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)=0,
\]
and both \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}\) and \(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\) equal exactly the uncontrolled Horvitz--Thompson estimator \(n^{-1}\mathbf1^{\top}\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}\).  Thus rank zero supplies no control-variate improvement.

For \(r\in\{1,2\}\), suppose
\[
[B]_+\in\mathcal S_{r,n}(\lambda_r,\overline a),
\qquad
\pi_{\min,n}=\min_{i,a}\pi_{i,a},
\]
and define
\[
U_{n,r}=\frac{\sqrt2\,\overline a\,s_n}
{n\lambda_r\pi_{\min,n}^2},
\qquad X_n=\frac{\overline a}{n},
\]
\[
\Gamma_{n,r}=\frac1{\pi_{\min,n}}
\sqrt{\frac{2s_n}{\lambda_r}},
\qquad
T_{n,r}=\frac1n\{\pi_{\min,n}^{-1}
+\overline a\Gamma_{n,r}\},
\]
\[
R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}
=4\mathfrak M_{4,n}U_{n,r}^2X_n^2
+4\mathfrak M_{3,n}T_{n,r}U_{n,r}X_n.
\]
Then, for every fixed bounded potential-outcome array,
\[
\left|\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid Y)
-\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\mid Y)\right|
\le R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\]
The oracle variance is exactly
\[
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\mid Y)
=n^{-2}Y^{\top}\left\{\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
-\Pi^{-1}\Omega B S_B^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\right\}Y.
\]
Under ass:iid-potential-pairs,
\[
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\mid Y)
=\ell_B^+(c_H).
\]

Let \(\widetilde B\) be pilot measurable and independent of the main experiment under ass:pilot-main-outcome-independence.  Suppose there are displayed constants \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2>0\) and \(\overline a<\infty\) such that, almost surely for each \(r\in\{1,2\}\),
\[
\{\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})=r\}
\subseteq
\{[\widetilde B]_+\in\mathcal S_{r,n}(\lambda_r,\overline a)\}.
\tag{S}
\]
Then the preceding finite bound holds conditionally on the pilot and
\[
\left|\mathbb E_P\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_{\widetilde B}^{\mathrm{CV}}
\mid\widetilde B,Y)
-\mathbb E_P\ell_{\widetilde B}^+(c_H)\right|
\le\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf1\{\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})>0\}
R_{n,\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})}^{\mathrm{ad}}
\right].
\]
The encoded CAD pilot output is covered only when its finite spectral-isolation record certifies the lower bound in (S) for its selected positive eigenvalue and an exact coordinate check certifies the displayed envelope \(\overline a\).  Pilot independence alone does not imply (S).

If ass:zhao-dependency-neighborhood-rate and ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate hold, then
\[
s_n\le d_n\le\overline d n^\alpha,
\qquad
\mathfrak M_{4,n}\le64(2n)^2d_n^2,
\qquad
\mathfrak M_{3,n}\le8(2n)d_n^2.
\]
For fixed positive lower bounds on \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\) and fixed \(\overline a\), a finite constant \(K_{\mathrm{ad}}\), independent of the selected basis, its positive rank, the bounded array, and \(H\), satisfies
\[
R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}
\le K_{\mathrm{ad}}
\{n^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
+n^{(7/2)\alpha+3\beta-2}\},
\qquad r\in\{1,2\}.
\]
Under ass:zhao-first-order-rate, \(\sup_{r\in\{1,2\}}nR_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\to0\).  At rank two, \(\mathcal S_{2,n}(\kappa,\overline a)=\iota(\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a})\), and the ordinary-inverse bridge in \(\texttt{lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence}\) is recovered.  Rank one requires the displayed positive-eigenvalue margin and moment/dependency rates.

Writing
\[
S(b_1,b_0)=
\begin{bmatrix}
b_1^{\top}\Omega_{11}b_1&b_1^{\top}\Omega_{10}b_0\\
b_0^{\top}\Omega_{01}b_1&b_0^{\top}\Omega_{00}b_0
\end{bmatrix},
\]
the exact design-side rank certificates are
\[
r_\Omega=2\Longleftrightarrow
\exists b_1,b_0:\det S(b_1,b_0)>0,
\]
\[
r_\Omega\ge1\Longleftrightarrow
\exists a\in\{1,0\},\ \exists b_a:
b_a^{\top}\Omega_{aa}b_a>0,
\]
\[
r_\Omega=1\Longleftrightarrow r_\Omega\ge1
\text{ and }\det S(b_1,b_0)=0\text{ for all }b_1,b_0,
\]
\[
r_\Omega=0\Longleftrightarrow\Omega_{11}=\Omega_{00}=0.
\]
For an individual class, ranks zero, one, and two are certified respectively by \(\operatorname{tr}(S_B)=0\), \(\det(S_B)=0<\operatorname{tr}(S_B)\), and \(\det(S_B)>0\).  No uniform bridge over uncontrolled rank changes, stability at a vanishing positive eigenvalue, or rank-zero gain is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Put \(N=2n\), index exposure coordinates by \(\ell\), and write \(Z_\ell=D_\ell-\pi_\ell\).  Known exposure and the signed convention in \(Y\) give
\[
\mathbb E_\nu\widehat Y_\ell^{\mathrm{obs}}=Y_\ell,
\qquad
\{\Pi^{-1}(\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}-Y)\}_\ell
=\frac{Y_\ell Z_\ell}{\pi_\ell^2}.
\tag{1}
\]
Exposure positivity licenses every division.

If \(S_B=0\), then \(S_B^+=0\).  Since \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B=0\) and \(\Omega\succeq0\), \(\Omega^{1/2}B=0\), hence \(B^{\top}\Omega=0\).  Both observable and oracle coefficients vanish.  Substitution in \(\texttt{def:observable-coefficient-map}\) gives exactly \(n^{-1}\mathbf1^{\top}\widehat Y^{\mathrm{obs}}\), proving every rank-zero assertion.

Fix \(r\in\{1,2\}\) and \([B]_+\in\mathcal S_{r,n}(\lambda_r,\overline a)\).  Set
\[
G_B=\widehat\gamma^{\mathrm{HT}}(B)-\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y).
\]
Equation (1) yields
\[
G_B=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}u_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad
u_{B,\ell}=S_B^+B^{\top}\Omega e_\ell\frac{Y_\ell}{\pi_\ell^2}.
\tag{2}
\]
The positive-eigenvalue margin gives
\[
\lVert S_B^+\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le(n\lambda_r)^{-1}.
\tag{3}
\]
Each covariance column has at most \(s_n\) nonzero entries, every covariance entry has absolute value at most one, and basis coordinates are bounded by \(\overline a\).  Hence
\[
\sup_\ell\lVert B^{\top}\Omega e_\ell\rVert_2
\le\sqrt2\,\overline a s_n,
\qquad
\sup_\ell\lVert u_{B,\ell}\rVert_2\le U_{n,r}.
\tag{4}
\]
The centered basis statistic is
\[
X_B=n^{-1}B^{\top}(D-\mathbb E_\nu D)
=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}x_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad x_{B,\ell}=n^{-1}B^{\top}e_\ell,
\tag{5}
\]
and \(\sup_\ell\lVert x_{B,\ell}\rVert_2\le X_n\).  The fourth-moment envelope in \(\texttt{def:rank-adaptive-stability-strata}\), applied coordinatewise, gives
\[
\mathbb E_\nu\lVert G_B\rVert_2^4
\le4\mathfrak M_{4,n}U_{n,r}^4,
\qquad
\mathbb E_\nu\lVert X_B\rVert_2^4
\le4\mathfrak M_{4,n}X_n^4.
\tag{6}
\]
The estimators satisfy
\[
\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{CV}}
=\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}+\Delta_B,
\qquad \Delta_B=-G_B^{\top}X_B.
\tag{7}
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz and (6) imply
\[
\mathbb E_\nu\Delta_B^2
\le4\mathfrak M_{4,n}U_{n,r}^2X_n^2.
\tag{8}
\]

Put \(T_B=\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}-\tau\).  Equation (1) makes the Horvitz--Thompson term unbiased, and the basis statistic is centered, so \(\mathbb E_\nu T_B=0\).  Direct expansion gives
\[
T_B=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}t_{B,\ell}Z_\ell,
\qquad
t_{B,\ell}=n^{-1}\left\{\frac{Y_\ell}{\pi_\ell}
-B_{\ell\cdot}\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)\right\}.
\tag{9}
\]
The projector identity in \(\texttt{lem:rank-stratified-projector-reduction}\) gives
\[
\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)^{\top}S_B\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)
\le Y^{\top}\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y.
\tag{10}
\]
There are at most \(2ns_n\) nonzero entries in the covariance sum, and every term is at most \(\pi_{\min,n}^{-2}\) in absolute value.  Thus
\[
Y^{\top}\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y
\le2ns_n\pi_{\min,n}^{-2}.
\tag{11}
\]
The coefficient lies in the positive spectral subspace of \(S_B\).  Combining (10)--(11) with \(\lambda_+(S_B)\ge n\lambda_r\) yields
\[
\lVert\gamma_B^{*,+}(Y)\rVert_2\le\Gamma_{n,r},
\qquad
\sup_\ell|t_{B,\ell}|\le T_{n,r}.
\tag{12}
\]
Expanding \(\operatorname{Cov}_\nu(T_B,\Delta_B\mid Y)\) in the two coefficient coordinates and applying the third-moment envelope gives
\[
|\operatorname{Cov}_\nu(T_B,\Delta_B\mid Y)|
\le2\mathfrak M_{3,n}T_{n,r}U_{n,r}X_n.
\tag{13}
\]
The variance identity combines (8) and twice (13) to give exactly \(R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\).

For the oracle identity, put \(q_B=B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}Y\).  Since
\[
q_B\in\operatorname{range}\{(\Omega^{1/2}B)^{\top}\}
=\operatorname{range}(S_B),
\]
one has \(S_BS_B^+q_B=q_B\).  Completing the quadratic variance expression gives
\[
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_B^{\mathrm{or},+}\mid Y)
=n^{-2}Y^{\top}\left\{\Pi^{-1}\Omega\Pi^{-1}
-\Pi^{-1}\Omega BS_B^+B^{\top}\Omega\Pi^{-1}\right\}Y.
\tag{14}
\]
Under the i.i.d. potential-pair assumption, \(\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}(YY^{\top})=Q(c_H)\).  Taking the trace expectation in (14) proves \(\ell_B^+(c_H)\).

Now let \(\widetilde B\) satisfy (S).  Pilot independence permits conditioning on its realized class.  On rank zero the difference is exactly zero.  On \(\{\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})=r\}\), (S) licenses the fixed-basis calculation with \(\lambda_r\) and \(\overline a\).  Integrating the eventwise inequalities over the pilot gives
\[
\left|\mathbb E_P\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_{\widetilde B}^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid\widetilde B,Y)
-\mathbb E_P\ell_{\widetilde B}^+(c_H)\right|
\le\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf1\{\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})>0\}
R_{n,\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widetilde B})}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right].
\tag{15}
\]
For an encoded CAD output, the rank and spectral-isolation record and the coordinate check must establish (S); pilot independence alone does not.

Under the directed local assumption, the same singleton-versus-complement count used in the preceding uniform proof gives
\[
s_n\le d_n\le\overline d n^\alpha,
\qquad
\mathfrak M_{4,n}\le64(2n)^2d_n^2,
\qquad
\mathfrak M_{3,n}\le8(2n)d_n^2.
\tag{16}
\]
Substitution of (16) and \(\pi_{\min,n}\ge\underline\pi n^{-\beta}\) into the displayed formula for \(R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\) gives a finite \(K_{\mathrm{ad}}\) such that
\[
R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}
\le K_{\mathrm{ad}}\{n^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
+n^{(7/2)\alpha+3\beta-2}\},
\qquad r\in\{1,2\}.
\tag{17}
\]
The constant is uniform in the basis, its positive rank, the bounded array, and \(H\), for fixed positive lower bounds on \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2\) and fixed \(\overline a\).  Since \((7/2)\alpha+3\beta\le4\alpha+4\beta\), the first-order rate assumption gives \(\sup_r nR_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\to0\).  At rank two the definitions give \(\mathcal S_{2,n}(\kappa,\overline a)=\iota(\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a})\), and the pseudoinverse is the ordinary inverse.

Finally, every \(S(b_1,b_0)\) is a \(2\times2\) positive-semidefinite matrix.  It has rank two exactly when its determinant is positive, rank one exactly when its determinant is zero and trace positive, and rank zero exactly when its trace is zero.  Taking existential maxima proves the first three design-side equivalences.  If \(\Omega_{11}=\Omega_{00}=0\), positive semidefiniteness forces \(\Omega_{10}=\Omega_{01}=0\), so \(r_\Omega=0\); conversely, a nonzero positive-semidefinite diagonal block has a vector with positive quadratic form.  This proves the last design-side equivalence and the individual rank tests. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The theorem gives the exact rank-zero identity and a finite observable-to-oracle variance bound on certified stable rank-one and rank-two strata, with rates proved from Zhao's directed local neighborhoods and explicit constants 8 and 64. \textit{Gap.} A pilot-selected positive-rank class is covered only after its spectral-isolation record and coordinate check certify a positive-eigenvalue margin and envelope; pilot independence does not imply either condition, and no stability across uncontrolled rank changes is claimed. \textit{Consumer.} It converts full-carrier objective performance into ex ante conditional design-variance performance only on certificate-verified rank strata.

\begin{theorem}[thm:cad-primitive-containment-error-gap-certificate]\label{thm:cad-primitive-containment-error-gap-certificate}
Let \(\mathcal K=\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\), let \(\mathcal Z\) be the normalized positive-definite rank-two carrier, and put \(a(B)=g_B(0)\),
\[
a^\star=\max_{B\in\mathcal K}a(B),
\quad A_0=\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal K}a(B),
\quad v=\min_{B\in\mathcal K}w(B),
\quad A_{\mathrm{mm}}=\operatorname*{argmin}_{B\in\mathcal K}w(B),
\quad q=\min_{B\in A_0}w(B).
\]
Fix the finite signed-pivot charts and their semialgebraic quotient distance \(d_{\mathrm{pr}}\).  A primitive geometry record \(\mathscr G_{\mathrm{CAD}}\) consists only of finite rational or real-algebraic CAD signs, KKT data, derivative bounds, and interval remainders as follows.

First, it certifies numbers \(\zeta_0,\zeta_{\mathrm{mm}}>0\) such that
\[
\sup_{B\in\mathcal Z\setminus\mathcal K}a(B)
\le a^\star-\zeta_0,
\qquad
\inf_{B\in\mathcal Z\setminus\mathcal K}w(B)
\ge v+\zeta_{\mathrm{mm}}.
\tag{G1}
\]

Second, it gives a finite semialgebraic stratification \(A_0=\bigcup_j V_j\).  On every signed-pivot chart meeting a stratum \(V_j\), it supplies: exact active constraints; nominal KKT multipliers; linear independence of active gradients; strictly positive multipliers for active inequalities; a certified tubular chart whose derivative splits feasible displacements into directions tangent to \(V_j\), feasible tangent directions transverse to \(V_j\), and active-normal coordinates; lower singular-value bounds for the transverse and normal coordinate map; and interval bounds for its first two derivatives and remainders.  The nominal Lagrangian Hessian is required to be strictly negative only on the feasible tangent directions transverse to \(V_j\), with a certified eigenvalue bounded above by a negative algebraic number.  No curvature is required along \(T_CV_j\), where \(a\) is constant.

Put \(A_q=\{C\in A_0:w(C)=q\}\).  After replacing the finite stratification by a common semialgebraic refinement, the record certifies a relative semialgebraic neighborhood \(N_q\) of \(A_q\) in \(A_0\) that is a union of strata.  On every stratum contained in \(N_q\), it supplies exact weights \(\theta_L(C),\theta_U(C)\ge0\), summing to one and supported only on endpoint losses attaining \(w(C)\), and exact KKT normal data proving that the first feasible directional derivative of \(\theta_L(C)\ell_B(c_L)+\theta_U(C)\ell_B(c_U)\) is nonnegative.  It also supplies uniform interval bounds on the endpoint Hessians and Taylor remainders.  On the compact remainder \(A_0\setminus N_q\), it certifies a strictly positive \(w-q\) gap.  These are primitive finite checks; neither quadratic growth nor a global comparison inequality is included in the record.

Third, after the local tube radius is derived from those derivative bounds, CAD certifies on its compact complement constants \(\gamma>0\) and \(R<\infty\) satisfying
\[
a^\star-a(B)\ge\gamma,
\qquad [q-w(B)]_+\le R.
\tag{G2}
\]
Finally, the record contains an exactly feasible algebraic witness \(B_R\in\mathcal K\) and \(\zeta_\Delta>0\) with
\[
w(B_R)\le q-\zeta_\Delta.
\tag{G3}
\]

Every valid \(\mathscr G_{\mathrm{CAD}}\) derives constants \(\rho,\mu>0\) and \(M<\infty\) for
\[
a^\star-a(B)\ge\mu d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)^2,
\qquad
[q-w(B)]_+\le M d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)^2
\quad\text{if }d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)\le\rho,
\tag{G4}
\]
proves
\[
\mathcal A_{0,Z}=A_0,
\qquad \mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm},Z}=A_{\mathrm{mm}},
\qquad q-v\ge\zeta_\Delta>0,
\tag{G5}
\]
and derives the global comparison
\[
[q-w(B)]_+
\le L\{a^\star-a(B)\}
\quad(B\in\mathcal K),
\qquad L=\max\{M/\mu,R/\gamma\}.
\tag{G6}
\]

Consequently, if
\[
e=\sup_{\mathcal K}|\widehat g_B(0)-g_B(0)|,
\qquad d=\sup_{\mathcal K}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|,
\]
then any empirical nominal maximizer \(\widehat B_0\) and robust minimizer \(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}}\) satisfy
\[
w(\widehat B_0)\ge q-2Le,
\qquad w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})\le v+2d.
\]
Define \(V_0\) and \(V_{\mathrm{mm}}\) to be the observable ex ante conditional design-variance performances of these two selected bases.  If
\[
|V_0-w(\widehat B_0)|\le R_0,
\qquad
|V_{\mathrm{mm}}-w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})|\le R_{\mathrm{mm}},
\]
where the errors may be supplied by thm:rank-adaptive-oracle-observable-variance-bridge only on its certified stability strata, then
\[
V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}}
\ge(q-v)-2Le-2d-R_0-R_{\mathrm{mm}}.
\tag{G7}
\]
Thus containment, local growth, the global comparison constant, and the positive robust gap are derived from primitive finite certificates rather than assumed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
All arguments take place in the finite signed-pivot charts fixed by the certificate.  The normalized class \(\mathcal K=\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\) is nonempty and compact, and \(a\), the two endpoint losses, and \(w\) are continuous and semialgebraic on it because \(S_B\succeq n\kappa I_2\).  Therefore \(a^\star,v,q\) are attained and \(A_0,A_{\mathrm{mm}},A_q\) are nonempty compact semialgebraic sets.

\emph{Containment and the positive gap.}
The normalized positive-definite carrier is \(\mathcal Z\), with \(\mathcal K\subseteq\mathcal Z\).  The first inequality in (G1) says that no point of \(\mathcal Z\setminus\mathcal K\) can attain \(a^\star\); hence every global ordinary-domain nominal optimizer lies in \(\mathcal K\), and within \(\mathcal K\) its equality set is \(A_0\).  Thus
\[
\mathcal A_{0,Z}=A_0.
\tag{1}
\]
The second inequality in (G1) says that no point of \(\mathcal Z\setminus\mathcal K\) can attain \(v\).  Consequently
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm},Z}=A_{\mathrm{mm}}.
\tag{2}
\]
The feasible witness in (G3) gives
\[
v\le w(B_R)\le q-\zeta_\Delta,
\qquad\text{so}\qquad
q-v\ge\zeta_\Delta>0.
\tag{3}
\]
This proves (G5) directly from the outside gaps and the witness.

\emph{Nominal transverse quadratic growth.}
Fix a stratum \(V_j\) and one of its certified tubular charts.  Write a feasible point near \(C\in V_j\) in the certified coordinates as
\[
B=\Psi_C(s,z,r),
\tag{4}
\]
where \(s\) is tangent to \(V_j\), \(z\) is feasible tangent and transverse to \(V_j\), and \(r\) is the active-normal coordinate.  The record's lower singular-value bounds provide constants \(c_1,c_2>0\), uniform over its finite chart list, for which
\[
c_1(\lVert z\rVert+\lVert r\rVert)
\le d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)
\le c_2(\lVert z\rVert+\lVert r\rVert)
\tag{5}
\]
after shrinking the tubes.  Motion in \(s\) changes the nearest point within \(V_j\) and therefore does not enter the distance.

The nominal KKT equations make the first derivative vanish in \(z\).  The certified transverse Lagrangian Hessian bound gives a number \(\lambda_j>0\) such that the second-order contribution to \(a^\star-a(B)\) is at least \(\lambda_j\lVert z\rVert^2\).  Linear independence and strict complementarity give a number \(\chi_j>0\) such that the feasible active-normal contribution is at least \(\chi_j\lVert r\rVert\).  The certified derivative and remainder bounds give \(C_j<\infty\) and a tube radius \(\rho_j>0\) for which Taylor's formula yields
\[
a^\star-a(B)
\ge\lambda_j\lVert z\rVert^2+\chi_j\lVert r\rVert
-C_j(\lVert z\rVert+\lVert r\rVert)^3.
\tag{6}
\]
Choose \(\rho_j\) smaller, if necessary, so the remainder is at most one half of the first two terms.  Because \(\lVert r\rVert\le\rho_j\), the linear normal term also satisfies
\(\chi_j\lVert r\rVert\ge(\chi_j/\rho_j)\lVert r\rVert^2\).  Hence there is \(\mu_j>0\) such that
\[
a^\star-a(B)\ge\mu_j d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)^2
\tag{7}
\]
throughout that tube, by (5).  Taking the minimum of the positive constants and radii over the finite refined chart and stratum list gives \(\rho>0\) and \(\mu>0\) for the first inequality in (G4).  Thus quadratic growth has been derived from KKT, transverse Hessian, strict-complementarity, and remainder data; it was not placed in the record.

\emph{Quadratic control of the robust deficit.}
For \(C\in N_q\), let
\[
\phi_C(B)=\theta_L(C)\ell_B(c_L)+\theta_U(C)\ell_B(c_U).
\tag{8}
\]
The weights are nonnegative and sum to one, so
\[
\phi_C(B)\le w(B)
\quad\text{for every }B.
\tag{9}
\]
Because weights are supported only on endpoint losses active at \(C\),
\[
\phi_C(C)=w(C)\ge q.
\tag{10}
\]
The certified KKT normal data state that the first feasible directional derivative of \(\phi_C\) at \(C\) is nonnegative.  Its uniform endpoint-Hessian and Taylor-remainder bounds therefore give \(M_0<\infty\), uniformly over the finitely many strata in \(N_q\), such that
\[
\phi_C(B)\ge\phi_C(C)-M_0d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,C)^2
\tag{11}
\]
in a sufficiently small certified tube.  Equations (9)--(11) imply
\[
[q-w(B)]_+\le M_0d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,C)^2.
\tag{12}
\]
Choose \(C\) as a chartwise nearest point in \(A_0\); the finite-chart definition of \(d_{\mathrm{pr}}\) makes the right side in (12) \(M_0d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)^2\).

On the compact remainder \(A_0\setminus N_q\), the certificate gives a positive gap \(w(C)-q\ge\chi_q>0\).  Uniform continuity and the interval Taylor bounds permit a common smaller tube in which \(w(B)\ge q\); there the positive part in (12) is zero.  Combining the two cases and again taking the maximum over finitely many bounds gives \(M<\infty\) such that
\[
[q-w(B)]_+\le Md_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)^2
\quad\text{whenever }d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)\le\rho.
\tag{13}
\]
Together, (7) and (13) prove (G4).

\emph{Global comparison.}
If \(d_{\mathrm{pr}}(B,A_0)\le\rho\), dividing (13) by (7) gives
\[
[q-w(B)]_+\le\frac M\mu\{a^\star-a(B)\}.
\tag{14}
\]
On the compact complement of this tube, (G2) gives
\[
[q-w(B)]_+\le R
\le\frac R\gamma\{a^\star-a(B)\}.
\tag{15}
\]
With \(L=\max\{M/\mu,R/\gamma\}\), equations (14)--(15) prove (G6) on all of \(\mathcal K\).

\emph{Plug-in and observable consequences.}
Let \(\widehat B_0\) maximize the empirical nominal gain.  If \(B_0\in A_0\), empirical optimality and the definition of \(e\) give
\[
a^\star-a(\widehat B_0)
\le |a(B_0)-\widehat a(B_0)|
+|\widehat a(\widehat B_0)-a(\widehat B_0)|
\le2e.
\tag{16}
\]
Applying (G6) to (16) yields
\[
w(\widehat B_0)\ge q-2Le.
\tag{17}
\]
Similarly, if \(B^\star\in A_{\mathrm{mm}}\), empirical optimality and the definition of \(d\) give
\[
w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})
\le\widehat w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})+d
\le\widehat w(B^\star)+d
\le v+2d.
\tag{18}
\]
Finally, the two supplied observable bridge inequalities imply
\[
V_0\ge w(\widehat B_0)-R_0,
\qquad
V_{\mathrm{mm}}\le w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})+R_{\mathrm{mm}}.
\tag{19}
\]
Subtracting (19) and using (17)--(18) proves
\[
V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}}
\ge(q-v)-2Le-2d-R_0-R_{\mathrm{mm}},
\]
which is (G7).  Every constant used above is obtained by a minimum or maximum of finitely many exact algebraic signs, singular-value bounds, interval derivative bounds, and compact-complement CAD bounds in \(\mathscr G_{\mathrm{CAD}}\).  Thus all stated consequences are derived from the primitive record. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} It derives optimizer containment, transverse quadratic growth, the global nominal-to-robust error constant, and a positive gap from finite auditable CAD, KKT, derivative, and remainder records. \textit{Gap.} The proposed record requires a refined stratum cover of the local neighborhood; any observable-variance use must separately satisfy the rank-adaptive bridge's spectral and coordinate certificates. \textit{Consumer.} Certified pilot comparisons can replace bare containment and bounded-error premises with a locally checkable CAD/KKT record.

\begin{theorem}[thm:cad-certified-positive-improvement-nonvacuity]\label{thm:cad-certified-positive-improvement-nonvacuity}
For the exact triangle instance in \(\texttt{thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate}\), take \(\kappa=1/8\), \(\overline a=2\), and define
\[
x=\sqrt{\frac32}(1,-1,0)^{\top},
\qquad
y_0=\frac1{\sqrt2}(1,1,-2)^{\top},
\]
\[
t=\frac{\sqrt{3410}}{110}+\frac{2\sqrt{330}}{55},
\qquad
u=-\frac{\sqrt{3410}}{55}+\frac{2\sqrt{330}}{55},
\qquad
y_R=(t,t,u)^{\top}.
\]
Let \(B_0=B(x,y_0)\) and \(B_R=B(x,y_R)\).  Exact CAD sign and equality certificates verify
\[
B_0\in\mathcal A_0,
\qquad
B_R\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}},
\qquad
\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_{B_0})
=\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_{B_R})=\frac4{27}>\frac18.
\]
Take the realized unpaired pilot samples in both arms to be \((0,1)\).  Then the empirical marginals, endpoint matrices, and population endpoint matrices agree exactly, so the two pilot objective errors are zero and the input is rational or real algebraic.

For the observable estimator in \(\texttt{def:observable-coefficient-map}\), exact enumeration of all eight assignments and all eight endpoint potential-outcome arrays gives
\[
V_{0,L}=\frac{3112847}{480000},
\qquad
V_{0,U}=\frac{1019349}{160000},
\]
\[
V_{R,L}
=\frac{948747698543}{133309920000}
-\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000},
\]
\[
V_{R,U}
=\frac{304083944181}{44436640000}
-\frac{8821467\sqrt{93}}{44795000}.
\]
The lower covariance endpoint is worst for both displayed bases.  Hence the observable robust-versus-zero variance gap is
\[
G_{\mathrm{obs}}
=V_{0,L}-V_{R,L}
=-\frac{87728973}{138864500}
+\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000}>0.
\]
The exact objective gap is
\[
\delta=\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{4912}{39525},
\]
and the exact contrast-level observable-versus-oracle bridge correction is
\[
E_{\mathrm{bridge}}
=\left|
\{V_{0,L}-521/1200\}
-\{V_{R,L}-2613/8432\}
\right|
=-\frac{314959399}{416593500}
+\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000}>0.
\]
Therefore
\[
G_{\mathrm{obs}}
=\delta+E_{\mathrm{bridge}}
>E_{\mathrm{bridge}}+0,
\]
so this exact lawful encoded instance has a positive observable robust-versus-zero gap strictly exceeding the displayed contrast bridge correction plus the zero pilot error.  This is a nonvacuity witness for one certified pair of tie representatives.  It does not assert universal strict dominance, improvement for every tie selection, full-carrier attainment in general, zero-tolerance selection, cross-rank optimizer convergence, an SDP reduction, or stability at an uncontrolled singular rank.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
The displayed scalar is \(u\), while \(\nu\) remains the assignment law.  Direct radical algebra gives
\[
\lVert x\rVert_2^2=\lVert y_0\rVert_2^2=\lVert y_R\rVert_2^2=3,
\qquad
\mathbf1^{\top}x=0,
\qquad
x^{\top}y_R=0,
\tag{1}
\]
and, more explicitly,
\[
2t^2+u^2=3,
\qquad
(2t+u)^2=\frac{216}{55}.
\tag{2}
\]
Also \(\mathbf1^{\top}y_0=x^{\top}y_0=0\), and every coordinate of \(x,y_0,y_R\) has absolute value below \(2\).  The complete equality sets in \(\texttt{thm:triangle-completion-separation-certificate}\) therefore give
\[
B_0\in\mathcal A_0,
\qquad
B_R\in\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm}},
\qquad
\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_{B_0})
=\lambda_{\min}(3^{-1}S_{B_R})=\frac4{27}>\frac18.
\tag{3}
\]
These are polynomial equalities and strict algebraic signs, so the stated CAD interface checks them exactly.

The empirical distribution of the pilot sample \((0,1)\) is \(\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2)\) in each arm.  Hence its means, variances, countermonotone and comonotone covariance endpoints, and both endpoint matrices equal the population quantities.  The two uniform pilot-objective errors are exactly zero, and every input is rational or real algebraic.

For \(e\in\{L,U\}\) and \(z\in\{0,1\}^3\), define endpoint arrays by
\[
Y_i^{(e,z)}(1)=z_i,
\qquad
Y_i^{(U,z)}(0)=z_i,
\qquad
Y_i^{(L,z)}(0)=1-z_i.
\tag{4}
\]
Potential outcomes at the residual label \(\star\) are irrelevant because both focal indicators vanish there.  Let \(T_B(w,z,e)\) be the observable estimator in \(\texttt{def:observable-coefficient-map}\), evaluated at assignment \(w\), array (4), the triangle matrices, and basis \(B\).  The endpoint performance is the finite algebraic sum
\[
V_{B,e}=\frac18\sum_{z\in\{0,1\}^3}
\left[
\sum_{w\in\{0,1\}^3}\nu(w)T_B(w,z,e)^2
-\left\{\sum_{w\in\{0,1\}^3}\nu(w)T_B(w,z,e)\right\}^2
\right].
\tag{5}
\]
There are sixty-four algebraic summands for each variance.  Exact substitution and common-denominator reduction give
\[
V_{0,L}=\frac{3112847}{480000},
\qquad
V_{0,U}=\frac{1019349}{160000},
\tag{6}
\]
\[
V_{R,L}=\frac{948747698543}{133309920000}
-\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000},
\tag{7}
\]
\[
V_{R,U}=\frac{304083944181}{44436640000}
-\frac{8821467\sqrt{93}}{44795000}.
\tag{8}
\]
Two exact checks are
\[
V_{0,L}-V_{0,U}=\frac{137}{1200}>0,
\tag{9}
\]
\[
V_{R,L}-V_{R,U}
=\frac{2943215-196776\sqrt{93}}{10750800}>0.
\tag{10}
\]
The last sign follows already from \(\sqrt{93}<10\).  Thus the lower covariance endpoint is worst for both bases.

Subtracting (7) from (6) gives
\[
G_{\mathrm{obs}}
=-\frac{87728973}{138864500}
+\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000}>0,
\tag{11}
\]
where positivity follows from \(\sqrt{93}>9\) and exact cross multiplication.  The triangle separation theorem gives
\[
\delta=\frac{521}{1200}-\frac{2613}{8432}
=\frac{4912}{39525}.
\tag{12}
\]
Using the same objective values, the signed expression inside the asserted absolute value reduces to the positive number
\[
E_{\mathrm{bridge}}
=-\frac{314959399}{416593500}
+\frac{9641367\sqrt{93}}{44795000}>0.
\tag{13}
\]
Exact subtraction of (13) from (11) yields
\[
G_{\mathrm{obs}}-E_{\mathrm{bridge}}
=\frac{4912}{39525}=\delta.
\tag{14}
\]
Therefore \(G_{\mathrm{obs}}=\delta+E_{\mathrm{bridge}}>E_{\mathrm{bridge}}+0\), the final zero being the exact pilot error.  Both bases have certified rank two, positive-eigenvalue margin, and coordinate envelope by (1)--(3), so the rank-adaptive bridge applies; the direct finite enumeration proves the stronger exact displayed comparison.  The conclusion remains a witness for the displayed tie representatives only. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The exact triangle enumeration shows that the certified machinery can produce a strictly positive observable robust-versus-surrogate variance gap. \textit{Gap.} The witness is existential over one displayed pair of tie representatives and is not a universal tie-breaking or neighborhood result. \textit{Consumer.} It supplies a finite lawful stress test for the observable deployment claim.

\begin{theorem}[thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer]\label{thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer}
Suppose ass:known-exposure and ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility hold.  Under the assumptions of lem:uniform-pilot-endpoint-matrix-rate and lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence, suppose ass:pilot-main-outcome-independence holds, and use the independent-pilot selectors of def:feasible-ht-pilot-selected-estimator.  Put
\[
M_n=\max_{e\in\{L,U\}}\lVert\widehat Q_e-Q(c_e)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},
\]
where \(C_n\) is exactly the deterministic sensitivity coefficient defined in lem:uniform-pilot-endpoint-matrix-rate, and assume along the triangular sequence that
\[
nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\longrightarrow0.
\]
Put also
\[
d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}=\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|,
\qquad
e_n=\sup_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}|\widehat g_B(0)-g_B(0)|.
\]
For every \(n\), almost surely under the pilot law,
\[
d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}\le C_nM_n,
\qquad e_n\le2C_nM_n.
\]
Write
\[
v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}=\min_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}w(B),
\qquad
\mathcal A_{0,\overline a}=\operatorname*{argmax}_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}g_B(0).
\]
Then the robust selector obeys the finite pilot-regret inequality
\[
0\le
\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})-v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}
\le2C_n\mathbb E_PM_n.
\]
Let \(R_n\) be the deterministic finite-\(n\) envelope in lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence, and, for \(s\in\{\mathrm{mm},0\}\), define
\[
V_s=
\mathbb E_P\left[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu
(\widehat\tau_{\widehat B_s}^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid\widehat B_s,Y)
\right].
\]
The feasible-estimator bridge is the finite bound
\[
|V_s-\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_s)|\le R_n,
\qquad s\in\{\mathrm{mm},0\}.
\]
Under \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\),
\[
\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})
=v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}+o(n^{-1}),
\qquad
V_s=\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_s)+o(n^{-1}).
\]
In addition, define
\[
g_n^\star=\max_{B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}}g_B(0),
\qquad
q_n=\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{0,\overline a}}w(B).
\]
If there are deterministic \(L_n\in[0,\infty)\) with \(\sup_nL_n<\infty\) such that, for every \(B\in\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\),
\[
[q_n-w(B)]_+\le L_n\{g_n^\star-g_B(0)\},
\]
then the finite zero-surrogate inequality
\[
q_n-\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_0)
\le4L_nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n
\]
holds, and consequently
\[
\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_0)
\ge\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{0,\overline a}}w(B)-o(n^{-1}).
\]
The linear error bound is a deterministic geometric property of the two ordinary-domain objective surfaces.  Without it, ordinary outer argmax consistency alone is only \(o(1)\) and does not justify a first-order \(o(n^{-1})\) ordering.  These selectors and limits are secondary regular rank-two deployment results.  No primitive condition in this theorem proves existence of uniformly bounded \(L_n\); the zero-surrogate first-order conclusion is conditional on the displayed global linear error bound.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Write \(\mathcal K_n=\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}\).  Coordinate-bounded feasibility makes \(\mathcal K_n\) nonempty.  Normalization and the coordinate envelope make it compact, while \(S_B\succeq n\kappa I_2\) makes inversion and all population and empirical criteria continuous.  Hence both optimizer sets are nonempty.  The fixed finite-chart lexicographic rule is pilot measurable: the empirical endpoints are Borel functions of finitely many order statistics, the objective is jointly Borel in the pilot and continuous in \(B\), and successive lexicographic coordinate minima are limits of infima over compact near-optimizer sets.  Thus both selectors exist and are measurable.

Let
\[
d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}=\sup_{B\in\mathcal K_n}|\widehat w(B)-w(B)|,
\qquad
e_n=\sup_{B\in\mathcal K_n}|\widehat g_B(0)-g_B(0)|.
\]
The uniform pilot lemma gives pathwise
\[
d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}\le C_nM_n,
\qquad e_n\le2C_nM_n.
\tag{1}
\]
Choose \(B_n^\star\in\operatorname*{argmin}_{\mathcal K_n}w\).  Empirical optimality gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})
&\le\widehat w(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})+d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}\\
&\le\widehat w(B_n^\star)+d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}\\
&\le v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}+2d_n^{\mathrm{pl}}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{2}
\]
The population minimum gives the reverse inequality.  Taking pilot expectations proves
\[
0\le\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})-v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}
\le2C_n\mathbb E_PM_n.
\tag{3}
\]

Conditional on the independent pilot, \(\widehat B_s\) is fixed in \(\mathcal K_n\).  The uniform observable-to-oracle lemma applies, and the sharp covariance-domain equality together with \(\texttt{thm:fixed-basis-endpoints}\) gives
\[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}\ell_B(c_H)=w(B).
\tag{4}
\]
Using \(|\sup_Hu_H-\sup_Hv_H|\le\sup_H|u_H-v_H|\) and integrating over the pilot yields
\[
|V_s-\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_s)|\le R_n,
\qquad s\in\{\mathrm{mm},0\}.
\tag{5}
\]
The exact premise \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\) makes the right side of (3) \(o(n^{-1})\), while the first-order design-rate assumption gives \(nR_n\to0\).  Equations (3) and (5) prove the two first-order conclusions.

For the nominal selector, choose \(B_n^0\in\mathcal A_{0,\overline a}\).  Inserting and subtracting empirical gains and using empirical optimality gives
\[
g_n^\star-g_{\widehat B_0}(0)\le2e_n\le4C_nM_n.
\tag{6}
\]
The displayed deterministic geometric inequality then implies
\[
[q_n-w(\widehat B_0)]_+\le4L_nC_nM_n.
\tag{7}
\]
Since \(x\ge q-[q-x]_+\), taking expectations gives
\[
q_n-\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_0)\le4L_nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n.
\tag{8}
\]
Boundedness of \(L_n\) and the exact sensitivity premise make (8) \(o(n^{-1})\).  The proof uses the global linear error bound only for this nominal first-order statement and does not infer it from outer argmax consistency. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This theorem combines uniform pilot endpoint control with the proved directed-local regular rank-two bridge to obtain finite selection regret and first-order feasible-estimator variance transfer. \textit{Gap.} The proposed notation keeps the plug-in error distinct from neighborhood size.  The zero-surrogate comparison still needs the displayed bounded deterministic geometry constant and remains restricted to an external independent pilot. \textit{Consumer.} prop:ordinary-domain-feasible-ht-gap-transfer

\begin{proposition}[prop:ordinary-domain-feasible-ht-gap-transfer]\label{prop:ordinary-domain-feasible-ht-gap-transfer}
Assume ass:known-exposure and ass:coordinate-bounded-kappa-feasibility and the conditions of thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer, including its exact hypothesis \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\) and its deterministic linear error bound for the zero-surrogate argmax.  Suppose the attained ordinary-domain optimizer sets satisfy
\[
\mathcal A_{\mathrm{mm},Z}\cup\mathcal A_{0,Z}
\subseteq\mathcal B_{\kappa,\overline a}.
\]
With \(M_n,C_n,L_n,R_n\) as in that theorem and lem:uniform-zhao-ht-oracle-variance-equivalence, define, for \(s\in\{\mathrm{mm},0\}\),
\[
V_s=
\mathbb E_P\left[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu
(\widehat\tau_{\widehat B_s}^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid\widehat B_s,Y)
\right].
\]
Then the finite-\(n\) end-to-end lower bound is
\[
V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}}
\ge
\Delta_Z-(4L_n+2)C_n\mathbb E_PM_n-2R_n.
\]
In particular, if explicit finite constants \(K_M,K_C,K_R\), independent of \(n\), certify
\[
\mathbb E_PM_n\le K_M\frac{n}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}},
\qquad
C_n\le K_Cn^{\beta-1},
\qquad
R_n\le K_Rn^{4\alpha+4\beta-2},
\]
then the directly checkable condition
\[
\Delta_Z>
(4L_n+2)K_CK_M
\frac{n^{\beta}}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}
+2K_Rn^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}
\]
implies \(V_0>V_{\mathrm{mm}}\) at that finite \(n\).  If, in addition,
\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}n\Delta_Z>0,
\]
then
\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}n(V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}})>0.
\]
Thus a positive first-order ordinary-domain intercept--slope gap transfers to the feasible independent-pilot estimator, with an explicit finite-\(n\) diagnostic and the sharpened pilot term.  The conclusion concerns ex ante expected conditional design variance; it is neither a superpopulation sampling variance nor a Wald limit law.  Neither the optimizer-containment condition nor a primitive theorem yielding uniformly bounded \(L_n\) is proved here; both are separate checkable hypotheses.  The proposition concerns the positive-definite rank-two restriction only.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Put
\[
v_n=\inf_{B\in\mathcal B_Z}w(B),
\qquad q_n=\inf_{B\in\mathcal A_{0,Z}}w(B).
\tag{1}
\]
Attainment and the asserted containment imply
\[
v_{\mathrm{mm},\overline a}=v_n,
\qquad
\mathcal A_{0,\overline a}=\mathcal A_{0,Z},
\qquad
\Delta_Z=q_n-v_n.
\tag{2}
\]
Indeed, each global optimizer is feasible in the smaller class, while any smaller-class point attaining the same global value is itself a global optimizer.  The robust regret and nominal lower bound in \(\texttt{thm:pilot-selected-ht-first-order-variance-transfer}\) therefore give
\[
\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_0)-\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})
\ge\Delta_Z-(4L_n+2)C_n\mathbb E_PM_n.
\tag{3}
\]
The two directions of the feasible-estimator bridge give
\[
V_0\ge\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_0)-R_n,
\qquad
V_{\mathrm{mm}}\le\mathbb E_Pw(\widehat B_{\mathrm{mm}})+R_n.
\tag{4}
\]
Subtracting proves
\[
V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}}
\ge\Delta_Z-(4L_n+2)C_n\mathbb E_PM_n-2R_n.
\tag{5}
\]
The explicit deterministic envelopes imply
\[
C_n\mathbb E_PM_n\le K_CK_M\frac{n^\beta}{\sqrt{m_1\wedge m_0}}.
\tag{6}
\]
Substitution of (6) and \(R_n\le K_Rn^{4\alpha+4\beta-2}\) in (5) gives the stated finite check, whose strict satisfaction implies \(V_0>V_{\mathrm{mm}}\).  Finally, multiplying (5) by \(n\), using \(nC_n\mathbb E_PM_n\to0\), boundedness of \(L_n\), and \(nR_n\to0\), gives
\[
\liminf_{n\to\infty}n(V_0-V_{\mathrm{mm}})
\ge\liminf_{n\to\infty}n\Delta_Z>0.
\]
Every variance here is an ex ante expected conditional design variance, not a superpopulation sampling variance or a Wald limit law. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The proposition transfers an attained ordinary-domain population gap to a finite-pilot observable Horvitz--Thompson-coefficient variance gap using explicit pilot and directed-local bridge remainders. \textit{Gap.} Optimizer containment, the exact pilot sensitivity premise, and a uniformly bounded geometry error constant remain separate hypotheses; the fixed triangle witness alone does not supply a triangular-sequence theorem. \textit{Consumer.} It is the operational finite-n design-side diagnostic for independent-pilot positive-definite rank-two deployment.

\begin{theorem}[thm:encoded-cad-stable-pilot-selector-certificate]\label{thm:encoded-cad-stable-pilot-selector-certificate}
Suppose ass:exposure-positivity holds, \(\lambda_{1,n}>0\), \(\lambda_{2,n}>0\), and \(\overline a\ge1\), and let \(\widehat{\mathfrak I}_{m,\mathrm{alg}}^{\mathrm{st},+}\) be the augmented algebraic input of def:encoded-cad-pilot-selector with exactly encoded \(\varepsilon_m>0\).  The constrained rank-stratified cylindrical-algebraic procedure terminates and returns \(\widehat{\mathscr K}_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD,st},+}\).  Its algebraic basis component lies in \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) and satisfies
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD,st},+})
\le
\min_{[B]_+\in\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon_m.
\]
The record exactly certifies the selected rank, every coordinate-envelope inequality, the applicable positive-eigenvalue margin, and rational isolating intervals with multiplicities for the positive eigenvalues.  It also gives
\[
0\le\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}
\le\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}},
\qquad
\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
-\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
\le\varepsilon_m.
\]
No agreement with either countable selector, zero-tolerance selection, uniform complexity bound, or finite arbitrary-real algorithm is asserted.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
All coefficients in the augmented input are rational or real algebraic.  Exposure positivity makes \(\Pi^{-1}\) algebraic and well defined.  On a normalized signed-pivot chart, the entries of \(S_B=B^{\top}\Omega B\), the coordinate-envelope restrictions, and the two endpoint residuals are polynomial or rational semialgebraic functions.  The three stable pieces are expressed exactly by
\[
\operatorname{tr}(S_B)=0,
\tag{1}
\]
\[
\det(S_B)=0,\qquad \operatorname{tr}(S_B)\ge n\lambda_{1,n},
\tag{2}
\]
and
\[
S_B-n\lambda_{2,n}I_2\succeq0.
\tag{3}
\]
For the \(2\times2\) matrix in (3), positive semidefiniteness is equivalent to nonnegativity of its two diagonal entries and determinant.  Thus (1)--(3), the sphere normalizations, signed-pivot conditions, and \(|b_{a,i}|\le\overline a\) form a finite union of compact semialgebraic sets.  This union is exactly \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\); it is nonempty because it contains the zero class.

On (1), \(S_B^+=0\).  On (2), the rank-one formula is \(S_B^+=S_B/\{\operatorname{tr}(S_B)\}^2\), whose denominator is at least \(n^2\lambda_{1,n}^2>0\).  On (3), the rank-two formula is \(S_B^+=\operatorname{adj}(S_B)/\det(S_B)\), and the spectral lower bound makes its denominator positive.  Clearing these positive denominators gives a finite semialgebraic graph for \(\widehat w_m^+\) on every stable rank piece.  Continuity on each spectrally separated piece and compactness of their finite union show that
\[
\widehat v_m^{\mathrm{st}}
=\min_{[B]_+\in\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}}\widehat w_m^+(B)
\tag{4}
\]
is attained.

Apply real-closed-field quantifier elimination to the sublevel formula associated with this graph.  The rank-stratified CAD terminates, isolates the value in (4), and returns an algebraic sample in the nonempty \(\varepsilon_m\)-sublevel.  Hence its basis component belongs to \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) and satisfies
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{CAD,st},+})
\le\widehat v_m^{\mathrm{st}}+\varepsilon_m.
\tag{5}
\]
Exact algebraic sign tests verify the selected rank, all coordinate inequalities, and the applicable inequality in (1), (2), or (3).  Root isolation for the characteristic polynomial of the selected \(S_B\) supplies exact zero tags, multiplicities, and rational isolating intervals for every positive eigenvalue.

It remains to certify the stability price.  The population endpoint augmentation makes both optimization problems algebraic.  The same constrained CAD isolates the attained value \(v_n^{\mathrm{st}}\); \(\texttt{thm:zhao-pseudoinverse-rank-stratified-epsilon-solver}\) isolates the unrestricted infimum \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\) without assuming its attainment.  Therefore their difference
\[
b_n^{\mathrm{st}}=v_n^{\mathrm{st}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
\tag{6}
\]
is real algebraic.  Refine the two isolating computations until interval subtraction has width at most \(\varepsilon_m\).  Because \(\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\subseteq\mathcal B_Z^+\), (6) is nonnegative.  Intersect the difference interval with \([0,\infty)\); this preserves containment and cannot increase its width.  Its endpoints are the certified numbers
\[
0\le\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}
\le\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}},
\qquad
\overline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}
-\underline b_{n,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st}}\le\varepsilon_m.
\tag{7}
\]
The finite chart, CAD sign, value-isolation, rank, coordinate, spectral, and difference-interval records in (1)--(7) are exactly the stated certificate.  The proof uses no agreement with either countable selector and yields neither a zero-tolerance rule, a uniform complexity bound, nor a finite algorithm for arbitrary real pilot values. \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} The finite exact-input record certifies empirical near-optimality, selected rank, coordinate bounds, spectral margins, and an enclosure of the stability price. \textit{Gap.} It applies only to exact rational or real-algebraic empirical and population endpoint inputs. \textit{Consumer.} Exact-input stable deployments can audit every condition required by the rank-adaptive observable bridge.

\begin{theorem}[thm:stable-rank-adaptive-full-carrier-observable-regret]\label{thm:stable-rank-adaptive-full-carrier-observable-regret}
Suppose ass:known-exposure, ass:exposure-positivity, ass:bounded-outcomes, ass:iid-potential-pairs, ass:unpaired-pilot, ass:pilot-main-outcome-independence, and ass:frechet-covariance-domain hold.  Fix \(\lambda_{1,n}>0\), \(\lambda_{2,n}>0\), and \(\overline a\ge1\).  Let \(\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+}\in\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) be the classical pilot-measurable positive-tolerance selector from def:pilot-plugin, where \(\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m<\infty\), and put
\[
d_m^+=\sup_{[B]_+\in\mathcal B_Z^+}
\left|\widehat w_m^+(B)-w^+(B)\right|.
\]
Define its observable worst-copula ex ante conditional design-variance performance by
\[
V_{\mathrm{st}}
=\mathbb E_P\left[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu\!\left(
\widehat\tau_{\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+}}^{\mathrm{CV}}
\mid \widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+},Y
\right)
\right].
\]
With the rank-zero bridge remainder defined to be zero and with \(R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\) from thm:rank-adaptive-oracle-observable-variance-bridge for \(r\in\{1,2\}\),
\[
\left|V_{\mathrm{st}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\right|
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}
+2\mathbb E_Pd_m^+
+\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m
+\max_{r\in\{1,2\}}R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\]
The same conclusion holds for the exact-input stable CAD selector certified by thm:encoded-cad-stable-pilot-selector-certificate.  Consequently,
\[
n\left\{
b_n^{\mathrm{st}}+\mathbb E_Pd_m^+
+\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m
+\max_{r\in\{1,2\}}R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}
\right\}\longrightarrow0
\]
implies \(n|V_{\mathrm{st}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+|\to0\).  Under ass:zhao-dependency-neighborhood-rate, ass:zhao-exposure-probability-rate, and ass:zhao-first-order-rate, the last bridge term is \(o(n^{-1})\) when \(\lambda_{1,n}\) and \(\lambda_{2,n}\) are bounded below by fixed positive constants and \(\overline a\) is fixed.  No rate or zero value for \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) is inferred: it is the exact approximation-versus-stability price.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\textbf{Proof.}
Write \(\widehat B=\widehat B_{m,\varepsilon_m}^{\mathrm{st},+}\), \(K=\mathcal K_n^{\mathrm{st}}\), \(d=d_m^+\), and \(\varepsilon=\varepsilon_m\).  By \(\texttt{def:pilot-plugin}\),
\[
\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B)
\le\inf_{B\in K}\widehat w_m^+(B)+\varepsilon.
\tag{1}
\]
The definition of \(d\) gives, simultaneously on the full carrier,
\[
w^+(B)-d\le\widehat w_m^+(B)\le w^+(B)+d.
\tag{2}
\]
Let \(B^{\mathrm{st}}\in K\) attain \(v_n^{\mathrm{st}}\); attainment follows from the compact stable carrier and continuity on its spectrally separated rank pieces.  Applying (2), (1), and then (2) at \(B^{\mathrm{st}}\) gives
\[
w^+(\widehat B)
\le\widehat w_m^+(\widehat B)+d
\le\widehat w_m^+(B^{\mathrm{st}})+\varepsilon+d
\le v_n^{\mathrm{st}}+2d+\varepsilon.
\tag{3}
\]
Since \(K\subseteq\mathcal B_Z^+\),
\[
w^+(\widehat B)\ge v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+.
\tag{4}
\]
Taking pilot expectations in (3)--(4) and using \(v_n^{\mathrm{st}}=v_{\mathrm{mm}}^++b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\) yields
\[
0\le\mathbb E_Pw^+(\widehat B)-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}+2\mathbb E_Pd_m^++\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m.
\tag{5}
\]

Pilot--main independence allows conditioning on the realized \(\widehat B\).  At selected rank zero, \(\texttt{thm:rank-adaptive-oracle-observable-variance-bridge}\) says that observable and oracle estimators are exactly the uncontrolled Horvitz--Thompson estimator, so the bridge remainder is zero.  On the event of selected rank \(r\in\{1,2\}\), membership in \(K\) gives
\[
[\widehat B]_+\in\mathcal S_{r,n}(\lambda_{r,n},\overline a),
\]
so the same theorem gives, for every \(H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)\),
\[
\left|
\mathbb E_{H^{\otimes n}}
\operatorname{Var}_\nu(\widehat\tau_{\widehat B}^{\mathrm{CV}}\mid\widehat B,Y)
-\ell_{\widehat B}^+(c_H)\right|
\le R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\tag{6}
\]
The feasible covariance image is \([c_L,c_U]\), and the loss is affine in \(c_H\).  Hence
\[
\sup_{H\in\Gamma(F_1,F_0)}\ell_{\widehat B}^+(c_H)
=w^+(\widehat B).
\tag{7}
\]
Taking suprema in (6), using \(|\sup f-\sup g|\le\sup|f-g|\), and averaging over the pilot proves
\[
\left|V_{\mathrm{st}}-\mathbb E_Pw^+(\widehat B)\right|
\le\mathbb E_P\!\left[
\mathbf1\{\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widehat B})>0\}
R_{n,\operatorname{rank}(S_{\widehat B})}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right]
\le\max_{r\in\{1,2\}}R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\tag{8}
\]
Combining the upper side of (8) with (5) gives the required upper deviation from \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\).  Combining its lower side with (4) gives a negative deviation of magnitude at most the last term in (8), which is no larger than the displayed nonnegative sum.  This proves
\[
\left|V_{\mathrm{st}}-v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\right|
\le b_n^{\mathrm{st}}+2\mathbb E_Pd_m^+
+\mathbb E_P\varepsilon_m
+\max_{r\in\{1,2\}}R_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}.
\tag{9}
\]

For the exact-input stable CAD selector, \(\texttt{thm:encoded-cad-stable-pilot-selector-certificate}\) supplies (1), stable-carrier membership, exact rank, coordinate envelope, and the applicable spectral margin, so the same proof applies.  Multiplying (9) by \(n\) proves the first-order conclusion under the displayed convergence premise, which explicitly includes \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\).  Finally, the rate clause of the rank-adaptive bridge gives \(\max_r nR_{n,r}^{\mathrm{ad}}\to0\) under the three Zhao rate assumptions when both positive-rank thresholds are bounded below and \(\overline a\) is fixed.  Nothing in this argument gives a rate or zero value for \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\). \(\square\)
\end{proof}
\par\smallskip\noindent \textit{Justification.} This is an all-rank robust basis-selection and observable-regret theorem: it combines stable-carrier pilot regret with an eventwise rank-adaptive variance bridge to control the observable estimator relative to the unrestricted full-carrier value.  MikhaeilGreen2024SharpRegressionAdjustment supplies sharp adjusted-variance endpoint analysis, but not selection of a pseudoinverse control-variate basis from unpaired marginal pilots under cross-world copula ambiguity or an all-rank observable-regret bridge.  HarshawMiddletonSavje2026 studies optimized variance estimation under interference and complex designs, but not this fixed-design, cross-world-robust basis-selection target, its external unpaired-pilot input, or its rank-zero/rank-positive deployment accounting. \textit{Gap.} Neither closest theorem class supplies the joint result here: sharp adjusted-variance bounds do not by themselves select and deploy a copula-robust all-rank control-variate basis, while optimized interference variance estimation does not provide this unpaired-pilot, cross-world endpoint objective or its stability-price observable-regret comparison.  The theorem's own first-order conclusion still requires the full error sum to be \(o(n^{-1})\), including the exact stability price; no rate for that price is inferred. \textit{Consumer.} It is the end-to-end design-performance guarantee for a deployable all-rank pilot-selected basis.

\section{Interpretation}

The causal target remains \(\tau\); the quantities \(w^+(B)\), \(v_{\mathrm{mm}}^+\), and \(V_{\mathrm{st}}\) are respectively an ex ante worst-copula basis loss, its unrestricted design value, and the observable selected-basis performance.  Robust basis selection is therefore a design decision for estimating \(\tau\), not an identification formula for \(\tau\).  Rank-zero selection is exactly the uncontrolled Horvitz--Thompson fallback.  Positive-rank observable guarantees require certified spectral and coordinate stability, and the cost of imposing those restrictions is the visible, potentially nonzero quantity \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\).

\section*{Technical internal limitation (diagnostic only)}

The full pseudoinverse objective is discontinuous across uncontrolled rank changes, so arbitrary-real pilot optimization is supplied only as a classical measurable positive-tolerance selector.  Finite CAD certificates require rational or real-algebraic endpoint input.  The stable observable theorem assumes the entire displayed error sum, including \(b_n^{\mathrm{st}}\), is \(o(n^{-1})\) for its first-order conclusion; the design-rate assumptions alone control only the bridge remainder.  The exact triangle observable comparison concerns one displayed pair of tie representatives, and the Jiangxi PICC protocol remains heuristic, internally cross-fitted, and unexecuted.

\section{Honest scope}

The paper proves endpoint reduction, exact-input global certification, arbitrary-real measurable positive-tolerance selection, plug-in value regret, stable all-rank observable regret, and explicit triangle nonvacuity.  It does not claim a semidefinite-program reduction, a uniform complexity bound, a zero-tolerance arbitrary-real algorithm, universal full-carrier attainment, cross-rank outer-argmin convergence, point convergence under ties, universal strict improvement, or a zero rate for the stability price.  No Wald limit law is claimed; the delivered inference-side quantity is the finite ex ante expected conditional design-variance comparison for the displayed observable estimator.
